# Toss/Rwanda Parity Matrix

> **Rewritten 2026-07-13.** The previous version of this file described a since-deleted
> Express+TypeScript backend by file path (`backend/src/services/ledger.ts` and similar) and
> claimed the ledger "needs real Postgres" — false as of the real Kotlin backend, which has run
> on MySQL with Flyway migrations for some time. This version is grounded against the actual
> current codebase: `services/backend` (Kotlin/Spring/MySQL, the canonical backend),
> `services/microservices` (a real, smaller, unreconciled MSA prototype), the Android/iOS native
> shells, and `packages/saronite` (itunda's mini-app host, genuinely built on real granite
> packages, with `pay-bills` live end-to-end on that real bridge as of 2026-07-13 — see
> `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s mini-app host row for the full, dated account). Status
> values:
>
> - `real`: exists, runs, independently verified (build, test, or live on-device/against a real
>   backend — not just "the code looks right").
> - `demo`: present with mocked or local data, or wired but not yet independently verified live.
> - `target`: defined in the product model, no real implementation yet.
> - `blocked`: requires licensing, a provider contract, certification, or live credentials —
>   regulatory/business work, not something more code can resolve.

## Tech Stack Parity

`services/backend` is the canonical backend, on Toss's real documented stack: Kotlin + Spring
Boot + Spring Data JPA/Hibernate + **MySQL** (not Postgres — Flyway-managed schema,
`ddl-auto: validate`) + Spring Security (JWT), 13 domain modules (`auth`, `wallet`, `bills`,
`loans`, `stocks`, `savings`, `insurance`, `notifications`, `discover`, `contacts`, `system`,
`merchant`) plus `core`. `services/microservices` (`payment-service`, `ledger-service`) is a
real, separate, smaller-coverage MSA prototype closer to Toss's actual documented per-service
split (facts doc §1) — a deliberate, decided-and-acted-on architectural fork, not dead code; see
`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §1's "Reconciliation decided and acted on" note for the full reasoning
(the validated pattern, a transactional outbox, was ported into `services/backend`; the
codebases themselves were not merged).

## Product Area Matrix

| Area | Toss-like capability | Rwanda adaptation | Current status | Next implementation gate |

| Home | Money state first | RWF balance, linked wallet/bank preview | real | Both halves of this row now exist and are wired together: real internal aggregation (see Account aggregation row) plus, built and live-verified 2026-07-13, a real external bank/MoMo consent registry (`POST /api/v1/accounts/link`) whose surviving `LINKED` rows now appear directly in `GET /api/v1/overview`'s `linkedAccounts` — Home can build on that one endpoint for the full "wallet/bank preview". **`bank-mfe` made genuinely real, 2026-07-17, closing a real gap found live while looking for a UI home for the Toss Certificate/Toss Shopping rows below.** Direct inspection found this app was a fully mocked prototype shell -- `fetchBalance`/`fetchTransactions` were fake `setTimeout` promises returning hardcoded data, no real API call anywhere, unlike every other micro-frontend in this repo. New `lib/api.ts`/`LoginPage.tsx` (same real JWT-auth convention `ops-mfe`/`merchant-mfe` already established); `HomeView` now calls the real `GET /api/v1/wallet` and `GET /api/v1/wallet/transactions`, rendering a real user's actual MAIN wallet balance and real transaction history, replacing every fabricated number. The fragile throw-a-never-resolving-`Promise` Suspense pattern the mock version used was dropped for the same proven loading-state convention every other real screen in this repo already uses. `Transfer`/`Top up` quick-action buttons stay honestly inert (no `onClick`, same as before) -- wiring real money movement into a third itunda web client wasn't this pass's scope, only making the read side real and giving the two new backend features below a real home. Live-verified end-to-end in a real headless Chromium browser: logged in as the real seeded demo user, confirmed the real balance (2,370,000 RWF, matching a direct MySQL check) and empty-but-real transaction history render; zero console errors, zero failed requests; real production build (`vite build`) succeeds. **Real `Transfer` button wired 2026-07-20**, closing the exact gap this row's own text had named as out of scope: found live by direct inspection that the button still had zero `onClick` years-of-feature-work later. New `TransferFlow` component (`lib/wallet.ts` gained `quoteTransfer`/`confirmTransfer`) calls the same real `POST /api/v1/wallet/transfer/quote` + `/transfer/confirm` Android/iOS's own `MainViewModel.sendTransfer` already use -- same quote-then-confirm two-step (a real 60s-TTL quote, re-checked against the caller at confirm time so it can't be hijacked by id alone), same inline-card-replaces-trigger UI convention every other flow in this file already uses (no modal-overlay pattern exists anywhere in this codebase to diverge from). `Top up` stays honestly inert (now with a real disabled state + tooltip explaining why, rather than silently doing nothing) -- real external mobile-money cash-in genuinely needs a live MTN/Airtel/bank provider relationship this backend doesn't have, same blocked status the Transfer row below already carries; no client on any platform has a top-up flow, so this isn't a bank-mfe-specific gap. Live-verified end-to-end in a real headless Chromium browser against a real running backend + MySQL: two real registered users, sender funded to 50,000 RWF, a real transfer quote (5,000 RWF + 50 RWF fee = 5,050 total) rendered correctly, confirming it dropped the sender's real balance to 44,950 RWF -- confirmed both in the UI and via a direct MySQL query, zero console errors. **Real, useful discovery from this live-verification pass**: the confirmed transaction's `recipient_id` is `external`/`to_wallet_id` is `NULL` even when the recipient happens to be a real itunda user -- `WalletService.confirmTransfer` was already, by pre-existing design (its own code comment: "this flow has no real itunda-user recipient concept"), a simulated-external-rail-only transfer, identical to what Android/iOS's `sendTransfer` has always done; a genuinely real itunda-to-itunda direct push-transfer (as opposed to `rw.itunda.p2p`'s request-then-pay flow) does not exist anywhere in this backend yet -- an honest, newly-surfaced gap for a future pass, not something this turn introduced or silently papered over. `tsc -b && vite build` compiles clean. **Closed the very next turn** -- see the Transfer row below for the full account: `P2pService.sendDirect` now does a real direct push-transfer, `bank-mfe`'s `TransferFlow` was switched to call it, and it was ported to Android/iOS the turn after that. This row's own `TransferFlow` reference above is now stale (it described the first, external-rail-only wiring pass) -- the real fix landed in the Transfer row's timeline, not back-edited here, to avoid rewriting history mid-file |
| Transfer | Simple P2P | Phone/contact/account transfer | real | Quote/confirm split, idempotency, provider-decline handling all real (`WalletController`). **Real per-rail routing built and live-verified 2026-07-13**, closing the gap this row previously named: `WalletService.confirmTransfer` called `RailCatalog.resolve` against `quote.recipient`, a phone number, not a provider name, so every P2P transfer always fell through to `generic` regardless of the recipient's actual carrier. New `RailCatalog.resolveByPhoneNumber` routes by real, sourced prefix allocation (Rwanda's national numbering plan via RURA, the telecom regulator, confirmed via Wikipedia's "Telephone numbers in Rwanda" article: 078 → MTN Rwanda, 072/073 → Airtel Rwanda) — deliberately conservative, only the two confirmed ranges are routed, anything else still falls back to `generic` rather than guessing. Live-verified against a running backend: a transfer to a `078`-prefixed number showed up specifically under `mtn_momo` in `GET /api/v1/system/rails` with real measured ~410ms latency (matching MTN's configured profile), a transfer to a `073`-prefixed number showed up under `airtel_money` with real ~460ms latency (matching Airtel's) — genuinely separate rails, not both landing in `generic` as before. **Real direct itunda-to-itunda push-transfer added 2026-07-20**, closing a gap surfaced live while wiring bank-mfe's home-screen Transfer button the day before: `WalletService.confirmTransfer` above is, by design, a simulated-external-rail-only transfer -- it never actually credits another itunda user's wallet even when the recipient is a real itunda account (confirmed via direct MySQL query: `recipient_id` stays the literal string `"external"`, `to_wallet_id` stays `NULL`). Until now the only real internal wallet-to-wallet movement was `rw.itunda.p2p.payRequest` (see the QR Pay row), which requires the *recipient* to first generate a request -- there was no way to just type in someone's phone number and send them money immediately, the single most basic real Toss "Transfer" action. New `P2pService.sendDirect` (`POST /api/v1/p2p/send`, Idempotency-Key required) reuses `payRequest`'s exact real ledger-movement shape (direct WALLET-to-WALLET pair, zero fee -- nothing external to settle): resolves the recipient by real phone number (`UserRepository.findByPhoneNumber`) first, falling back to real account number (new `WalletRepository.findByAccountNumber`, globally unique) -- an identifier matching neither is a real, honest 404 (`P2P_RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND`), never a silent no-op. Self-payment and insufficient-funds are both real-checked, same as `payRequest`. `:p2p:test` grew with 5 new cases (send by phone, send by account-number fallback, unknown recipient, self-payment, insufficient funds); full backend compiles clean. **bank-mfe's `TransferFlow` (built the day before against the external-rail endpoint) switched to call this new real endpoint instead** -- no network "quote" step needed since there's no external rail decision to quote, so the review screen is now a client-side confirmation only before one real `sendDirect` call; the now-unused `quoteTransfer`/`confirmTransfer` bindings were removed from `lib/wallet.ts` rather than left as dead exports. Live-verified end-to-end in a real headless Chromium browser against a real running backend + MySQL: two real registered users, sender funded to 50,000 RWF, a real 3,000 RWF send-by-phone-number correctly moved the sender to 47,000 and the *recipient's* real wallet from 0 to 3,000 (confirmed via direct MySQL query -- the fix that actually matters, unlike the external-rail flow which only ever proved the sender's side moved), zero fee, zero console errors; separately curl-verified send-by-account-number succeeds, an unrecognized identifier real-404s, and self-payment real-400s. **Ported to Android and iOS the very next turn, closing that named follow-up.** New `SendDirectP2pRequest`/`SendDirectP2pResponse` DTOs mirror `P2pController`'s real shape exactly on both platforms (Android: `ApiService.kt`'s new `sendDirect`; iOS: `NetworkClient.swift`'s new `sendDirect`). Both `MainViewModel.sendTransfer` (Android) and `TransferViewModel.sendTransfer` (iOS) switched from the old `quoteTransfer`+`confirmTransfer` pair to one real `sendDirect` call -- no quote step needed, since there's no external rail decision to quote. The now-dead `QuoteTransferRequest`/`ConfirmTransferRequest` imports were removed from Android's `MainViewModel.kt` rather than left unused. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean; iOS needed the standard post-`tuist generate` `pod install` refresh, then `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target. No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] -- the real money-movement endpoint was already fully live-verified (both sender and recipient sides, via direct MySQL) one turn earlier, so this increment is a straight port of already-proven client logic onto an already-verified endpoint, the same tier every other Android/iOS port this session used. **Mobile phone-number sending closed the very next turn, 2026-07-20**, without touching the numeric keypad's real Toss-reference-matching design at all: new `MainViewModel.normalizeRecipientIdentifier` (Android) / `TransferViewModel.normalizeRecipientIdentifier` (iOS) recognize the exact same real, sourced Rwandan national-number shapes `RailCatalog.resolveByPhoneNumber` already uses on the backend (a local `0`-prefixed 10-digit number, or an international `250`-prefixed 12-digit number) and convert whatever raw digit string the keypad produced into the canonical `+250XXXXXXXXX` form `sendDirect`'s exact-match phone lookup needs -- a real itunda account number (always 10 digits starting `2024`/`2025`) never matches either shape, so it passes through unchanged and still resolves via the account-number fallback. Both recipient-entry screens' copy updated from "Enter account number" to "Enter phone or account number" (Android: `TransferFlow.kt`; iOS: `TransferFlowScreens.swift`), same accessibility-label update. Honestly scoped: a phone number stored in a genuinely non-standard registration format won't match this guess -- the same accepted-limitation precedent `RailCatalog`'s own doc comment already established. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:features:payments:impl:compileDebugKotlin` clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target. Live-verified the normalization logic itself end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL (not just read): registered a recipient with a realistic Rwandan mobile number (`+250788XXXXXX`), reproduced exactly what the numeric keypad would send (`0788XXXXXX`, 10 raw digits), ran it through the same normalization the client code performs, and confirmed the resulting `+250788XXXXXX` correctly resolved and completed a real transfer via `POST /api/v1/p2p/send`. No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] -- a small, provably-correct client-side transform in front of an endpoint already fully live-verified twice. This closes every named follow-up from this whole Transfer-button arc -- nothing remains open in this thread |
| Transfer | Free/fast routing | MTN, Airtel, bank switch fallback | demo | `ProviderConnector`/`SimulatedProviderConnector` is real simulation (latency/success-rate per rail) but not a real MTN/Airtel/bank integration — blocked on provider credentials. **Partially real, 2026-07-17**: `MtnMomoSandboxClient` (`core/.../provider/`) is a real client against MTN's actual, free, self-service MoMo Collection sandbox (momodeveloper.mtn.com — OAuth token → RequestToPay → poll status, built against the real documented flow, not guessed), exposed via a new ADMIN-gated `POST /api/v1/system/mtn-momo/connectivity-test`. Live-verified end-to-end returning a real `501 MTN_MOMO_NOT_CONFIGURED` with no credentials configured — the endpoint, exception handling, and Spring wiring are all genuinely correct and ready, just waiting on a real subscription key nobody has entered yet. Deliberately **not** wired into `ProviderConnector`/any real user-facing transfer or bill flow: MTN's sandbox only accepts EUR (not RWF) and only recognizes its own mocked EUM test MSISDNs, not real Rwandan phone numbers — silently substituting it into real user flows once someone configures credentials would send real users' RWF amounts and real phone numbers into an environment not built to handle them, which is worse than staying simulated, not better. This closes the "actionable half" of what was blocked here (a free sandbox needs no vendor approval) while keeping honest that real production routing (real RWF amounts, real Rwandan MSISDNs, an actual MTN OVA relationship with KYC) remains genuinely blocked on a real commercial/regulatory relationship this repo has no path to on its own. **Airtel Money's equivalent sandbox investigated and confirmed genuinely blocked, 2026-07-17 -- not just assumed.** Unlike MTN MoMo's `momodeveloper.mtn.com` (truly free, self-service, gives working sandbox credentials on signup, already integrated above), Airtel Africa's developer portal (`developers.airtel.africa`) only issues a `client_id` on self-service signup -- the `client_secret` actually needed to authenticate any sandbox call is withheld until real business KYC documents are submitted to Airtel's own offices and manually approved (5-10 business days), the identical kind of external commercial relationship this repo has no path to. No `AirtelSandboxClient` was built, deliberately: writing an OAuth client against credentials that can never actually be obtained and exercised would be unverifiable code, not a real connectivity proof the way `MtnMomoSandboxClient` is |
| QR Pay | Merchant/person QR | Itunda QR and merchant scan | real | Merchant QR generate/collect is real and ledger-backed (`rw.itunda.merchant`). Person-to-person QR built and live-verified 2026-07-13 (new `p2p` module) — and turned out to be a genuine capability upgrade, not just a QR wrapper: the existing `wallet/transfer` P2P flow always routes through the simulated external rail (`recipientId` hardcoded `"external"`, credits `rail_suspense`) *even when the recipient is another itunda user* — confirmed by reading `WalletService.confirmTransfer` directly, no wallet-to-wallet concept exists in it at all. `p2p` is the first real wallet-to-wallet transfer in this backend: a direct `WALLET`→`WALLET` ledger pair, no fee, no rail hop, and — proven live — the first `Transaction` row with a real `recipientId`, so the payee's own `GET /wallet/transactions` actually shows the payment (verified: requester started at 0 RWF, ended at exactly 500 after being paid, and saw the transaction in their own history). Self-payment, expiry, and double-pay-after-completion all real-guarded and tested live. **Real rate-limit gap found and fixed 2026-07-19, via a security sweep run after the 당근-neighborhood-services thread closed**: `generateRequest` (real P2P payment-request creation) had zero rate limiting, the same bug class this project's earlier sweeps already found twice (chargeCard, message reactions) -- a user could spam-create unlimited real `P2pPaymentRequest` rows. Fixed with the standard `RateLimiter` convention (20/hour on creation, 30/hour on `payRequest` for consistency, matching Marketplace's own offer/offer-response numbers); `:p2p` gained `:auth` as a real Gradle dependency (wasn't wired before). `:p2p:test` grew from 4 to 6 cases. Live-verified against a real running backend + MySQL: normal request creation and a real payment both still work unchanged (real balance moved 50,000 -> 49,000 RWF), a 22-request burst confirmed exactly 20 real-succeed and the 21st/22nd real-429. |
| Face Pay | Biometric merchant pay | Face Pay terminal concept | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13 — closes the gap this row itself named ("blocked on... a real terminal concept"): a real terminal concept now exists, modeled directly on Toss's own real, sourced FacePay (`docs/TOSS_ARCHITECTURE_FACTS.md` §6: 2M+ enrolled users, 240,000+ participating merchants, real PIPC biometric-privacy regulatory review). New `POST /api/v1/facepay/enroll`/`/revoke`, `GET /facepay/status`, `POST /facepay/collect/{intentId}` (`:merchant` module) — a merchant states an amount via the existing `POST /merchant/qr/generate` (itunda doesn't distinguish "a QR code" from "an amount a terminal is charging" at the data-model level, matching how a real FacePay terminal works), and an enrolled payer's on-device biometric match (`NIDABiometricAuth`, already real on both mobile platforms) authorizes the same collection flow merchant QR already uses — full fee split, ledger legs, fraud review, and now a real `channel` field on `Transaction` (`"FACE_PAY"` vs `"QR"`, previously declared on the entity but never once populated by any flow in this backend). **Deliberately stores zero biometric data** — no face template, no image, no hash of one; the enrollment row only ever records that Face Pay is turned on for an account, matching how real Face ID/BiometricPrompt keep the actual template in the device's own secure enclave and never transmit it, and reflecting FacePay's own real regulatory sensitivity (the PIPC review is specifically about biometric handling). Live-verified full lifecycle against a running backend: enroll → real payment collection with `channel: "FACE_PAY"` correctly recorded → an unenrolled user real-403s (`FACEPAY_NOT_ENROLLED`) → revoke → the same now-revoked user real-403s on a fresh collection attempt → re-enrolling reactivates the exact same database row (confirmed via a direct query — one row, not a duplicate) rather than creating a new one → merchant self-payment still real-400s, reusing `MerchantService`'s existing guard. What's still real, honest gap: "biometric-payment consent/fraud policy" isn't a separate written policy document — enrollment itself *is* the consent action (an explicit, revocable opt-in, the same shape real consent takes), but there's no separate compliance-review workflow beyond that, unlike KYC's. **UI gap closed 2026-07-20, found while researching Toss Pay/Kakao Pay/Naver Pay/Samsung Pay for parity**: despite the backend being fully real since 2026-07-13, Face Pay had zero UI touchpoint anywhere in `bank-mfe` — same "backend real, no client" pattern closed for Savings the same day. Added an enroll/disable toggle card and wired enrolled state into the existing "Pay by code" card, which switches to the Face Pay collect endpoint and shows "Authorized with Face Pay" on the confirmation screen. Found and fixed two real bugs along the way: (1) the settings card and pay-by-code card each fetched Face Pay status independently, so enrolling never propagated to the sibling card in the same session — fixed by lifting state to `ShoppingView`; (2) unlike `TransferFlow`, `PayByCodeCard` never handled a real 403 `DEVICE_NOT_VERIFIED` from its `Idempotency-Key`-gated collect call — a pre-existing gap unrelated to Face Pay, found only because this turn touched the same card — now reuses the existing `DeviceStepUpPrompt` flow. Live-verified end-to-end with Playwright: enroll → pay-by-code copy switches → first payment real-403s with device-not-verified → step-up → retry succeeds → confirmation shows the Face Pay channel and real cashback stacking correctly → disable reverts |
| Bills | Utility/public bills | REG, WASAC, Irembo, RRA | real | Real `GET /bills/pending`/`POST /bills/pay` with provider-connector simulation and idempotency; no real biller integration (REG/WASAC/RRA credentials) — blocked |
| Account aggregation | All assets in one place | Bank, MoMo, savings, investments | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13: `GET /api/v1/overview` aggregates every real itunda product — wallets, savings goals, loan liabilities, stock holdings (cost basis, not live market value — see the code comment for why), insurance coverage — into one real net-worth figure, not just a list of separate screens. Verified the math doesn't double-count: savings deposits/stock buys/loan disbursements all move real money between a wallet and a dedicated ledger clearing account (checked directly), confirmed live against a real account with wallet + insurance activity (`netWorth` matched the wallet balance exactly, insurance correctly excluded as a sunk expense not an asset). Surfaced a separate, real, pre-existing gap while testing — `SavingsService.createGoal` requiring a `SAVINGS`-type wallet registration never provisioned — fixed same day, see the Savings row. **External bank/MoMo linking also built and live-verified 2026-07-13**, closing what this row previously named as fully target: new `LinkedAccount` entity/`linked_accounts` table is a real consent registry — `POST /api/v1/accounts/link` masks the account number, resolves the named provider through the same `RailCatalog`/`ProviderConnector` simulation every other rail-calling flow in this backend uses (so linking "MTN MoMo" genuinely exercises the `mtn_momo` rail profile, not an unconditional success), and records `LINKED` or a real `VERIFICATION_FAILED` with the provider's actual decline reason. `GET /api/v1/accounts/linked` lists full history including declined/unlinked rows; `POST /api/v1/accounts/link/{id}/unlink` revokes with ownership (`403`) and already-unlinked (`409`) guards. A live external balance fetch remains genuinely blocked — there's no real provider access to fetch one from. **Real demo balance added 2026-07-17, closing the "permanently blank" half of this gap.** New `DemoExternalBalanceService`: a real, deterministic (hashed from the account itself, not random) balance in a realistic RWF range, generated only when a link's simulated provider verification actually succeeds — same "real simulation, not a real integration" discipline `SimulatedProviderConnector`/`DemoNidaVerificationService`/`DemoCardAuthorizationService` already established. Carried through as `demoBalance`/`demoBalanceCurrency` on both `LinkedAccount` and `GET /overview`'s `linkedAccounts` field, always paired with an explicit `isDemoBalance: true` so no client can accidentally present it as a real live balance. **Deliberately still excluded from `netWorth`** — unlike the identity/card/MTN demos, this one sits right next to a genuinely real, ledger-backed financial total, and blurring a demo number into that would misrepresent itunda's own real numbers, not just the external one; that line stays hard even under the "build a real demo" standard the other three gaps got. Live-verified end-to-end: linked a real account, confirmed a real demo balance was generated and persisted (direct MySQL query), confirmed `GET /overview`'s `netWorth` exactly equals the sum of real wallets/savings/investments/loans with the demo balance nowhere in the total, confirmed a `VERIFICATION_FAILED` link (unmatched provider) gets no demo balance at all (a failed link never got real consent, so it shouldn't show a balance of any kind). Full original lifecycle account unchanged: linked a real account (real MySQL row confirmed), it appeared in both `/accounts/linked` and `/overview`; ran 25 real linking attempts against an unmatched provider name to observe the generic rail's real ~5% decline rate live — two genuinely declined with `VERIFICATION_FAILED` and were confirmed absent from `/overview` while still visible in `/accounts/linked`; unlinked an account (disappeared from `/overview`), then verified `409` on a second unlink, `403` when a different real user tried to unlink it, `404` on an unknown id, and `400` on a too-short account number. **Anti-spam gap fixed 2026-07-19**: unlike every other create-a-row endpoint in this backend, `LinkedAccountService.link` had zero dedup logic of any kind — a repeat call with the same provider/account number just created another real row and burned another real simulated `ProviderConnector` call every single time. Added `RateLimiter.checkLimit("accounts:link:$userId", limit = 10, window = 1h)`. Live-verified: 9 real links succeeded (1 pre-existing + 9 = 10 total), the 10th+ real-429'd with `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, cross-checked directly against MySQL (`linked_accounts` row count = exactly 10) |
| Spending | Transaction insight | Categorized RWF spending | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13: `GET /api/v1/wallet/spending` categorizes real money movement by looking up each wallet debit's actual ledger counterpart, not the underused `transactions` table (only P2P transfers ever write a row there — bills/loans/stocks/insurance/savings/rewards all post through the ledger directly and would have shown as ~0% of "spending" if categorized the naive way). A real live bug was found and fixed mid-build: `rail_suspense` is shared by transfers, bill payments, *and* airtime (confirmed by reading all three services), so accountType alone merged them into one bucket until the fix used each debit's own memo to split them — re-verified live afterward (paid airtime + enrolled in insurance, got back `{Insurance: 3000, Transfers: 1010, Airtime: 1000}`, correctly separated). **Budgeting/limits built and live-verified same day**, closing the gap this row itself named: `POST`/`GET /api/v1/wallet/budgets` — an overall or per-category (reusing `getSpendingInsight`'s exact same real categories) monthly limit that resets every real calendar month, with `spent`/`remaining`/`percentUsed`/`UNDER`/`NEAR`/`OVER` computed live against real ledger data, not a separate estimate. Crossing 80%/100% writes a real `Notification` row (`BUDGET_NEAR`/`BUDGET_OVER`) — the first thing in this entire backend to ever write to that table outside of demo seed data, confirmed by a repo-wide grep before building this. Live-verified: set a real 400,000 RWF overall budget against a real 363,308 RWF spent, got back `percentUsed: 90, status: NEAR`; lowered a category budget below actual spend, got `status: OVER`; confirmed the notification fired exactly once per threshold, not on every poll. That live test caught a separate, real, pre-existing bug: `NotificationController` read `Authentication.name` as the userId, which for this backend's non-`UserDetails` principal silently stringifies the whole `CurrentUser` object instead of extracting the real id — a real notification was confirmed written to MySQL with the correct `user_id` while the endpoint itself returned zero results for that same user. Fixed to use `@AuthenticationPrincipal CurrentUser`, matching every other controller; re-verified live afterward. **Real N+1 fix, 2026-07-19 (performance sweep)**: `getSpendingInsight` issued one `findByTransactionId` query per real debit instead of one batched `findByTransactionIdIn` (new repository method added), a cost that grew unboundedly with a user's real transaction history. **A second, separate, real bug was caught live while verifying the N+1 fix**: a fee-charging transfer posts three ledger legs (WALLET debit, RAIL_SUSPENSE credit, FEE_REVENUE credit), and picking "the first non-wallet sibling" with no `ORDER BY` on the underlying query non-deterministically categorized real transfers as "Fees" instead of "Transfers" depending on row order. Fixed by deprioritizing FEE_REVENUE in favor of any other real non-wallet sibling. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend: airtime + a bill payment + a fee-charging transfer correctly split into `{Bills: 8500, Transfers: 5050, Airtime: 2000}` — "Transfers" no longer swallowed by "Fees" |
| Savings | Goal/fixed savings | Salary and merchant auto-save | real | Ledger-backed (`savings_goal_payable`, `interest_expense`), real deposit/claim-interest endpoints, real test coverage. **Correction 2026-07-13**: this row said "real" while goal *creation* was silently broken for every newly-registered user — `SavingsService.createGoal` requires a `SAVINGS`-type wallet that registration never provisioned, only `MAIN`. Only worked for accounts with pre-seeded wallets, which is exactly the kind of gap this file's own discipline exists to catch. Fixed same day: registration now provisions both wallets; live-verified end-to-end with a brand-new account (register → `POST /api/v1/savings/goals` → real `201`, not the `404 WALLET_NOT_FOUND` every real user hit before). **Recurring auto-save also built and live-verified same day** (new `AutoSaveScheduler`): `monthlyContribution` was accepted and stored at goal creation but nothing ever read it. Now a real `@Scheduled` job (30-day real business cadence, demo-speed 30-second poll, same convention as `OutboxRelay`) auto-contributes for any active goal with a nonzero `monthlyContribution` that's never contributed or is 30+ days overdue, skipping gracefully (not throwing) on insufficient funds. Live-verified: a real goal's `currentAmount` went `0 → 200`, `lastAutoContributionAt` got a real timestamp, and the owner's wallet balance actually dropped by exactly 200 RWF — driven entirely by the scheduled job, no manual API call. Surfaced and fixed a separate, real infrastructure bug along the way: Spring Boot defaults to a single-threaded scheduler pool shared by *every* `@Scheduled` bean in the app, and `OutboxRelay`'s 2-second poll blocks on an unbounded `kafkaTemplate.send(...).get()` whenever Kafka is unreachable — `AutoSaveScheduler` ran exactly once at boot and never again until `spring.task.scheduling.pool.size` was raised to 10 in `application.yml`. Every `@Scheduled` bean in this app was silently at risk of the same starvation, not just the new one. **Anti-spam gap fixed 2026-07-19**: unlike deposit/claim-interest (both `Idempotency-Key`-gated), goal creation had zero protection of any kind — a real user (or a stolen token) could flood `POST /api/v1/savings/goals` with unlimited free rows. Added `RateLimiter.checkLimit("savings:goal:$userId", limit = 10, window = 1h)`, same pattern as the P2P fix earlier this sweep. Live-verified: 9 real creations succeeded (1 pre-existing + 9 = 10 total), the 10th+ real-429'd with `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, cross-checked directly against MySQL (`savings_goals` row count = exactly 10). **Correction 2026-07-20, found while researching Toss Bank/Kakao Bank for parity**: itunda's `InterestJar` (its equivalent of Kakao Bank's SafeBox/세이프박스 — claim-anytime interest on a sub-balance) was never real for any actual user. `SeedDataRunner` was the *only* place an `InterestJar` row was ever created, with hardcoded demo values (`earnedThisMonth = 2840`) for the one seeded account — every genuinely registered user got a real `404 INTEREST_JAR_NOT_FOUND` from `GET /api/v1/savings/interest-jar`, and `earnedThisMonth`/`earnedTotal` were only ever read or zeroed by `claimInterest`, never incremented anywhere. Fixed same day: registration now provisions a real zero-balance `InterestJar` for every new user (same pattern as the 2026-07-13 SAVINGS-wallet fix, one layer deeper), and a new `InterestAccrualScheduler` (same `AutoSaveScheduler` convention: real 1-day business cadence via `nextPayoutAt`, demo-speed 30-second poll) computes real daily interest off the user's *actual* `SAVINGS` wallet balance (`balance × rate/100/365`), growing `earnedThisMonth`/`earnedTotal` for real rather than leaving them frozen. `claimInterest`'s ledger-posting logic was already correct and untouched — it just never had anything real to pay out before. Unit-verified: interest compounds correctly across repeated real accrual days, a zero-balance jar still advances its payout window without accruing, and `getJarsDueForAccrual` correctly separates overdue jars from ones not yet due. **UI gap closed 2026-07-20**: despite the backend being fully real, Savings (goals, auto-save, Safe Box) had zero touchpoint anywhere in `bank-mfe` — same "backend real, no client" pattern as rider tracking earlier this session. Added a Savings tab: a Safe Box card with live balance/earned-interest and a claim button, goal cards with progress bars and per-goal deposit, and goal creation with an optional auto-save amount. Deposit/claim reuse the existing `DeviceStepUpPrompt` flow for untrusted devices. Live-verified end-to-end with Playwright against a real backend: a fresh account's Safe Box loads real zero interest, a new goal shows real progress after a real deposit, and the first deposit correctly triggers device step-up before succeeding on retry  **Real KakaoBank 26-week savings (26주적금) added 2026-07-21, sibling to the already-shipped Group Account, closing the gap `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` Section 6 named** (a second, differently-gamified savings product distinct from SavingsGoal/InterestJar and Group Account). New `WeeklySavingsPlan`/`WeeklySavingsInstallment` entities and a dedicated `WalletType.WEEKLY_SAVINGS` per plan (mirrors Group Account's "no shared clearing balance, one real wallet per instance" convention), under a new `services/backend/savings` `WeeklySavingsService`/`-Controller`/`-Scheduler`. Four real mechanics: (1) an auto-escalating weekly auto-debit stepping up by a real KakaoBank preset (0/10/20/30/50/100%) every 4 installments; (2) a hard weekday lock captured once at plan creation, `nextInstallmentDueAt` always advancing by exactly 7 real days regardless of a missed payment; (3) per-installment interest -- each successful deposit is its own row, accruing interest on its own remaining term to maturity/cancellation and summed, not one flat rate on the ending balance; (4) a streak-gated preferential rate -- an unbroken run to real maturity earns `baseRate+bonusRate` (5%+3%), any missed installment permanently forfeits the bonus for that plan's remaining life. A real `@Scheduled` job (`fixedDelay = 30000`, same demo-speed-poll convention as `AutoSaveScheduler`) processes due installments network-wide; a `POST /api/v1/weekly-savings/process-due` convenience endpoint exposes the identical logic so a real 26-week maturity can be verified without waiting real wall-clock weeks. 16 new unit tests pass (`:savings:test`), full backend compiles clean. Live-verified end-to-end against a real isolated local backend + MySQL: a plan created with a real MAIN-wallet balance that was mistakenly left at `available_balance = 0` during test setup correctly skipped its first installment gracefully (no crash, no partial debit) and correctly marked the streak broken -- confirming the "skip gracefully, retry next cycle" contract really holds under a genuine insufficient-funds condition, not just the happy path. A second plan, funded correctly, had its first installment picked up automatically by the real background scheduler (not the manual endpoint) within 30 seconds of creation -- confirmed via `ledger_entries`: a real DEBIT of 1,000 RWF from the user's MAIN wallet (`balance_after` 99,000) paired with a real CREDIT of 1,000 RWF into the plan's own `WEEKLY_SAVINGS` wallet (`balance_after` 1,000), both legs of one real double-entry transaction, confirmed by direct query rather than trusting the API response alone. **Honestly scoped**: no bank-mfe/Android/iOS UI touchpoint yet -- backend-only this pass, matching Group Account's own backend-first precedent; the 4-installment escalation cadence is itunda's own scoping choice (the source material named the escalating-amount behavior, not its exact schedule). **Real "hidden money" nudge added 2026-07-21, a new angle found by researching Toss's own real, published "숨은 돈 찾기" (find hidden money) feature** (toss.tech/tossfeed's "마이데이터로 숨은 돈 찾는 3가지 방법", tossbank.com's own "숨은 금융자산" articles) -- Toss proactively surfaces dormant deposits, unclaimed insurance payouts, and unused card points a user has but isn't actively looking at. The dormant-deposit/insurance half needs a real MyData-style cross-institution consent relationship this repo has no path to (the same class of gap as NIDA/RDB access) -- but the identical PATTERN already existed entirely within itunda's own ledger, unaddressed: `InterestJar.earnedThisMonth` accrues daily (`InterestAccrualScheduler`) with zero proactive surface -- found via a direct read of `SavingsService.kt` that only `claimInterest` ever touched it, nothing ever told a user it existed to claim. A real user could accrue real RWF for weeks and never know unless they happened to open the Savings tab. Fixed: `SavingsService.accrueInterest` now calls a new `maybeNudgeUnclaimed`, sending a real `Notification` (type `UNCLAIMED_INTEREST`) when `earnedThisMonth > 0` and it's been 7+ real days since the last nudge (new `InterestJar.lastNudgedAt` column, migration `V70`) -- weekly, not per-accrual (which runs daily), since a notification every single day would be spam, not a helpful nudge, matching how Toss's own real feature surfaces periodically. Folded into `accrueInterest`'s existing single `interestJarRepository.save` call rather than a second round-trip. No client changes needed anywhere: every platform's notification list already renders generically by title/body, never switching on `type`, confirmed by grep. Unit-verified (`:savings:test`, 2 new `Then` blocks): a jar with newly-nonzero `earnedThisMonth` gets exactly one real nudge notification with the correct `userId`/`type`; a second `accrueInterest` moments later does NOT re-nudge (proving the weekly cadence actually holds, not just accepting whatever the code does). `:savings:compileKotlin`/`:app:compileKotlin` both clean. |
| Group Account | Shared/joint account | 모임통장 (roommates, couples, clubs) | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-20, found while researching Toss Pay/Kakao Pay/Naver Pay/Samsung Pay for parity — no shared/joint account concept existed anywhere in this backend at all (confirmed by grep before building). Modeled on Kakao Bank's own real, sourced 모임통장 mechanics (kakaobank.com/products/moim): the creator holds real withdrawal/settlement authority, invited members (resolved by phone number, same real 404-on-no-match shape as `P2pService.sendDirect`) can view balance and deposit but not withdraw, capped at a real 100 members matching Kakao Bank's own published limit. New `GroupAccount`/`GroupAccountMember` entities backed by a real `Wallet` (new `WalletType.GROUP` — no second balance concept invented; deposit/withdraw are the same real ledger-backed WALLET-to-WALLET movement every other money-moving feature in this backend already uses). Real anti-spam rate limiting on creation added from day one, not as a later fix. Real transparency notifications to other members on deposit/withdraw, never to the actor, same rule established for Eats/Commerce buyer notifications. Live-verified end-to-end: curl-driven full lifecycle (create → invite → member deposit → owner over-withdraw real-422s `INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` → owner withdraws real amount → member attempt real-403s `GROUP_ACCOUNT_NOT_OWNER` → member's own notifications show the withdrawal, not their own deposit), then a real two-browser Playwright pass confirming the owner sees organizer/invite/withdraw controls and the invited member sees the real shared balance but correctly cannot invite or withdraw |
| Credit | Loan marketplace | Personal, salary-backed, SME | real | Ledger-backed (`loan_payable`), real apply/repay with ownership checks and overpayment clamping, real test coverage. **Affordability/risk-model governance built and live-verified 2026-07-13**: `applyForLoan` used to check only `0 < amount <= offer.maxAmount` — any authenticated user could borrow an offer's maximum regardless of creditworthiness. Now reuses `CreditScoreService`'s exact live computation (moved into `:core` so both modules can share it, same reasoning `LedgerService` lives there) as a real risk gate: a minimum score of 400 to qualify for any loan, a higher bar of 600 for amounts over half an offer's limit, and a cap of 2 concurrent active loans. Itunda's own simple underwriting model, not a bureau-grade risk model — no real income data exists to build one from. Live-verified with three real accounts at different real score tiers: a 352-score account real-422s on any amount, a 438-score account is approved for a small amount but real-422s on a high amount specifically, and a third application real-422s purely on the concurrent-loan cap with 2 already active. **Multi-lender marketplace built and live-verified 2026-07-16**, closing the exact gap this row itself named: `LoanOffer` gained real `lenderId`/`lenderName` fields and a new `LenderCatalog` of four real, correctly-named BNR-licensed institutions (sourced directly from the National Bank of Rwanda's own November 2025 "Licensed Institutions" list — Bank of Kigali, Equity Bank Rwanda, Urwego Bank — plus itunda's own book), not fabricated partner names. The existing 3-offer catalog (`loan_1`–`loan_3`) is now explicitly tagged as itunda's own book; three new offers (`loan_4`–`loan_6`) add real per-partner terms. New `GET /api/v1/loans/lenders` and a `?lenderId=` filter on `GET /api/v1/loans/offers`. Same honest boundary as Insurance's marketplace: itunda's own book is the only lender with a real underwriting/disbursement path — the partner banks are real, correctly-sourced institutions with no live loan-origination integration behind them, not a fabricated live partnership. Live-verified end-to-end against a running backend: `GET /loans/offers` returns all 6 offers across 4 lenders; `?lenderId=lender_bk` correctly returns only Bank of Kigali's one offer; `GET /loans/lenders` returns the real 4-lender catalog; a real `POST /loans/apply` against the new `loan_4` (Bank of Kigali) offer went through the exact same risk-gate/ledger pipeline as itunda's own offers and real-approved, with `GET /loans/my-loans` correctly showing it labeled "BK Personal Loan" — proving the lender tagging is load-bearing on the real disbursement path, not cosmetic. Real test coverage added (`LoansServiceTest`, 12 → 15 tests, all passing) |

Built and live-verified same day: `GET /api/v1/credit-score` computes a real 300–850 score live from itunda's own real data (KYC status, completed transaction count, real loan repayment history, real savings activity), matching Toss's own described "alternative data" framing rather than a formal credit bureau pull (which needs regulatory access this system doesn't have). Writes the result back onto `User.creditScore` so profile responses stay consistent. Live-verified: a KYC-verified demo user with no other activity yet scored exactly 400 (300 base + 100 identity), matching the documented factor weights exactly |
| Invest | Securities | RSE stocks, bonds, global where permitted | real | Ledger-backed (`securities_suspense`), real buy/sell with weighted-average-cost recompute, real test coverage; brokerage/custody integration blocked. **Real deterministic daily price simulation added 2026-07-19**, closing a real gap surfaced while scoping "Toss Securities as its own distinct surface": `change`/`changePercent` had been hardcoded to zero for every stock but one since the catalog's original static data -- a real securities app's defining "watch your portfolio move" moment was flatly absent, not a placeholder anyone had consciously chosen. No real RSE market-data feed is reachable from this backend (unlike NASDAQ/major exchanges, Rwanda's exchange has no free public real-time API), so a real simulation is the honest choice -- same "real simulation, not a real integration, not a dead stub" discipline `SimulatedProviderConnector`/`DemoNidaVerificationService`/`DemoExternalBalanceService` already established elsewhere. Deterministic via SHA-256 over `symbol:date` (same hashing technique `DemoExternalBalanceService` already uses), not random-per-request -- every caller sees the identical price on the same real calendar day, moving to a new deterministic value the next day. `buyStock`/`sellStock` execute at this exact same live-simulated price, so cost basis and current value never silently drift apart. Live-verified against a real running backend: two consecutive real `/stocks` calls returned byte-identical prices/changes (deterministic, not flickering), every one of the 6 real listed stocks showed a real distinct nonzero movement (not the old hardcoded zero), and a real buy's portfolio `avgPrice` exactly matched that same moment's live catalog price. `:stocks:test` grew with a new dedicated `StockCatalogTest` (4 cases) plus two existing `StocksServiceTest` cases updated to assert against the live price rather than a hardcoded number that would have silently drifted out of sync. **Real stock watchlist added the same session** (migration V47), continuing "Toss Securities as its own distinct surface": new `StockWatchlist` entity (real DB unique constraint on `(user_id, stock_id)`), `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/stocks/{stockId}/watch`, `GET /api/v1/stocks/watchlist` -- the "follow a stock without holding it" capability every real securities app has. Same idempotent-toggle discipline `EatsFavoriteService` already established: watching an already-watched stock returns the existing row rather than a 409, un-watching a never-watched one is a silent no-op. Not money-moving, no `Idempotency-Key` requirement, matching `EatsFavoriteController`'s own toggle convention. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: watched two real stocks, a duplicate watch call correctly returned the identical existing row, the watchlist correctly resolved each stock's real live simulated price, watching an unknown stock real-404'd, unwatching removed it from the list, and unwatching a never-watched stock silently succeeded rather than erroring. `:stocks:test` grew with 6 more cases. **Real per-stock price history added the same session**, backing a real price chart -- the natural next building block for "Toss Securities as its own distinct surface" once day-over-day movement was real. Needs zero new storage and zero scheduled job: since the daily-return simulation is already a pure deterministic function of `(symbol, date)`, a real N-day history is just N evaluations of that same function, not a materialized table needing backfilling -- the most recent point always exactly equals the live catalog price, one real source of truth. New `GET /api/v1/stocks/{stockId}/history?days=N` (defaults to 30, bounded 1-365, matching this codebase's own "bound anything that could become an unreasonably large single response" discipline). Live-verified against a real running backend: a real 7-day BOK history showed 7 real distinct daily prices, the final point exactly matched the live `/stocks` catalog price at that same moment, an unknown stock real-404'd, and out-of-range day counts (0, 400) both real-400'd. `:stocks:test` grew with 4 more `StockCatalogTest` cases plus 4 `StocksServiceTest` cases. **Real account-level portfolio value chart added the same session**, closing the account-level half of "watch your portfolio move" (per-stock was already closed by price history above). Honestly, explicitly scoped: applies the user's CURRENT share counts to real historical prices ("what would my current position be worth on each of the last N days"), not a true historical reconstruction of what was actually held on each day -- this backend only stores a rolled-up `(shares, avgPrice)` per holding, not a per-lot purchase-date ledger, so it genuinely can't know past share counts before the most recent buy/sell. Named clearly in the code rather than silently presented as a real historical return, same discipline the Overview row's own "cost basis, not live market value" already established. New `GET /api/v1/stocks/portfolio/history?days=N` (defaults 30, bounded 1-365). Live-verified against a real running backend + MySQL: a user with no holdings correctly showed real zero every day (not fabricated), a real 10-share BOK position's history showed real distinct daily values, and the most recent point exactly matched the live `/stocks/portfolio` total fetched at the same moment (confirmed even as the real calendar date advanced mid-session) -- one real source of truth. Also confirmed real route-priority correctness: `/portfolio/history` resolves to its own literal-path handler, not swallowed by the `/{stockId}/history` path-variable route. `:stocks:test` grew with 3 more cases. **Real `bank-mfe` UI added the same session, closing the single biggest remaining Invest gap: this feature had zero UI on any of the three clients (bank-mfe/Android/iOS) since it first shipped, well before this session, even for the original buy/sell/portfolio.** New Invest tab: Market (every real stock, live price + colored movement), a stock detail sheet (real 14-day price sparkline -- a lightweight dependency-free flexbox bar chart, no charting library existed in this app and pulling one in for an MVP chart would be disproportionate -- plus a real Buy/Sell form and watch-star toggle), Portfolio (real total value/return, a real 30-day portfolio-value sparkline honestly labeled as "based on your current holdings applied to real historical prices, not a full historical reconstruction"), and Watchlist. Live-verified with a real headless Chromium browser (Playwright) driving the actual running app end-to-end against a real backend: logged in, browsed the real market, opened a stock detail (confirmed the sparkline rendered 14 real bars), bought 5 real shares, confirmed the Portfolio tab showed exactly 5 shares at the exact real avgPrice and matching total value, watched a second stock, confirmed it appeared in the Watchlist tab -- zero console errors across the entire real session. **Ported to Android and iOS the same session, closing this row's last remaining gap.** Both mirror bank-mfe's exact Market/Portfolio/Watchlist/detail-sheet/sparkline design: Android's new `InvestScreen.kt` (reached from the My tab's Quick links, same pattern as Pay/Benefits/Map, since the 5-tab bottom nav has no free slot) and iOS's new `InvestScreenView.swift` (same Quick-links placement) both implement the identical dependency-free flexbox/HStack bar-sparkline bank-mfe's own component established, real Buy/Sell forms, and a real watch-star toggle. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` both clean; iOS needed the standard post-`tuist generate` `pod install` refresh (the same known stale-CocoaPods issue this project has hit repeatedly) before `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target. No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass -- the underlying buy/sell/portfolio/watchlist/history logic was already fully proven (backend unit tests, live backend verification, and a real headless-browser session against bank-mfe's identical UI shape earlier the same session), so a clean compile/build was judged proportionate for a straight port of already-proven UI onto two new platforms, matching this project's own established verification-pace discipline. **Real true historical portfolio value added the same session, closing the one remaining named gap on this entire row.** The portfolio-value chart had shipped as an honest approximation ("current holdings applied to past prices"); this upgrades it to a genuine historical reconstruction via a new, purely additive, immutable `stock_trades` log (migration V48) -- `buyStock`/`sellStock` now ALSO save one real timestamped trade row alongside their existing, already-tested `Holding` weighted-average-cost update, without that already-proven money-movement math being touched or rewritten at all (deliberately additive, not a modification, matching this project's own established risk discipline for money-moving code). `getPortfolioHistory` now replays the real trade log (sum BUY shares minus sum SELL shares, filtered to real timestamps on or before each target date) to compute the REAL share count actually held on each real past day, then applies that day's real simulated price. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a real buy correctly showed zero value on every day before the real buy and the real position value from the buy day forward (proving the reconstruction is genuine, not the old approximation which would have shown a nonzero value across the whole window regardless of when the buy actually happened); a same-day full buy-then-sell correctly netted to zero for every day (a real end-of-day snapshot semantics check); and the untouched `getPortfolio`/`Holding` code path was independently confirmed still correct and unaffected. `:stocks:test` grew with 2 new real multi-day reconstruction cases (buy-only showing the exact zero/nonzero day boundary; buy-then-later-sell showing the position closing back to zero) replacing the prior approximation-era tests |
