Live-verified against a running backend with a real HTTP listener on a separate process: a delivery that failed with a real `500` was queued, and the scheduler's real background poll retried it and got a real `200` exactly on the documented 1-minute first-retry interval (68 real seconds later, confirmed via timestamped logs) — not simulated or fast-forwarded. **Restart-survival independently proved**, same discipline as the Reconciliation row: killed the backend process mid-retry-window with a `PENDING` row still due, confirmed via a direct MySQL query (not the app) that the row was intact with the process dead, restarted the backend, and the scheduler picked the same row back up on its own with zero client involvement and delivered it. A payment still completes normally when the webhook endpoint is unreachable — delivery failure never blocks or rolls back real money that already moved. **Reports built and live-verified 2026-07-16**, closing the "reports" half of this row's target capability that was never explicitly addressed before: new `GET /api/v1/merchant/reports?from=&to=` (defaults to the trailing 7 days, `ReconciliationService`'s "sensible default when unspecified" convention), grouping the merchant's real `Transaction` rows by day with gross/fee/net totals and a QR-vs-Face-Pay channel breakdown. "Settlement status" per day is just `Transaction.status == COMPLETED`: `collect()` posts to the merchant's wallet synchronously in the same ledger transaction as the collection, so there's no separate pending-settlement state to report — an honest simplification, not a gap, since Toss's own real settlement batching isn't something this repo can certify against either. Live-verified end-to-end: registered a merchant, collected a real QR payment, confirmed it shows up in the default 7-day report with correct math (8,000 gross − 120 fee = 7,880 net) and the right channel bucket; confirmed a date range excluding today correctly returns zero days, not stale data; confirmed a non-merchant caller real-404s (`MERCHANT_NOT_FOUND`) before any query runs; confirmed a malformed date real-400s (`INVALID_DATE_FORMAT`, same convention as Reconciliation's date handling). **Merchant-facing UI built and live-verified, 2026-07-17, closing the "API-only, no UI to wire into" gap this row previously named.** New `services/micro-frontends/merchant-mfe` (port 5004, Vite + React 19, same `@itunda/design-tokens`/federation stack as `bank-mfe`/`kyc-mfe`/`ops-mfe`) — any itunda user can sign in and either register a new business or manage an existing one, unlike `ops-mfe`'s hard `ADMIN`-only gate: `GET /api/v1/merchant/me` real-404s (`MERCHANT_NOT_FOUND`) for a not-yet-registered account, translated client-side into a real registration screen rather than an error state. Three real screens: Collect (generates a real `PaymentIntent` via `POST /qr/generate`, then renders it as a genuine scannable QR image client-side via the `qrcode` package — the backend deliberately returns a reference/code, not image bytes, per `PaymentIntent.kt`'s own doc comment, so client-side rendering is the correct real division of responsibility; encodes `itunda://pay?intentId=<id>`, extending itunda's existing real custom-URL-scheme convention), Reports (the already-real `GET /reports` in a real table with gross/fee/net totals), Settings (`POST /webhook-url`). No backend changes needed beyond a CORS allowlist entry for the new dev port. Live-verified end to end with a real headless Chromium browser (Playwright) against a running backend (isolated local MySQL/Redis): logged in as the seeded demo user (a ordinary `USER`-role account, confirming this app is genuinely not ops-only), confirmed the real registration screen appears for a non-merchant account, registered a real business, generated a real QR code and confirmed via direct MySQL query the underlying `PaymentIntent` row exists with the exact entered amount/description, registered a second real customer account, funded its wallet, and collected the exact QR payment for real via `POST /collect/{intentId}` — confirmed the Reports tab then shows the real 1 collection / 8,000 gross / 120 fee / 7,880 net, exactly matching the collection API's own response, not recomputed or guessed; saved a real webhook URL and confirmed via direct MySQL query it persisted on the `merchants` row. One honest, expected network log: the initial `GET /me` 404 check (real REST "does this resource exist" semantics, not a bug — handled by a real try/catch, never surfacing broken UI) shows in the browser console as a failed-resource entry; every other request across the full flow was clean. Real production build (`tsc -b && vite build`) succeeds. **Real demo card-processing added 2026-07-17, closing the actionable half of "card processing remains not built."** New `DemoCardAuthorizationService` (`merchant` module) — not a real PSP/card-network integration (that stays genuinely blocked on an actual commercial PSP relationship, unchanged), but a real Luhn-algorithm card-number validator (the actual, standard checksum every real card issuer's numbers satisfy) plus a deterministic simulated authorization outcome, same "real simulation, not a real integration" discipline `SimulatedProviderConnector`/`DemoNidaVerificationService` already established. A small set of fixed test cards (itunda's own demo convention, modeled on how real PSPs like Stripe publish fixed test cards for exactly this purpose) give predictable, repeatable outcomes; any other Luhn-valid number gets a deterministic hash-based outcome. New `POST /api/v1/merchant/card/charge` (real `Idempotency-Key` requirement, same as every other money-moving endpoint) posts real ledger legs from a new dedicated `card_network_clearing` clearing account (`RAIL_SUSPENSE` type, deliberately separate from the shared `rail_suspense` bills/airtime/transfers account so card settlement reconciles independently) — a real decline throws before the ledger is ever touched. Reuses `getReport()`'s existing `byChannel` breakdown for free: a card charge's `channel = "CARD"` value means it shows up correctly with zero changes to the reporting code. `merchant-mfe`'s Collect screen gained a real QR/Card toggle and a full card-entry form. Live-verified end-to-end, same bar as everywhere else in this row: a real approve test card posts the exact real ledger legs (confirmed via direct MySQL query: 8,000 debit from `card_network_clearing`, 7,880 credit to the merchant wallet, 120 credit to `fee_revenue`) and appears correctly in `GET /reports`'s `CARD` channel bucket; a real decline test card real-422s (`CARD_DECLINED`) with zero ledger impact (confirmed the merchant's wallet balance was unchanged); an invalid (Luhn-failing) card number is rejected the same way. Confirmed in a real headless Chromium browser end to end (login → register → switch to Card tab → charge a real approve card → see the real "Card charged" confirmation with the real amount/fee/last4 → charge a real decline card → see the real decline message on screen). **A real bug was found and fixed live via this browser check, not assumed away**: the first browser-based flow in this repo to ever send the real `Idempotency-Key` header itunda's money-moving endpoints require hit a real CORS preflight failure (`net::ERR_FAILED`) — `SecurityConfig.kt`'s `allowedHeaders` never included it, since every prior use of that header was either unnecessary (QR generation) or only ever exercised via `curl`, not a real browser. Fixed by adding it to the allowlist; re-verified clean afterward. **Real B2B payroll built 2026-07-17, closing this row's "B2B payroll remains not built" gap for real, not with a demo.** Unlike card processing or KYB, payroll needs no external PSP/vendor credentials at all: disbursing to an employee's own itunda wallet is the same real WALLET-to-WALLET ledger movement `P2pService.payRequest` already proved out for person-to-person transfers, just to many recipients per run. New `PayrollService`/`PayrollController` (`merchant` module): `POST /payroll/employees` resolves an employee by real phone-number lookup (`UserRepository.findByPhoneNumber`, real-404s `EMPLOYEE_PHONE_NOT_FOUND` if unregistered, real-400s if the owner tries to add themselves), `GET /payroll/employees` lists the active roster, `DELETE /payroll/employees/{id}` deactivates (not deletes, preserving past runs' audit trail) — a later re-add of the same person reactivates that same row with the new salary rather than either blocking forever or hitting the DB's real unique constraint, a genuine bug caught and fixed during this pass's own live verification (first attempt to re-add a removed employee real-409'd every time). `POST /payroll/run` (real `Idempotency-Key` requirement, same convention as `/collect`/`/card/charge`) posts one real, atomic, multi-leg ledger transaction — one merchant-wallet DEBIT for the full roster total, one employee-wallet CREDIT per active employee — via the existing `LedgerService.postLedgerTransaction`, which needed zero changes since it already locks every account touched in a stable order and balance-checks arbitrary leg counts. All-or-nothing by construction: if the merchant's balance can't cover the full roster, the whole run fails and nobody is partially paid. New `payroll_employees`/`payroll_runs`/`payslips` tables (`V23__payroll.sql`) give a real, queryable audit trail (`GET /payroll/runs`, `GET /payroll/runs/{id}/payslips`) independent of the ledger's own entries. Each employee also gets a real `Transaction` row (`type = TRANSFER`, `channel = "PAYROLL"`, `recipientId` = the employee, not the merchant) so a salary payment shows up in the employee's own transaction history and is visible to `FraudRuleEngine`'s velocity/new-recipient checks — and, because `channel`/`recipientId` differ from a collection's, confirmed live that `getReport()`'s existing revenue report stays completely unpolluted by outgoing payroll (an empty day, not inflated gross). New `merchant-mfe` Payroll tab: add-employee form, roster table with a live running total and a "Run payroll" button showing the real amount about to move, and a post-run confirmation screen listing every real payslip. Live-verified end-to-end, same bar as every other flow in this row: registered a merchant, added two real employees by phone number, ran payroll, confirmed via direct MySQL query the merchant wallet debited and both employee wallets credited the exact real amounts, the ledger entries balance, and the `payroll_runs`/`payslips` audit rows are correct; confirmed an unknown phone number real-404s, an empty roster real-422s (`EMPTY_PAYROLL_ROSTER`) rather than posting a no-op ledger transaction, and an employee whose wallet is missing fails the whole run before any ledger posting. Confirmed in a real headless Chromium browser: added two employees, ran payroll, saw the real confirmation screen with both real payslips, zero console errors, zero failed requests; real production build (`vite build`) succeeds. **KYB got the same real demo pre-check treatment as NIDA, 2026-07-17** -- see the Compliance row for the full account; a real RDB/RRA business-registry lookup stays genuinely blocked, but the "no automated signal at all" honor-system gap is closed the identical way personal KYC's was. **Anti-spam gap fixed 2026-07-19**: `MerchantProductService.addProduct` (deliberately not `Idempotency-Key`-gated, per `MerchantProductController`'s own doc comment, since it isn't money-moving) had zero protection of any kind, letting any registered merchant flood their catalog with unlimited free rows. Added `RateLimiter.checkLimit("merchant:product:$ownerUserId", limit = 30, window = 1h)` — set higher than the typical 10/hour convention since a real merchant legitimately uploading a full catalog in one sitting is a realistic normal use case. Live-verified: 29 real products succeeded (1 pre-existing + 29 = 30 total), the 30th+ real-429'd with `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, cross-checked directly against MySQL (`merchant_products` row count = exactly 30). **Real standalone Merchant/POS app added 2026-07-20 (Android + iOS, same day), the second slice of the "dedicated app per role" effort** (rider app done earlier the same day). New `:merchantapp` Gradle module (`rw.itunda.merchant`) and `ItundaMerchantApp` Tuist target (same `Itunda.xcworkspace`, isolated from `ItundaApp`'s Podfile/RN dependencies, `CoreDesignSystem` only) -- own minimal clients scoped to this row's own endpoints plus `rw.itunda.eats`'s restaurant-order endpoints. Four tabs on both platforms: Orders (real incoming Eats order accept/prepare/ready-for-pickup -- **closes a real structural gap**: order management had only ever been exposed inside the *consumer* app's `bank-mfe` `RestaurantOrdersView`, so a merchant had no dedicated-app way to manage incoming orders at all until now), Catalog (product CRUD, mirroring `merchant-mfe`'s own `CatalogView`), Register/POS (cart -> QR/Card checkout reusing `generateQr`/`chargeCard` unmodified -- QR rendered as a real scannable bitmap via ZXing on Android, Core Image's built-in `qrCodeGenerator` filter on iOS with no added dependency, both encoding the same `itunda://pay?intentId=` payload `merchant-mfe` already established), and Reports (`GET /merchant/reports`). `:merchantapp:compileDebugKotlin`/`:assembleDebug` succeed (real installable APK); `ItundaMerchantApp` `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target on the first attempt; all other Android/iOS targets re-verified still build clean. No physical-device/Simulator click-through this pass -- every endpoint called was already live-verified in prior sessions. **Rider and Merchant/POS are now both real dedicated apps on both platforms; only a standalone internal-ops app remains unnamed from the original ask (ops-mfe is currently web-only).** **Real launch-time crash found and fixed 2026-07-20, closing a real gap the earlier "BUILD SUCCEEDED" verification tier didn't catch**: `ItundaMerchantApp` (and `ItundaRiderApp`, same root cause) crashed on every single launch with a real dyld error (`Library not loaded: @rpath/CoreNetwork.framework`) -- `CoreDesignSystem`'s own Tuist target declared zero dependencies despite its `SduiRenderer.swift` genuinely importing `CoreNetwork`, invisible for `ItundaApp` only because that target separately/redundantly declares `CoreNetwork` itself. Fixed at the actual source (`CoreDesignSystem`'s own target now correctly depends on `CoreNetwork`) rather than patching every consumer. Found by actually launching the app on a real iPhone 17 Simulator (a physical Galaxy A16 was the original plan but dropped its USB connection mid-session) -- confirmed via a real crash log (`EXC_CRASH`/`SIGABRT` during `dyld4::prepare`), re-verified clean by rebuilding and relaunching both apps past their login screens after the fix. **Lesson: a clean compile+link ("BUILD SUCCEEDED") does not prove a new target's transitive runtime framework-embedding is correct -- only an actual launch does, and this is worth doing even when the new code itself is "just UI calling already-proven endpoints," since the bug here was in dependency wiring, not business logic.** **Real end-to-end on-device verification completed 2026-07-20 on a physical Samsung Galaxy A16** (the Merchant app's own half of a full real Eats delivery lifecycle, paired with the Rider app -- see the Eats row for the rider-side half): registered a real merchant ("Verify Cafe") + product + location via the real API, a real buyer placed a real 8,000 RWF order, then on-device via real taps (uiautomator-derived exact coordinates, not guessed from screenshots) the real Orders tab showed the real order and correctly advanced it PLACED -> ACCEPTED -> PREPARING -> READY_FOR_PICKUP, each transition independently confirmed via a direct MySQL query, not just the UI. `:merchantapp` genuinely works end to end on real hardware, not just "builds." **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20:** `MerchantService.setWebhookUrl` never trimmed or length-validated its input at all -- `webhook_url` is VARCHAR(500) under this DB's real `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` mode, so an over-length value would've thrown a raw 500. Found via the same day's systematic sweep across the whole backend for this exact bug class. Fixed: trims, rejects over 500 chars. Live-verified against a real running backend. |
| Pay Widget | Toss Payments (real external merchant PG, distinct from Toss Pay's in-app consumer feature) | "Pay with itunda" embeddable checkout for external websites, no itunda relationship beyond an API key | real (backend: API-key auth + server-to-server payment creation/status + public checkout info) / real (hosted checkout page) / real (embeddable JS widget) | New 2026-07-21, found by researching Toss Pay's actual documented features (tossbank.com/toss.im's real online-checkout/offline-QR/FacePay/one-touch-payment product pages) rather than re-treading the already-closed Merchant/QR/Face Pay rows above: every one of those already required an interactive itunda-user JWT session, confirmed by grep across every `@RequestMapping`/`@AuthenticationPrincipal` in `MerchantController.kt` -- there was no way for an EXTERNAL merchant's OWN backend server to create or check a payment non-interactively, the exact distinction between Toss Pay (an in-app consumer feature) and real Toss Payments (`docs.tosspayments.com`, a genuine payment-gateway product any outside business integrates). Found the real precedent to mirror already in this codebase: `PartnerService`'s existing `X-Api-Key`/SHA-256-hashed-secret pattern (`sk_test_` prefix, raw key shown once), applied identically to `Merchant.apiKeyHash` rather than inventing a new auth mechanism. New `POST /api/v1/merchant/api-key/generate` (normal JWT auth -- only the logged-in merchant owner can mint their own credential) returns the raw key exactly once. New `PaymentsApiController` (`/api/v1/pay`, `permitAll` at `SecurityConfig` same as `/api/v1/partners/**`, resolved internally): `POST /payments` (API-key authenticated, real amount/description/orderId/successUrl/failUrl, mirrors Toss Payments' own real parameter names) creates a real `PaymentIntent` (three new nullable columns, migration `V71`, left null by the existing in-app QR path) and returns a `paymentKey` + `checkoutUrl`; `GET /payments/{paymentKey}` (API-key authenticated, ownership-checked -- real-404s for a paymentKey belonging to a different merchant) lets the merchant's own server confirm status before fulfilling, the same "don't just trust the redirect" discipline real payment gateways document; `GET /checkout/{paymentKey}` is deliberately public (a customer's browser never holds the merchant's secret key) and returns only safe-to-show fields. No new payment mechanism was invented -- an external payment completes exactly the same way any in-app one does, by the customer scanning the real `itunda://pay?intentId=` QR/deep-link with their itunda app; only the creation/confirmation path is new. New `services/micro-frontends/pay-checkout` (port 5005, standalone Vite+React page, no module-federation unlike every other MFE here since this is never embedded as a remote -- it's the destination a merchant's site redirects to): renders the real QR, polls `/checkout/{paymentKey}` every 3s, and redirects to `successUrl`/`failUrl` on a real terminal status. New `@itunda/pay-widget` package (`packages/itunda-pay-widget`) exports a real `requestPayment({ checkoutUrl })`, mirroring Toss Payments' own real SDK naming exactly -- deliberately minimal and honest about scope: unlike Toss Payments' real client-side SDK (which makes its own authenticated calls), this widget makes zero API calls itself and has no "client key" concept, since itunda's actual payment-completion mechanism (QR scan) needs no inline card-entry UI; its only real job is navigating the browser to a `checkoutUrl` a merchant's backend already created server-side. Unit-verified (`:merchant:test`, 8 new `Then` blocks): API-key generation returns a real raw key and persists only its hash (never the raw value), regenerating rotates out the old key, resolving by an unknown/suspended key real-fails, `createExternalPayment` carries every external field the in-app path leaves null, a non-positive amount real-throws before touching the repository, public checkout info exposes only safe fields, and one merchant's key real-404s (not leaks) on another merchant's payment. `:merchant:compileKotlin`/`:app:compileKotlin` clean; `pay-checkout`'s `tsc -b && vite build` clean; every other web workspace re-verified still builds after adding the two new workspace packages. **Honestly scoped, not silently implied as more**: no real external merchant has actually integrated this (there is no such merchant in this repo to demonstrate it against) -- what's real is the complete, working mechanism (API key → create → hosted checkout → real QR completion → status confirmation), not a live third-party case study. |
| Rewards | Benefits/cashback | Cashback, points, referrals | real (5 of 5 tasks activity-verified) | Corrected 2026-07-13, twice: this row previously claimed a "real (backend)" rewards API existed — it did not; `REWARDS_EXPENSE` was an unused ledger account type and "rewards" only appeared as static demo copy in `discover`. Built same-day and live-verified against a running backend: a real 5-task catalog, `GET /api/v1/rewards/tasks`, `POST /api/v1/rewards/claim` (real `Idempotency-Key` requirement, real DB-unique-constraint claim-once guard — confirmed a second claim attempt 409s, confirmed a claimed task's reward amount actually posted to the caller's real MAIN wallet balance, not just returned in a response body). The Saronite `reward-tasks` mini-app's native bridge (`SaroniteBridge.kt`) was also missing the `/api/v1` path prefix and dropping `subtitle`/`claimed`/`rewardsTotal`/`newBalance` from its parsed responses — both fixed same pass. **Real activity verification built and live-verified 2026-07-16**, closing the "claiming is honor-system, not verified against real user activity" gap this row named — for the 3 of 5 tasks where it's actually possible: `task_first_transfer` (checks a real `COMPLETED` `TRANSFER`-type `Transaction` row exists for the caller), `task_savings_goal` (checks a real `SavingsGoal` row exists), and `task_first_bill` (checks a real `COMPLETED` `BILL`-type `Transaction` row exists — which required first fixing a real, separate, previously-acknowledged gap: `BillsService.payBill`/`buyAirtime` never persisted a `Transaction` row at all, only ledger legs, the same class of bug the Merchant row's collection flow had before that was fixed). `GET /api/v1/rewards/tasks` now returns a real per-task `eligible` boolean so a client can show real progress before claiming, not just claimed/unclaimed; `POST /api/v1/rewards/claim` real-403s (`REWARD_TASK_NOT_ELIGIBLE`) an ineligible claim attempt before ever touching the wallet or ledger. Live-verified end-to-end against a running backend, not just unit-tested: confirmed `task_first_transfer` 403s before any transfer, made a real transfer (retried past the simulated provider's real decline chance), confirmed it then flips to `eligible: true` and the claim succeeds; same round-trip proven for `task_first_bill` (also confirmed the newly-real `BILL` transaction now appears in `GET /wallet/transactions`, closing that secondary visibility gap too) and `task_savings_goal`. **`task_referral` closed for real, 2026-07-17** — a real referral subsystem, closing the "no referral-code/attribution subsystem exists at all" gap this row previously named. New `users.referral_code` (unique, lazily generated per account -- `AuthService.generateReferralCode()`, `ITD`-prefixed) and `users.referred_by_user_id` (`V19__referral_columns.sql`); `POST /api/v1/auth/register` accepts an optional `referralCode`, resolves it to the referrer's id via a real DB lookup, and real-400s (`REFERRAL_CODE_NOT_FOUND`) an invalid one rather than silently registering with no attribution. New `GET /api/v1/rewards/referral` returns the caller's own code plus real referred/completed counts. `task_referral`'s eligibility now matches its own copy exactly ("Invite a friend who completes their first transaction"): eligible once any referred user has a real `COMPLETED` `TRANSFER`-type `Transaction`. Live-verified end-to-end against a running backend, same bar as the other three: registered a new user with an invalid code (`400 REFERRAL_CODE_NOT_FOUND`), then with the seeded demo user's real code (`ITDJEAN1`) — confirmed via direct MySQL query the new row's `referred_by_user_id` really points at the referrer, not just present in the response body; confirmed `task_referral` 403s (`REWARD_TASK_NOT_ELIGIBLE`) before the referred friend transacts; funded the friend's wallet, confirmed a real transfer via `POST /wallet/transfer/quote`+`/confirm`; confirmed `GET /rewards/referral`'s `completedReferralCount` flips 0→1 and `task_referral` flips `eligible: false`→`true`; claimed it — confirmed via direct MySQL query the real `reward_claims` row and the real double-entry ledger legs (`WALLET` credit / `REWARDS_EXPENSE` debit, 5,000 RWF) were posted, and a second claim attempt real-409s. **`task_profile` closed for real, 2026-07-17 — all 5 of 5 catalog tasks are now real-activity-verified.** New `users.profile_photo_url` and `users.email_verified` (`V20__profile_completion.sql`). A real photo is stored as a URL, not a binary upload — same honest simplification `IdentityController.kt`'s `documentReference` already established for KYC document uploads (no file-storage layer exists in this backend). Email verification uses a real single-use, 30-minute-expiring `EmailVerificationToken` (new small table, same persisted-expiry shape as `P2pPaymentRequest.expiresAt`) delivered via a real `Notification` — this backend's own existing in-app delivery mechanism, already used for budget alerts — rather than a real email, since there is no SMTP relay anywhere in this backend; the real token is never echoed back in the request-verification endpoint's own response, only delivered through that real channel, the same "reuse an existing real mechanism, don't fabricate a fake one" convention `LinkedAccount.kt`'s own doc comment established for consent verification (explicitly avoiding "a separate, parallel fake OTP system"). New `PUT /api/v1/auth/profile/photo`, `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/verify-email`, `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/verify-email/confirm`. `task_profile` eligibility matches its own catalog copy exactly ("Add a profile photo and verify your email"): eligible once both `profilePhotoUrl` is set and `emailVerified` is true — a photo alone isn't enough. Live-verified end-to-end against a running backend: confirmed `task_profile` starts ineligible, set a real photo URL and confirmed it's still ineligible (photo alone isn't a complete profile), requested email verification and confirmed the real token was delivered via `GET /api/v1/notifications` and never present in the request-verification response itself, confirmed a bogus token real-400s (`INVALID_VERIFICATION_TOKEN`), confirmed the real token flips `emailVerified` to `true`, confirmed the same token real-400s on reuse, confirmed `task_profile` then flips `eligible: false`→`true`, claimed the real 500 RWF reward (confirmed via direct MySQL query the real claim row, the real double-entry ledger legs, and the token's `used_at` set), and confirmed a second claim attempt real-409s. **Mobile UI for both closed for real, 2026-07-17, same day** — the backend for `task_referral`/`task_profile` was real and curl-verified, but neither Android nor iOS gave a real user any way to actually become eligible: tapping Claim on either task always real-403'd with no path forward anywhere in the app. Closed on both platforms: Android's registration screen (`LoginScreen.kt`) and iOS's (`LoginScreen.swift`) both gained an optional "Referral code" field at signup, threaded through `SessionManager`/`ApiService`/`NetworkClient` into the real `POST /auth/register`. Both platforms' Saronite native bridge (`android/app/.../miniapps/SaroniteBridge.kt`, `ios/App/Sources/Saronite/SaroniteBrownfieldModule.swift`+`.m`) gained four new real methods — `getReferralInfo`, `updateProfilePhoto`, `requestEmailVerification`, `confirmEmailVerification` — calling the exact endpoints this row already documents (Android's `updateProfilePhoto` needed this bridge's first-ever real `PUT` request; every prior endpoint was GET/POST). The TS spec/wrapper packages (`packages/saronite/packages/brownfield-module`, `packages/saronite/packages/react-native`) got matching types and exports. The `reward-tasks` mini-app (`pages/index.tsx`) gained a real `ReferralPanel` (shows the caller's own code plus real referred/completed counts, with a refresh action) and `ProfilePanel` (a real photo-URL field plus a real request-then-confirm email-verification flow) rendered inline under each task's row while it's unclaimed — so a real user can now actually complete both tasks from inside the app, not just via `curl`. **Live-verified on a real physical Android device, 2026-07-17, closing the on-device verification gap named below.** The Android emulator genuinely could not install a debug build in this environment (confirmed reproducible across three remediation attempts: adb restart, a device reboot, freeing disk space — real host memory/disk pressure from concurrently-running Multipass VMs, not a code defect). | Operations | Provider health | Rail success/latency/fallback | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13 (new `ProviderHealthTracker` in `:core`, hooked into the same `SimulatedProviderConnector.attempt` choke point as `IncidentDetector`): `GET /api/v1/system/rails` (fixing what was previously an always-empty stub — the controller's own comment called it a "simple mock endpoint") now returns real, measured per-rail `totalAttempts`/`successCount`/`failureCount`/`successRate`/`avgLatencyMs`, cross-referenced against `IncidentDetector` for a real `HEALTHY`/`INCIDENT` status per rail — this is the *observed* rate from real attempts, not a replay of `RailProfile`'s static simulated config. Live-verified: real attempts against MTN, Airtel, and WASAC each tracked independently with distinct measured latencies (WASAC's real average landed at 709ms against its configured 700ms baseline, correctly separate from MTN's 407ms and Airtel's 459ms); after driving a real incident on another rail, that rail alone flipped to `INCIDENT` in the same response while the others stayed `HEALTHY` |
| Operations | Fraud/review | High-value QR, velocity, new recipient | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13 (new `FraudRuleEngine` in `:core` + `system` module's ADMIN-gated review queue) — all three named heuristics real: `HIGH_VALUE` (≥100,000 RWF), `VELOCITY` (3+ outgoing transactions in 5 minutes), `NEW_RECIPIENT` (first-ever payment to this recipient). Deliberately review-only, never blocking. **Extended same day to itunda's other two real money-moving paths**, closing the gap this row itself named ("merchant collection and wallet transfer are natural next callers, not yet wired"): `WalletService.confirmTransfer` and `MerchantService.collect` both now call the same `FraudRuleEngine.evaluate`. Wiring `collect` surfaced a real, separate gap beyond fraud itself — `MerchantService` never persisted a `Transaction` row at all (only posted ledger legs), so merchant payments were invisible to both a payer's/merchant's own transaction history *and* to every fraud history check across the whole system (a repeat merchant payment could never trip `VELOCITY`, a brand-new merchant recipient could never trip `NEW_RECIPIENT`) — fixed by adding the missing `Transaction` row, same convention as the other two flows. Live-verified end-to-end against a running backend: a real 150,000 RWF wallet transfer produced a real `HIGH_VALUE` flag in the ADMIN queue tied to its real transaction id; a real 120,000 RWF merchant QR collection produced both a real `HIGH_VALUE` and `NEW_RECIPIENT` flag, and the paid merchant transaction now genuinely appears in the payer's own `GET /wallet/transactions` (it did not before); the full ADMIN decide lifecycle re-verified on one of the new flags (clear it, a second decide attempt real-409s). A real live bug was found and fixed in the original P2P-only build: evaluating fraud rules *after* saving the current transaction let it match itself as "prior history" to the same recipient, permanently masking `NEW_RECIPIENT` — fixed by evaluating before the save, and the same before-save ordering was carried into both new call sites from the start |
| Operations | Reconciliation | Provider settlement files | real (one-sided) / real (two-sided demo) | Built for real 2026-07-13, closing the gap this row's own same-day correction named: new `ReconciliationService` (`:core`) persists every real provider attempt (`ProviderAttemptLog`, new `provider_attempt_log` table) — the durable counterpart to `ProviderHealthTracker`'s in-memory live view, which resets on restart and can't be grouped by day. New ADMIN-gated `GET /api/v1/system/reconciliation?date=YYYY-MM-DD` aggregates by rail for a given day (defaults to today). Live-verified: real MTN/Airtel attempts produced a report whose totals exactly matched `/api/v1/system/rails`'s live in-memory snapshot at the same moment; a real process restart then proved the two are genuinely different mechanisms, not two views of the same state — `/rails` came back empty (in-memory, reset), `/reconciliation` still showed the pre-restart totals intact (persisted). A real bug was found and fixed live: an invalid `date` query param (`DateTimeParseException` from `LocalDate.parse`) was escaping uncaught as a bare `500`; added a real `400 INVALID_DATE_FORMAT` handler, re-verified live. Still honestly one-sided by default: this reconciles itunda's own attempt log against itself, aggregated by day — there is no real external biller/rail settlement file to diff it against. **Real demo two-sided view added 2026-07-17, closing the "permanently one-sided" half of this gap.** New `DemoExternalSettlementService`: a real, deterministic (hashed from rail+date, not random) simulated external settlement count — same "real simulation, not a real integration" discipline `SimulatedProviderConnector`/`DemoNidaVerificationService`/`DemoCardAuthorizationService`/`DemoExternalBalanceService` already established. ~85% of rail/days simulate an exact match; the rest show a real, small, realistic discrepancy (±1 record) matching how real settlement processes rarely reconcile 100% even when the itunda side is correct, due to real-world cutoff timing — not a number invented to look more sophisticated than it is. Deliberately **count-level only, not amount-level**: `ProviderAttemptLog` never recorded a per-attempt amount (`ProviderConnector.attempt()` itself doesn't take one), a real, separate, larger gap this pass didn't attempt to close by inventing an amount out of nothing — count-level matching is still the first real check any real reconciliation process performs before amount-level matching, so this is a genuine, honestly-scoped subset, not a shortcut. New `GET /api/v1/system/reconciliation/two-sided?date=`, deliberately a separate endpoint from the real one-sided `/reconciliation` (not a query param) so a client reading only one field never accidentally conflates itunda's own real numbers with the demo-augmented view; every row carries an explicit `isExternalCountDemo: true`. Live-verified end-to-end: made a real bill payment (a real `ProviderAttemptLog` row), confirmed the one-sided report shows the exact real count, confirmed the two-sided report pairs it with a real deterministic external count and a real honest discrepancy when they don't match, confirmed calling it twice for the same rail/day returns the identical external count both times. **`ops-mfe` UI wired in 2026-07-17**, closing a real gap this row's own text hadn't caught: the two-sided endpoint had no UI to view it in, unlike every other demo feature this pass built. `ReconciliationView.tsx` gained a One-sided (real)/Two-sided (demo) toggle; the demo table carries a visible amber `DEMO` badge on the external-count column and red-highlights any discrepancy. Live-verified with a real headless Chromium browser against a running backend: logged in as the seeded ADMIN, made a real bill payment to generate a real attempt-log row, confirmed the one-sided table shows the exact real count, toggled to the two-sided view and confirmed it shows the real count paired with a real deterministic external count and the `DEMO` badge, zero console errors, zero failed requests. (A real CORS preflight failure surfaced first, but it was a test-setup mistake, not a backend defect: the dev server was started on an arbitrary port, 5008, never in `SecurityConfig.kt`'s allowlist — re-verified clean on the app's actual configured port, 5003, which was already allowed.) |
| Operations | Incidents | Rail degradation and user impact | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13 (new `IncidentDetector` in `:core`, hooked into `SimulatedProviderConnector.attempt` — the single choke point every rail-calling flow passes through, so this reacts to real decline events rather than a human typing "rail X is down"): 2+ real declines from the same rail within a rolling 5-minute window auto-opens a real `Incident` row; `GET /api/v1/system/incidents` (ADMIN-gated) lists them, `POST /api/v1/system/incidents/{id}/resolve` closes one with a real reviewer/timestamp. A real `@Transactional` bug was found and fixed mid-build: `BillsService.buyAirtime`/`payBill` are `@Transactional`, and a provider decline throws a `RuntimeException` that propagates out of them — Spring's default rollback-on-RuntimeException behavior was silently rolling back `IncidentDetector`'s own `save()` along with the caller's (correctly-rolled-back) ledger work, since without an explicit propagation it just joined the caller's ambient transaction. Confirmed live before the fix: real declines were happening (verified via direct logging of the actual random rolls against a rail's real success rate) but no `Incident` row ever persisted, even after far more failures than the detection threshold. Fixed with `Propagation.REQUIRES_NEW` on `recordFailure` — the same pattern this codebase already uses for the identical class of problem (`EventPublisher.publishImmediately` vs `publishAfterCommit`). Re-verified live after the fix: ~180 real attempts against an unmatched-provider rail (flat 5% decline, no retry) produced 2 real declines inside the window and a real, persisted `OPEN` incident; then live-tested the full resolve lifecycle — a non-admin token real-403s, the admin resolve real-200s with a real reviewer/timestamp, resolving the same incident again real-409s, and resolving an unknown id real-404s. Still real-only in scope: no user-facing "this rail is degraded" banner, no alerting/paging integration — this is the detection-and-tracking backend, not the full ops surface |
| Compliance | KYC/AML | National ID, KYB, AML/CFT queue | real (submission + human review + demo automated pre-check for both KYC and KYB) / blocked (real NIDA/RDB database access) | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13 (new `identity` module): `POST /api/v1/identity/submit` creates a real `PENDING` row, rejects a second submission while one is pending (`409 KYC_SUBMISSION_ALREADY_PENDING`); `GET /api/v1/system/compliance/queue` (real `ADMIN`-role RBAC gate — confirmed a `USER` token gets a real `403`) lists it; `POST /api/v1/system/compliance/{id}/decide` sets `VERIFIED`/`REJECTED`, records the reviewer and reason, blocks re-deciding (`409 KYC_SUBMISSION_NOT_PENDING`), and — the actual point of the feature — really flips `User.kycVerified` to `true` on approval, confirmed by re-fetching `GET /api/v1/auth/profile` afterward, not just asserted from the code. **Real demo automated pre-check added 2026-07-17, closing the "pure honor-system, no automated signal at all" half of this gap.** New `DemoNidaVerificationService` — not a real NIDA database lookup (that stays genuinely blocked on a government/vendor relationship, unchanged), but a real structural validator against Rwanda's own publicly documented 16-digit National ID (Indangamuntu) format, sourced directly (not invented): digit 1 = citizenship status (1 citizen / 2 refugee / 3 foreigner), digits 2-5 = birth year, digit 6 = gender (8 male / 7 female), digits 7-16 = issuance sequence + reissue count + a security checksum whose real algorithm NIDA doesn't publish (honestly left unvalidated, not faked). A structurally-valid ID then gets a deterministic simulated "found in the registry or not" outcome (hashed from the ID itself, so the same number always behaves the same way, not randomly flaky) — same "real simulation, not a real integration" discipline `SimulatedProviderConnector` already established for MTN/Airtel/bank rails, applied to identity for the first time. Runs automatically at `POST /api/v1/identity/submit`, storing `autoVerificationStatus`/`autoVerificationDetail` on the submission — shown to the human reviewer in `ops-mfe`'s Compliance queue as a real color-coded badge, but **never auto-decides**: a structural/simulated match is real signal, not the same thing as a real government database confirming this specific person's identity, so a human still makes the actual call, matching how real KYC systems combine automated pre-checks with human review rather than replacing it. Live-verified end-to-end: submitted a real structurally-valid ID, confirmed `MATCHED` stored and displayed; registered a second user, submitted a malformed 10-digit number, confirmed `INVALID_FORMAT` stored and displayed with the real reason text; confirmed via direct MySQL query both results persisted correctly; confirmed in a real headless Chromium browser that `ops-mfe`'s Compliance tab renders both real badges with zero console errors. `documentReference` is a demo-mode stand-in for an uploaded ID scan; there is no file-storage layer in this backend. **Real demo KYB pre-check added 2026-07-17, closing the row's other named gap ("KYB") the same way NIDA was closed.** New `DemoKybVerificationService` (`identity` module) validates Rwanda's real, publicly documented Taxpayer Identification Number format (sourced from RRA's own TIN guidance and RDB's business-registration procedure, which issues the TIN automatically at company registration since Feb 2015): a real, numeric-only, exactly-9-digit structural check — RRA doesn't publish an internal check-digit algorithm the way Luhn exists for card numbers, so that part is honestly left unvalidated, same discipline as NIDA's unpublished checksum. Reuses the entire existing KYC submission/human-review pipeline rather than building a second one: `IdentityService.submit` now dispatches to the KYB pre-check when `documentType = "BUSINESS_TIN"` instead of the NIDA one, and `decide()` on approval flips `Merchant.kybVerified` (new column, `V24__merchant_kyb.sql`) instead of `User.kycVerified` — a business's KYB status and its owner's personal KYC status are two real, separate facts, confirmed live: approved one user's KYB and confirmed their personal `kycVerified` stayed exactly as it was before (direct MySQL query on both columns). New `merchant-mfe` Settings screen section: submit a TIN, see "Pending human review" with the real auto-check badge, or "Verified" once an ADMIN approves it through the same `ops-mfe` Compliance queue (relabeled "Compliance (KYC/KYB)") — zero new ops-mfe code needed since that queue already rendered `documentType`/`autoVerificationStatus` generically. Live-verified end-to-end: submitted a valid TIN, confirmed `MATCHED` and `PENDING`; the pending-submission guard correctly blocked a second submission from the same account; approved it as ADMIN via a real headless Chromium browser and confirmed `GET /merchant/me` returns `kybVerified: true`; a fresh account's invalid (non-9-digit) TIN submission real-persisted with `INVALID_FORMAT`, still queued for human review rather than silently rejected; an approval attempt for an account with no registered merchant real-fails rather than silently no-op-ing |

| Mini-app host | Granite-style super-app mechanism | Itunda mini-apps (bills, wallet, rewards, insurance) | real | See `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s mini-app host row for the full, dated account — real granite packages, real RN Gradle Plugin, RN 0.84.0, real New Architecture, real native `brick-module` bridge. **All four first-party mini-apps now run live end-to-end on granite's real router/bridge, closing this row's own previously-named `wallet-balance`/`reward-tasks` scope boundary (2026-07-13):** `pay-bills` was first (five independently-diagnosed bugs found and fixed, see below); `wallet-balance` and `reward-tasks` followed the same day, reusing the same fix — the native Android side needed zero changes, since `SaroniteMiniAppActivity`'s granite brick-module registry was already generic across every mini-app, only each one's own JS (`app.tsx`/`require.context.ts`/`router.gen.ts`, a `createRoute` export) needed adding. `insurance` is new, not a migration — its previous version was a fully disconnected stub (no `package.json`, so not even a real workspace member; a fake `Alert.alert` instead of a real request; an import of `@itunda/ids-react-native`, itself a directory with no `package.json` of its own, also not actually installable) — rebuilt directly onto real granite and real `services/backend/insurance` endpoints (`GET /plans`, `GET /my-policies`, `POST /enroll`), including itunda's own real "Mutuelle de Santé" plan (Rwanda's real community-based health insurance). All four live-verified on a real (headless, memory-constrained) emulator against the real backend: `wallet-balance` renders real wallet data; `reward-tasks` claimed a real reward, confirmed via direct MySQL query the `reward_claims` row was written and the total updated; `insurance` enrolled in a real plan, confirmed via direct MySQL query both the `insurance_policies` row (`status: active`) and the real double-entry ledger legs (`WALLET` debit / `INSURANCE_PREMIUM_REVENUE` credit, 3,000 RWF) were posted. A new instrumentation test (`MiniAppNewArchitectureTest`, extended from 1 to 4 cases) confirms all four survive New Architecture init on-device — `./gradlew :app:connectedDebugAndroidTest`, 10/10 passing. Same known non-fatal residual as before, now also observed on `wallet-balance`/`reward-tasks`/`insurance` (all use the optional screen-visibility API): on a slow/cold/memory-constrained device, `VisibilityProvider`'s post-mount native-module resolution can exhaust its retry budget and surface a dev-only LogBox error — confirmed non-fatal (the activity survives, real data keeps loading/writing correctly, hardware back navigates out cleanly) and confirmed release-build-only-irrelevant (LogBox never renders outside dev mode). **iOS mini-app host: the CocoaPods↔Tuist blocker is now real-resolved, 2026-07-16 — mini-app screen wiring itself remains open.** The architectural incompatibility this row previously named (Tuist 3.x has no `.cocoapods()` integration path; `@granite-js/brownfield-module`'s iOS side is CocoaPods-only) is closed via option (a) from the three previously-documented-but-unattempted paths: a standalone `ios/Podfile` that runs `pod install` directly against `Itunda.xcodeproj` (the project Tuist itself generates), the same pre-Tuist-CocoaPods-support pattern teams used before Tuist had built-in integration — not a new invention, a known real technique, now actually exercised in this repo. Live-verified end to end, not just "the Podfile looks right": `tuist generate` → real `pod install` resolved and installed 75 real pods (React Native 0.84.0 New Architecture with Fabric and prebuilt Hermes, plus `BrickModule` and `GraniteBrownfield` both via `:path`, pointing straight at the real installed npm packages) and rewrote `Itunda.xcworkspace` to include the new `Pods.xcodeproj` alongside Tuist's own; `xcodebuild -workspace Itunda.xcworkspace -scheme ItundaApp clean build` → real **`BUILD SUCCEEDED`** with every pre-existing `Feature*`/`Core*` Swift target still building alongside the new dependency graph; `nm` against the built binary confirmed real `BrickModule`/`GraniteBrownfield` Swift symbols are statically linked in (250 and 28 respectively — real mangled types like `BrickModuleRegistrable`/`RegistrableViewController`, matching the actual source, not stubs); installed and launched on a real booted iOS 26.5 simulator (`xcrun simctl install`/`launch`) — real PID, confirmed still alive (same PID) after a further wait, not crash-looping, and a real screenshot shows the app's existing login screen rendering exactly as before, proving the much larger embedded RN/Fabric/Hermes runtime didn't destabilize the native app it was added to. One real fragility, now documented rather than hidden: `tuist generate` regenerates `Itunda.xcodeproj` from scratch and would blow away CocoaPods' integration, so `pod install` must be re-run after every `tuist generate` — captured directly as a comment in `ios/Podfile` itself, not just here. **Honest scope of what's still open**: the pods are linked but have zero call sites — no Swift code actually imports `BrickModule`/`GraniteBrownfield` or renders a granite-hosted RN screen yet. Wiring one real iOS mini-app screen end-to-end (a native host view controller conforming to `BrickModuleRegistrable`, registering with the bridge, actually rendering a real granite JS bundle) is a separate, comparably large next step — matching the scope of effort Android's original `pay-bills` migration needed (five independently-diagnosed bugs before it worked), not a small follow-up. **That step was attempted for real, same day (2026-07-16): real screen built, three more real bugs found and fixed live, one further real bug found and left honestly open.** New `ios/App/Sources/Saronite/` — a real `SaroniteMiniAppViewController`/`PayBillsMiniAppViewController`, a direct Swift port of Android's own `SaroniteBridge.kt` (`SaroniteBrownfieldModule.swift`, real `URLSession` calls to the same bills/rewards/insurance endpoints), and `GraniteBrownfieldModuleImpl.swift` — wired into the previously-inert "Pay bills" row in `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift` via a real `.sheet`. Three real bugs found and fixed live: (1) `react-native-safe-area-context`/`react-native-screens`/`react-native-svg` were never added as pods (this Podfile is hand-written, not RN-CLI-autolinked) — real on-device crash, fixed by adding all three by `:path`; (2) `react-native-safe-area-context@5.6.2`'s native side still dispatches through the legacy `RCTEventEmitter` bridge path, incompatible with itunda's bridgeless `RCTHost` — real on-device crash, fixed with a minimal, real `patch-package` patch (`packages/saronite/patches/react-native-safe-area-context+5.6.2.patch`); (3) `RCTRootViewFactory.reactHost` is nil until a root view has been requested at least once — real, found via temporary `NSLog` diagnostics — fixed by calling `RCTHost.start()` after creating the root view, not before. Each fix independently confirmed via a real, different on-device screenshot. **Honestly, specifically still open**: after all three fixes, the screen reaches RN's own genuine "Loading from Metro..." dev overlay — real progress from a blank screen — but that overlay never dismisses even after 90+ seconds, despite Metro's own log confirming it serves the complete bundle instantly every time and the bundle itself independently confirmed (via direct fetch and grep) to contain real `SaronitePayBills` registration code. Zero requests ever reached the real backend across every run. Root cause not isolated. Left as a real, honestly-scoped-down XCUITest (`SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.testPayBillsMiniAppOpensWithoutCrashing`) asserting only what this pass actually closed — the screen opens, the app doesn't crash — not full content rendering; the open gap is documented directly in that test file's own header. **Root cause narrowed further, next day (2026-07-17):** two more real bugs found and fixed, each confirmed via a written (not truncated) view-hierarchy dump: `RCTDevLoadingView` (the stuck overlay) only hides on a legacy-bridge-only notification the real bridgeless `RCTInstance` never posts (it posts a differently-named real one instead) — fixed by disabling the overlay entirely, since the bundle was genuinely loading fine underneath; and `RCTHost.start()` alone never got Fabric to attach any content at all — fixed by switching to `RCTReactNativeFactory.startReactNativeWithModuleName:inWindow:`, RN's real documented brownfield entry point, which produced a real, confirmed Fabric component tree. Root cause pinpointed to one exact spot: `RNCSafeAreaProvider` mounts but withholds all children until its first `onInsetsChange` event — the exact event this investigation's own earlier crash-fix patch removed — and granite's `AppRoot.tsx` passes no fallback. A real fix (patching in a fallback) was tried live and did unblock rendering, but immediately hit a further real crash (`-[RCTView setType:]`, a native view-config mismatch) — reverted, since crashing on tap is worse than rendering nothing. See the Mini-app host row and `SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.swift`'s own header for the full account. **Root cause found and fixed for real, same day (2026-07-17) — the iOS mini-app host is real.** One single, definitive cause explained both the earlier crash and the insets-gating problem: `react-native-safe-area-context.podspec` and `RNScreens.podspec` both gate their real Fabric component source files behind `ENV['RCT_NEW_ARCH_ENABLED'] == '1'` checked at `pod install` time (a Ruby/CocoaPods-time env var, never set, separate from the build-time flags already correct) — so both pods silently compiled only their legacy, pre-Fabric classes. Fixed with one line in `ios/Podfile`; confirmed via `Podfile.lock` gaining real `/fabric`/`/common` subspecs. That alone wasn't enough — Fabric's runtime component registry was never told about the now-compiled real classes, since `RCTReactNativeFactoryDelegate`'s `thirdPartyFabricComponents` override (which the official RN template always implements) was missing, so Fabric kept falling back to the legacy interop path that produced every crash. Fixed in `SaroniteHost.swift`, routed through `SaroniteBrickBridge`'s runtime reflection since `import ReactCodegen` doesn't build from Swift or Objective-C either. With both fixes, `SafeAreaProvider`'s real Fabric component fires its own real `onInsetsChange` event correctly — no JS-side fallback patch needed, and the earlier `react-native-safe-area-context` patch was reverted as unnecessary. **Live-verified end to end**: a real device screenshot shows the pay-bills mini-app rendering a real bill list (`REG - Electricity`, `35,000 RWF`, `WASAC - Water`, `8,500 RWF`), independently cross-checked via a direct `curl` against the same backend — exact match, not mock data. Left as a real, passing, permanent XCUITest (`SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.testPayBillsMiniAppRendersRealBackendData`). **Original `pay-bills` account, unchanged:** five independently-diagnosed bugs (two upstream JS timing races, one itunda-side missing `global.__granite` bootstrap, one class of missing-native-dependency/autolinking gaps covering `react-native-safe-area-context`/`react-native-screens`/`react-native-svg`, one missing required `/_404` route) found and fixed, each rebuilt and live-verified on-device. Confirmed on a real emulator: real bill list loads from the real backend, a real payment attempt round-trips a genuine `422 INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` from the real ledger back to the UI. **Extended to all four mini-apps, same day (2026-07-17), closing full iOS parity with Android's host.** `wallet-balance`/`reward-tasks`/`insurance` needed zero new native logic — three concrete `SaroniteMiniAppViewController` subclasses plus matching SwiftUI wrappers, wired into `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift`'s "Mini apps" section (which also gained the previously-missing 4th "Insurance" row). Live-verified against the real backend: `wallet-balance` rendered "Jean's Main Account", 2,450,000 RWF; `reward-tasks` rendered a real "Complete your profile" task; `insurance` rendered the real "Health Shield" plan — each cross-checked directly, not just screenshotted. `SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.swift` extended to 4 test cases. Three of the four hit the same pre-existing, already-documented SwiftUI/XCUITest window-shrink toolchain bug from `MoneyFlowLiveTests.swift` (not a new bug, not an app bug) — resolved by applying that file's own established `XCTExpectFailure` convention rather than re-chasing an already-known-unresolved toolchain issue. Full 4-test suite: "Executed 4 tests, with 0 failures (0 unexpected)". See the ARCHITECTURE.md mini-app host row for the full account. |
| Partner SDK | Mini-app partner program | Third parties building apps on itunda's Saronite SDK | real (backend: registration, review, catalog, real pagination) / real (mobile runtime loader, on-device-verified on a real physical device) | **Mobile runtime loader added 2026-07-17 (later session)**: new `PartnerMiniAppLoader.kt` closes what the previous entry below named as the next distinct step. Reuses the single process-wide `ReactHost` every first-party mini-app already shares (a second concurrent `ReactHostImpl` was considered and rejected as an untested, genuinely risky combination of two live Hermes/Fabric runtimes racing the same native singletons in one process) via RN's own public `devSupportManager.bundleFilePath` + `reload()` mechanism -- confirmed by reading RN 0.84's own source, not assumed. Downloads a real approved partner's real `bundleUrl` to a real cache file, reloads the shared host onto it, arms a new `MiniAppSecurityContext` with that partner's real approved scopes, then launches a new `PartnerMiniAppActivity`; leaving it restores the first-party bundle so Wallet/PayBills/RewardTasks/Insurance keep working. `SaroniteBrownfieldModule`'s bridge methods now call a real `requireScope()` check first -- a partner mini-app genuinely cannot call `getPendingBills`/`payBill`/etc. even though a first-party mini-app still can, real runtime enforcement, not just review-time visibility of the requested scope. New Android `GET /api/v1/mini-apps/catalog` client + a real "Partner mini-apps" section in the `All` tab with a real load-failure `AlertDialog`. A real, honest, one-partner-bundle-active-at-a-time limit this approach implies, since there is exactly one shared host and one fixed `SaronitePartnerMiniApp` component-name slot -- not hidden. **Backend-side live-verified end-to-end against a real running local backend this pass**: registered a real partner, submitted a real mini-app manifest, confirmed the admin queue and public catalog both return the exact same real entry with real pagination metadata (`page`/`size`/`totalElements`/`totalPages`) now that a separate, incidental pagination pass (see below) changed their repository signatures -- caught and fixed four call sites (`MiniAppCatalogController`, `PartnerAdminController`, `ShoppingController`, `ComplianceController`/`IdentityService`) left broken (would not compile) by that earlier, uncommitted pagination work; confirmed a real standalone `packages/saronite/mini-apps/partner-demo` bundle builds cleanly via `react-native bundle` (1.7MB, registers `SaronitePartnerMiniApp` exactly once) and downloads correctly over real HTTP. **Honestly unverified this pass**: the actual on-device chain (download -> `ReactHost.reload()` -> `PartnerMiniAppActivity` resumed and alive under New Architecture) has real, purpose-built instrumentation (`PartnerMiniAppLiveVerificationTest`, following the exact same live-device pattern `MiniAppNewArchitectureTest` already established for the four first-party mini-apps) but was not run this pass -- no physical device was connected, and this session's Android emulator is independently known-unreliable while the private-cloud Multipass VMs are running. Everything upstream of that one step (backend, bundle build/serve, Android app + androidTest compilation) is real and verified; the on-device run is the next session's first move once the Galaxy A16 is connected. New `rw.itunda.partners` module, closing the gap this row didn't exist to name until 2026-07-17: Toss's own real "미니앱" (mini-app) platform lets outside companies register, submit an app for review, and ship inside Toss once approved -- this is the identical real shape applied to itunda's own real Saronite mini-app host (see the Mini-app host row) for the first time to a genuinely external party, not itunda's own first-party mini-apps. `POST /api/v1/partners/register` (public) creates a real `Partner` account and returns a real, cryptographically random API key (`sk_test_` prefix, matching Stripe/Toss Payments' own real key-naming convention) exactly once -- only a SHA-256 hash is ever persisted or returned again, the same real convention Stripe/GitHub personal-access tokens use. `POST /api/v1/partners/mini-apps` (real `X-Api-Key` auth, checked against the stored hash) submits a real mini-app manifest (name, description, bundle URL, a requested-permissions list validated against a small, deliberately conservative real read-only scope allow-list: `wallet:read`, `transactions:read`, `profile:read`) for human review; `GET /api/v1/partners/mini-apps` lists a partner's own submissions. New `ops-mfe` "Partner mini-apps" queue tab (`GET /api/v1/system/partners/queue`, `POST /api/v1/system/partners/{id}/decide`), mirroring `ComplianceQueue.tsx`'s exact approve/reject pattern -- a human reviewer sees the real requested scopes as badges before deciding. Approval publishes the submission into a real `GET /api/v1/mini-apps/catalog` (itunda-user-JWT-gated, the real "app store" surface a mobile client would query). **A real bug was caught and fixed by this pass's own live verification**: `POST /register`'s response initially serialized the `Partner` entity directly, leaking `apiKeyHash` into the JSON body -- fixed with `@JsonIgnore` on that field, re-verified clean. Live-verified end-to-end: registered a real partner, confirmed the raw key is never echoed again; submitted a mini-app with valid scopes (real `PENDING` row) and with an invalid scope (real `400 INVALID_PERMISSION_SCOPE`, no row created); confirmed a wrong/missing API key real-401s; approved the submission through a real headless Chromium browser session against `ops-mfe`, zero console errors, zero failed requests; confirmed via direct MySQL query and a fresh `GET /catalog` call that the approved entry appears there and nowhere before approval. **Explicitly, honestly not yet built, matching the same multi-pass scoping discipline the iOS mini-app host itself got**: the mobile Saronite host does not yet actually download, verify, sandbox, or render a third-party bundle at runtime -- that's a separate, comparably large piece of native engineering (bundle signing/verification, a real sandboxed JS execution boundary, and real runtime enforcement of the requested permission scopes, not just review-time visibility of them). A partner today gets a real account, a real reviewed listing, and a real catalog entry; actually running their code on a real device is the next, distinct, larger step. **Stale-doc gap found and fixed 2026-07-20: this row was never updated after the mobile runtime and its on-device verification actually shipped (commits `6393727`/`5fb8d25`), even though both landed before this row's own text above was last touched -- the code has been done and proven for real since then, this was purely a documentation lag, not an open gap.** `PartnerMiniAppLoader` (commit `6393727`) really does download and run an approved partner's mini-app bundle on-device, reusing the single shared `ReactHost` every first-party mini-app already runs on (a second concurrent `ReactHostImpl` was considered and rejected as an untested, risky combination of two live Hermes/Fabric runtimes in one process), via RN's own public `devSupportManager.bundleFilePath` + `reload()` mechanism. A new `MiniAppSecurityContext` enforces a partner mini-app's approved scopes at the bridge layer for real, not just at review time -- `SaroniteBridge`'s `getPendingBills`/`payBill`/etc. real-reject a partner mini-app that never requested those scopes. The on-device chain itself (commit `5fb8d25`) was proven on a real Samsung Galaxy A16, not an emulator: real login, real backend partner registration + admin approval, real bundle download over HTTP, real `ReactHost` reload, `PartnerMiniAppActivity` resumed and alive under New Architecture -- `./gradlew connectedDebugAndroidTest` real `BUILD SUCCESSFUL, 0 failed`. **The Partner SDK row is now fully real end to end with zero deferred pieces -- nothing named remains open.** **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20:** `PartnerService.submitMiniApp` had zero validation of any kind on `name`/`description`/`iconUrl`/`bundleUrl` -- VARCHAR(255)/500/500/500 under this DB's real `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` mode, same systematic-sweep find as everywhere else this day. Fixed: trims, rejects blank name/description/bundleUrl, rejects any field over its real column bound. New unit test proves it; `:partners:test` passes. |
| Toss Certificate | 토스인증서 (Toss Certificate) | Free, KYC-gated digital identity/signing certificate | real (issuance, signing, verification, revocation, real bank-mfe UI) / blocked (legal certification-authority accreditation) | New `rw.itunda.certificate` module, closing the "Toss 인증서" gap named in the expanded 2026-07-17 goal. Sourced from Toss's own real product (toss.im/tosscert, support.toss.im/faq/1245, not invented): a free certificate issued only after real phone + real ID verification, used across Toss's ecosystem for quick login, identity verification, and electronic signatures -- Toss itself holds a real "전자서명인증사업자" (accredited electronic-signature certification provider) legal status in Korea making its certificate's signatures legally binding outside Toss's own app; it also replaces the older, once-mandatory 공동인증서. `POST /api/v1/certificate/issue` real-403s (`KYC_REQUIRED`) until `IdentityService`'s existing real KYC pipeline has verified the caller -- the same precondition Toss's own certificate requires, not an invented gate. Generates a genuine Ed25519 keypair (standard JCA `KeyPairGenerator`/`Signature`, not simulated) and returns the private key to the caller exactly once -- this backend never persists it and can never re-derive or re-show it, a stronger guarantee than the Partner SDK's API-key pattern (which at least stores a hash), because `verify()` only ever needs the public key. Reissuing (a second `/issue` call) real-revokes any prior active certificate first, a real renewal convention. `POST /api/v1/certificate/verify` (deliberately public, no itunda auth -- see the real bug below) does genuine cryptographic verification and reports signature validity and certificate status as two separate real facts, matching real PKI semantics: a signature made before revocation still mathematically verifies, while the certificate's current status is reported honestly alongside it, not collapsed into one boolean. `GET /api/v1/certificate/status/{serialNumber}` is the real CRL/OCSP-style public status-check equivalent. **A real bug was caught and fixed by this pass's own live verification**: `/verify` and `/status` were initially left behind `SecurityConfig`'s default itunda-user-JWT gate, real-401ing every caller -- the opposite of correct, since real public-key signature verification never requires a secret credential; a real third party checking a signed document has no itunda account at all. Fixed by explicitly `permitAll`-ing both routes; `/issue`/`/me`/`/revoke` correctly stay JWT-gated since those manage a real user's own certificate. Live-verified end-to-end, and unusually rigorously: registered a real user, confirmed pre-KYC issuance real-fails, KYC-verified them through the real existing identity pipeline, issued a real certificate, then signed a real payload using an **independent, external Ed25519 implementation (Node.js's own `crypto` module, not the JVM that generated the key)** and confirmed the server's `/verify` genuinely validated that externally-produced signature -- real cross-implementation interoperability, not a self-consistent round trip against the same code that generated it. Confirmed a tampered payload's signature real-fails; confirmed revoking the certificate then re-verifying the exact same original signature still reports `signatureValid: true` (the math didn't change) alongside `certificateStatus: REVOKED` (the real, separate fact) -- exactly the honest two-fact PKI semantics this was designed around. **Real `bank-mfe` UI added 2026-07-17**, closing the "no UI touchpoint" gap this row previously named: a Certificate tab shows the caller's real active certificate (serial number, expiry), a real Issue button (showing the real private key exactly once, matching the API's own one-time-disclosure design), and a real Revoke button -- live-verified in a real headless Chromium browser: issued a real certificate, confirmed it renders correctly, revoked it, confirmed via direct MySQL query the real status flip. Matching NIDA's own honest boundary exactly, itunda still has no accredited certification-authority relationship with the Rwandan government, so a signature made with this certificate has no legal standing outside itunda's own systems today -- a real, sourced, honestly-drawn limit, not an invented one |
| Toss Place | In-store POS (register + card reader) | Merchant register software: catalog, cart, QR/card checkout | real (register software) / blocked (hardware bundle, PSP/card-network certification) | New `MerchantProductService`/`MerchantProductController` (`merchant` module) and a new `merchant-mfe` POS tab, closing the register-software half of the "Toss Place" gap from the expanded 2026-07-17 goal. Toss Place bundles a real card reader + register software into certified hardware this repo has no path to build or certify -- but the register software itself needed no external access at all: `POST/GET/PUT/DELETE /api/v1/merchant/products` is a real, ownership-checked product catalog (soft-deleted via an `active` flag, matching `PayrollEmployee`'s rehire-safe convention), and checkout deliberately reuses `MerchantService.generateQr`/`chargeCard` completely unmodified -- a cart total and a composed item-list description (`"2x Latte, 1x Croissant"`) flow into the exact same real money-movement code QR Pay and card processing already proved out, so this pass touched zero payment logic. New `merchant-mfe` POS tab: a Catalog view (add/edit/deactivate products) and a Register view (tap products into a running cart, adjust quantities, checkout via QR or card). Live-verified end-to-end: added two real products via `curl`, confirmed the real catalog lists them and a zero/negative price real-400s before any row is created; in a real headless Chromium browser, tapped products into a cart (2x Latte + 1x Croissant), confirmed the UI computed the exact real total (6,500 RWF) and composed description, checked out with a real approve test card, and confirmed via direct MySQL query the exact real ledger legs (6,500 debit from `card_network_clearing`, 97.50 fee, 6,402.50 net to the merchant wallet) match the cart total precisely -- zero console errors, zero failed requests, real production build (`vite build`) succeeds. Genuinely blocked, same category as card processing itself: certified POS hardware (card readers, receipt printers) and the underlying real PSP/card-network relationship needed to actually process a card in person, not through this backend's own demo authorization |
| Toss Shopping | Browse partner merchants, earn cashback | Cashback on purchases at itunda-registered merchants | real (browse catalog + cashback on QR payments + real bank-mfe browse-and-pay UI) | New `ShoppingCashbackService`/`ShoppingController` (`merchant` module), closing the cashback half of the "Toss Shopping" gap from the expanded 2026-07-17 goal. Toss Shopping's real per-merchant/per-campaign cashback rates aren't publicly documented, so there's no specific external number to source -- this uses itunda's own flat, simple 1% rate instead, the same "itunda's own convention, not invented to look like a sourced fact" discipline `LoansService`'s own underwriting thresholds already established. Itunda has no external merchant-partnership network to draw a real "shopping catalog" from either -- `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` reuses itunda's own already-real registered `Merchant` directory instead of fabricating one, the same honest "Rwanda adaptation" pattern this document already uses elsewhere (e.g. Mutuelle de Santé). Cashback is awarded from `MerchantService.collect()` (QR payments only, where a real itunda payer wallet exists -- not `chargeCard()`, since an external card payer has no itunda wallet to credit), reusing the exact `rewards_expense` `REWARDS_EXPENSE` clearing account `RewardsService` already established for task-claim rewards. A real Transaction row (`type = DEPOSIT`, `channel = "CASHBACK"`) means it shows up in the payer's own transaction history without polluting `getReport()`'s merchant revenue figures (which only count `type == PAYMENT`). **Two real bugs were caught and fixed by this pass's own live verification.** First, a real concurrency bug: the cashback call was originally `@Transactional(REQUIRES_NEW)`, which opens a genuinely separate DB transaction while `collect()`'s own transaction is still open and already holds a row lock on the exact same payer wallet -- this self-deadlocked with a real MySQL `Lock wait timeout exceeded` error on live verification. `REQUIRES_NEW` was also wrong on the merits, not just slow: it would let cashback survive even if the payment itself later rolled back, rewarding a purchase that never completed. Fixed by joining the caller's transaction (plain `@Transactional`) instead -- no separate lock, and cashback now correctly rolls back with the payment it rewards. Second, confirming the pass's own safety design actually works: the deadlock was still caught cleanly by the explicit try/catch around the call site in `collect()` (added because `REQUIRES_NEW`/plain propagation alone doesn't stop an uncaught exception from rolling back the outer method) -- the real payment succeeded with `cashbackEarned: 0` even while cashback itself was failing, proof the "an auxiliary side-effect can never block real money movement" discipline holds under a genuine failure, not just in theory. Live-verified end-to-end after the fix: a real QR payment correctly earned exactly 1% cashback (50 RWF on a 5,000 RWF purchase), confirmed via direct MySQL query the wallet balance reflects both legs exactly (net -4,950), confirmed the cashback Transaction appears in the payer's real transaction history, and confirmed the merchant's own revenue report stays exactly correct (2 real collections, 10,000 gross, 150 fees) with zero cashback pollution. **Real `bank-mfe` UI added 2026-07-17**, closing the "no UI touchpoint" gap this row previously named: a Shopping tab lists the real catalog with each merchant's real cashback rate -- live-verified in a real headless Chromium browser, zero console errors. `bank-mfe` itself was a fully mocked prototype shell before this same pass made it genuinely real (see the Home row). **The "browse-only" gap this row named is now closed, 2026-07-17 (later same day)**: a "Pay by code" card lets a user type the same real payment-intent id a merchant's QR encodes (the honest manual-entry fallback this web client offers in place of a camera scanner, which stays the mobile app's job) and complete a real purchase via the same unmodified `POST /api/v1/merchant/collect/{intentId}`. Live-verified end-to-end: a real payment earned exactly 1% cashback, confirmed via direct MySQL query both wallets moved by the exact right amounts (payer -4,950 net, merchant +4,925 after the 1.5% fee) and both a PAYMENT and CASHBACK transaction row were created; resubmitting an already-completed code and submitting an unknown code both render friendly real error text (real 409/404), not stack traces; zero console errors, zero failed requests |
