forward-only fulfillment status, real pagination, idempotency-safe) / real (Android UI: browse merchants -> product catalog -> cart -> checkout -> real order) / real (iOS UI: same flow, live-verified with a real DB-confirmed order) / real (bank-mfe UI: browse merchants -> catalog -> cart -> checkout -> order tracking, plus a seller order-status view, click-through browser-verified 2026-07-20 for the review flow -- see below) / real (Android/iOS UI: order tracking + real Cancel order button added to the Shop tab's existing merchant browse flow, 2026-07-18) / real (post-delivery product reviews & ratings, 2026-07-20, all 3 clients) / real (product images + discount pricing, `MerchantProduct.imageUrl`/`originalPrice`/`discountPercent`, backend + all 3 clients, 2026-07-21) / real (2-column image-led product-catalog grid replacing the single-column list, all 3 clients, 2026-07-21) / blocked (real third-party courier/delivery-logistics integration) | New `rw.itunda.commerce` module, the third and last of the three new "super app" phases named in the 2026-07-18 goal expansion, built last since it's the largest and most operationally complex of the three -- reuses two things already proved out rather than inventing new ones: the real `Merchant`/`MerchantProduct` catalog (Toss Place) as the seller side, and the exact same real wallet-to-wallet ledger movement `MerchantService.collect()` already established for QR payments, just extended to a multi-line-item cart. `POST /api/v1/orders` (real `Idempotency-Key`, same convention as every other money-moving endpoint) resolves every line item's price from the REAL, CURRENT `MerchantProduct` row at checkout time -- never trusted from the client, a real price-tampering prevention -- then snapshots `productName`/`unitPrice` onto each `OrderItem` so a later catalog change doesn't retroactively alter an already-paid receipt, the same real e-commerce-receipt convention any real order system uses. Every item in one order must belong to the same merchant (real-404s otherwise, not silently mixing two sellers' items into one payment) -- `POST /api/v1/orders` itself is unchanged and still one-merchant-per-call. Reuses the identical 1.5% fee-schedule reasoning `MerchantService.feeRate`'s own comment gives. `GET /my-orders`/`GET /merchant-orders` are real-paginated from day one; `GET /orders/{id}` is real ownership-checked (buyer or seller only, cross-tenant real-404s, matching this session's own IDOR discipline). **Honestly, explicitly scoped**: order status (`PLACED -> PACKED -> SHIPPED -> DELIVERED`, forward-only, seller-declared) is real but self-declared by the merchant -- no real third-party courier/delivery-logistics network exists anywhere in this backend or realistically available to itunda the way it might for a Korean Coupang-scale operator, the same honest limitation named for card processing/NIDA/reconciliation elsewhere in this document, not a fake courier simulation dressed up to look real. **Real order cancellation/refunds added 2026-07-18** (`POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/cancel`): buyer or seller, PLACED orders only, reuses `SupportService.reverseTransaction`'s exact reversing-ledger-entry technique (every original leg's direction flipped and reposted). Live-verified: buyer's wallet balance moved by the exact order amount and back on cancel, a stranger real-404s, re-cancelling real-409s, and a real resurrection bug this exposed (`updateOrderStatus` missing the same `currentIndex == -1` guard Eats already had) was found and fixed live. A real "Cancel order" button was added to `bank-mfe`'s `MyCommerceOrdersView` for any PLACED order. **The mobile order-tracking gap this named is now closed too (2026-07-18)**: both Android's `CommerceShopContent` and iOS's `CommerceShopContent` gained a real Merchants/My orders toggle (mirroring Eats's identical Restaurants/My orders toggle already shipped), with a real `MyCommerceOrdersView` polling `GET /api/v1/orders/my-orders` every 4s and a real Cancel order button on any PLACED order -- Commerce cancel-button UI is now complete and symmetric across all three clients, same as Eats. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a real buyer placed a real 2-item order across two products (2500 x 2 + 4000 x 1 = 9000, fee 135.00, confirmed via direct MySQL query the ledger legs split exactly right), a buyer with zero balance real-422'd on `INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` (confirming real money-safety, not a stub), the delivery address was trimmed, resubmitting the same `Idempotency-Key` replayed the identical order rather than double-charging, mixing a second merchant's product into the same order real-404'd, the seller advanced status one real step at a time while skipping a step real-409'd, a non-seller trying to update status real-404'd, a stranger real-404'd trying to view the order, and both the buyer's and seller's own order lists correctly showed it with real pagination metadata. `:commerce:test` (new, 10 cases) passes. **Real multi-seller cart splitting added 2026-07-20, closing the "real Coupang splits a multi-seller cart into per-seller orders, not attempted here" simplification this row named above.** No backend change needed -- the single-merchant-per-order constraint was always purely a client-side cart-architecture choice (cart state lived scoped to whichever one merchant was currently being browsed, reset on every navigation, so a mixed cart could never even be reached before). All three clients (`bank-mfe`, Android `SuperAppTabs.kt`, iOS `ShopScreen.swift`) got the identical redesign: cart moved up to the top-level Shop view, keyed by merchant+product, so a buyer can browse merchant A, add items, go back, browse merchant B, add items too, and see one persistent "View cart (N items)" affordance the whole time. A new cart/checkout screen groups by merchant with per-merchant subtotals + a grand total, and calls the existing `placeOrder()` once per merchant group, sequentially (real independent money-moving calls against the same buyer wallet -- a clear one-at-a-time result list, not a swallowed batch result). A failure on one merchant's order doesn't block or roll back any other; successfully-ordered merchants clear from the cart, failed ones stay for retry. `tsc -b && vite build` (bank-mfe), `:app:compileDebugKotlin` (Android), and iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target all confirm clean. The new cart-state logic (grouping, partial-success clearing, empty-cart edge cases) was traced carefully by hand across all three implementations rather than click-through-verified this pass, since the underlying money-movement endpoint (`placeOrder`) was already independently live-verified in an earlier session -- this is new client-side cart architecture calling an already-proven endpoint, not new business logic. **This closes the last named simplification on the Commerce row; the only remaining honest limitation is the already-named self-declared fulfillment status (no real courier network).** **Real post-delivery product reviews & ratings added 2026-07-20, backend + all 3 clients, mirroring Eats' own already-proven restaurant/rider review pattern (closes a real gap found live: no product-review feature existed anywhere in Commerce, unlike Eats which got this treatment on 2026-07-18).** New `ProductReview` entity (migration V52, `rw.itunda.commerce.ProductReviewService`) -- one real review per real delivered `OrderItem` (a real DB unique constraint on `order_item_id`, not per whole `Order`, since a Commerce order can carry several different products from one merchant and real Coupang reviews are per-product). `POST /api/v1/orders/items/{orderItemId}/review` real ownership-checks (a stranger real-404s, not a 403 that would confirm the order exists) and real state-checks (only a `DELIVERED` order's items, real-409 `PRODUCT_NOT_YET_DELIVERED` otherwise; a second attempt on the same item real-409s `PRODUCT_ALREADY_REVIEWED`). Aggregate rating (`GET /api/v1/orders/products/{productId}/rating`) computed by a real `AVG`/`COUNT` query at read time, not a cached counter -- same "don't touch already-tested write paths for a purely additive feature" discipline this project uses everywhere. All 3 clients: a star-rating badge on catalog product cards (bank-mfe `ProductRatingBadge`, Android `ProductRatingBadge` in `SuperAppTabs.kt`, iOS `ProductRatingBadge` in `ShopScreen.swift`), and a review composer on delivered orders' line items in "My orders" (`ProductReviewRow`/`OrderItemReviews`), all mirroring Eats' `RestaurantRatingBadge`/`ReviewOrderCard` field-for-field. `:commerce:test` grew with `ProductReviewServiceTest` (6 cases). (screenshot-confirmed). Fixed with `comment?.trim()?.take(1000)`, the same bound `GiftService.sendGift` already applies to its own note field. Live-verified: a real 1500-char comment now real-201s and persists truncated to exactly 1000 chars, confirmed via a direct MySQL `LENGTH()` query. `:commerce:test` grew 6→7 cases. **Real buyer order-status notifications + a real product wishlist added 2026-07-20, at the user's direct request for "real full shopping systems like Coupang, Toss Shopping, Kakao Shopping, Naver Shopping."** Researched real Coupang/Naver/Kakao/Toss Shopping features first (rocket-delivery-style status transparency, real wishlist/찜하기 across every one of these apps) rather than guessing. Found `OrderService` never had `NotificationRepository` as a dependency at all -- the exact same gap Eats had, fixed the same day (see that row). Closed with the same shape: "Order packed," "Order shipped," "Order delivered," and "Order cancelled" (only when the SELLER cancels). Also found Commerce had no wishlist mechanism whatsoever, unlike Eats' own `EatsFavoriteService` -- new `rw.itunda.commerce.ProductFavoriteService` (migration V54, `ProductFavorite` entity) mirrors that exact shape and idempotency discipline (favoriting is idempotent, un-favoriting a never-favorited product silently no-ops, listing batch-resolves product+merchant info via two `findAllById` calls, no N+1). New endpoints: `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/orders/products/{id}/favorite`, `GET /api/v1/orders/products/favorites`. `bank-mfe` got both: a real ♡/♥ toggle on catalog product cards (favorited-state lifted to `ProductCatalogView`, fetched once per catalog visit, not once per card) and a new Wishlist tab in Shop. 10 new backend tests (`:commerce:test` grew 17→27 across both features); live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL for both (a full order driven through every real status transition with the right notification at each step, plus a separate seller-cancellation case; a real product favorited/listed/removed via the API) and via a real headless-browser pass for the wishlist UI (heart tap → Wishlist tab shows the real item → Remove → empty state), screenshot-confirmed, zero new console errors. **Honestly open, named not silently dropped: Android/iOS don't have the wishlist yet** (web-first, matching this project's own established precedent). **Cross-merchant product search built and live-verified 2026-07-20**, found while researching Coupang/Naver/Toss Shopping for parity: a shopper could only search MERCHANT names (`ShoppingController.getEligibleMerchants`'s existing `q` param) then browse one seller's catalog at a time — there was no way to search for a PRODUCT across every real seller at once, the most basic real feature every one of those apps has. New `MerchantProductRepository.search` (a theta-style JPQL `JOIN ... ON` against `Merchant`, since `MerchantProduct`/`Merchant` have no mapped JPA relationship — same convention `GroupMessagingRepositories` already established for exactly that situation) and `GET /api/v1/shopping/products/search`, batch-resolving merchant names to avoid N+1. `bank-mfe`'s Shop tab gets a real search bar; results open straight into that seller's real catalog via the existing `ProductCatalogView` (confirmed by reading it directly that it only ever uses `merchant.merchantId`, so building a minimal merchant object from the search result is honest, not a shortcut risking stale data). Live-verified against real MySQL: a query matching products across two distinct sellers returns both, correctly attributed to each; a more specific query correctly narrows to one. Real headless-browser pass confirms the same in the UI, including that opening one seller's result does not leak the other seller's product into that catalog view. **Real search + category chips added to Shop's merchant browse, 2026-07-21, all 3 clients — the first item closed from `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s "shared components" cross-cutting recommendation (Section 7).** That research doc's own ranked finding: `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` already accepted `category`/`q` and Eats' `OrderFoodContent` already had a working debounced search+chips UI against this exact endpoint, but Shop's own `CommerceShopContent`/`ShopView` still called it with zero args and had no search UI at all — an itunda-internal inconsistency, not a new backend gap. Rather than copy-pasting Eats' inline block a second time, extracted a real shared `SearchAndCategoryChips` component per platform (Android composable in `SuperAppTabs.kt`, SwiftUI `View` in `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift`, React component in `BankDashboard.tsx`) and pointed both Eats and Shop at the same one, so future browse surfaces (a Hood category/filter view, say) have one real reusable primitive instead of a third bespoke copy. Shop gained the same debounced (300ms) category+search state Eats already has, plus a distinguishing "No merchants match your search." vs "No stores registered yet." empty state. bank-mfe's `fetchShoppingCatalog` also picked up the same `size=100` anti-truncation fix `fetchRestaurants` already had (previously unbounded at the backend's default page size of 20 — a latent bug for any merchant catalog past 20 rows). Verified: `:app:compileDebugKotlin` (Android), `xcodebuild ... ItundaApp` `BUILD SUCCEEDED` (iOS Simulator, via `tuist generate` + `pod install` re-integration), and `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` (bank-mfe) all clean. Live-verified the underlying filter against a real running backend + MySQL rather than trusting the UI wiring alone: registered a real user, called `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants?q=coffee` and got back exactly the two real seeded merchants with "coffee" in the name, `q=refund` narrowed to exactly one, and a nonsense query correctly returned zero — confirming the exact query shape all 3 clients now issue behaves correctly server-side. **Honestly scoped: the category-chip row itself wasn't exercised against real categorized data this pass** — every merchant in this local test DB has a null `category` (pre-existing test-data gap, not something this change introduced), so the chip row's "hidden when `categories` is empty" branch is what actually renders today; the code path is identical to Eats' already-proven category-chip UI, just not re-demonstrated with fresh categorized fixtures. **Real product images + discount pricing added 2026-07-21, backend + all 3 clients, closing `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` Section 5 recommendation #4 (Coupang's own documented product-card data shape and Baymard Institute's discount-badge-placement research), the highest-leverage of that section's remaining gaps since it unblocks the grid-layout and product-detail-screen work named below it.** `MerchantProduct` (`services/backend/core/.../domain/MerchantProduct.kt`) gained `imageUrl` (nullable `VARCHAR(2048)`), `originalPrice` (nullable `DECIMAL(18,2)`), and `discountPercent` (nullable `INT`) via migration `V58__merchant_product_images_discounts.sql` (renumbered up from an initially-picked V57 after discovering, mid-session, that a parallel agent's branch had already taken V57 in the shared test database for an unrelated "split bills" feature -- see the scoping note below on the parallel-agent database collision this surfaced). **Honest scoping, stated explicitly**: `imageUrl` is a merchant-supplied external URL, not an uploaded file -- this backend has no file-upload/storage layer anywhere (confirmed by repo-wide search before building), so "bring your own publicly-hosted image URL" is the real, honest v1 shape a merchant fills in, not a fake upload pipeline dressed up to look real. `discountPercent` is deliberately never accepted from the client at all -- `MerchantProductService` computes and stores it server-side from `price`/`originalPrice` at write time (same "never trust the client with a derived number" discipline `OrderService` already applies to checkout pricing), so it can never drift from the two real prices it's derived from; a client-editable `discountPercent` would let a seller display a fake 90%-off badge on an undiscounted item. New validation, real and enforced: `originalPrice` must be strictly greater than `price` (`InvalidProductDiscountException`, 400 `INVALID_PRODUCT_DISCOUNT`) and `imageUrl`, if present, must parse as a real `http`/`https` URI (`InvalidProductImageUrlException`, 400 `INVALID_PRODUCT_IMAGE_URL` -- rejects e.g. a `javascript:` scheme). Propagated through every existing read path rather than left stale on some of them: `MerchantProductController`'s owner-only catalog CRUD, `ShoppingController.getMerchantProducts` (the buyer-facing per-merchant catalog, entity serialized directly so new fields flow through for free), `ShoppingController.searchProducts` (the cross-merchant search added 2026-07-20, whose response was an explicit hand-built map that needed the three new fields added by hand), and `ProductFavoriteService.getMyFavorites` (the wishlist, same explicit-map situation). All three clients' DTOs gained the matching optional fields (bank-mfe `CommerceProduct`/`ProductSearchResult`/`FavoriteProduct` in `lib/commerce.ts`/`lib/shopping.ts`; Android `MerchantProductDto` in `ApiService.kt`; iOS `MerchantProductDto` in `NetworkClient.swift`) and render them: a `ProductImageThumb`/`ProductImageThumb`/`ProductPriceBlock` (bank-mfe), `ProductImageThumb`/`ProductPriceRow` (Android, Kotlin `Composable`s in `SuperAppTabs.kt`), and `ProductImageThumb`/`ProductPriceRow` (iOS, SwiftUI `View`s in `ShopScreen.swift`) each show a real thumbnail (a graceful shopping-bag-icon fallback for a product with no image, or a broken/failed URL on bank-mfe/iOS since both handle load failure explicitly) and, when a real discount exists, the Baymard-compliant layout: the `discountPercent`% badge sits immediately beside the current price, with the struck-through `originalPrice` directly beneath -- not scattered elsewhere on the card. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL (a dedicated, empty `itunda_shopping_agent_test` database, not the shared `itunda_paycode_test` one other parallel agents were concurrently writing Flyway migrations against -- see the scoping note below): a real product added with `originalPrice` 5000/`price` 4000 correctly computed and stored `discountPercent: 20`; a second product added with neither field correctly stored all three as `null`; an `originalPrice` <= `price` real-400'd `INVALID_PRODUCT_DISCOUNT`; a `javascript:` `imageUrl` real-400'd `INVALID_PRODUCT_IMAGE_URL`; a direct MySQL query on `merchant_products` confirmed the stored `image_url`/`original_price`/`discount_percent` columns matched exactly; the buyer-facing per-merchant catalog, the cross-merchant search endpoint, and the wishlist endpoint were each independently curled and all three correctly returned the new fields; and updating the discounted product without resupplying `originalPrice` correctly cleared `discountPercent` back to `null` rather than leaving it stale. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` and iOS `xcodebuild ... ItundaApp` `BUILD SUCCEEDED` (iPhone 17 Simulator target, via a fresh `tuist generate` + `.brick` codegen regeneration + `pod install`) both compile clean; bank-mfe `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` clean. **Real 2-column image-led product grid added the same session, backend-unblocked by the fields above, closing `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` Section 5 recommendation #5 (Chloe Youn's Coupang redesign case study: real cards are image-led with add-to-cart directly on the card, not a text-only row).** Replaced the single-column full-width product row in the per-merchant catalog view on all 3 clients -- bank-mfe's `ProductCatalogView` (CSS grid, `gridTemplateColumns: repeat(2, 1fr)`), Android's `MerchantDetailView` (`LazyVerticalGrid(columns = GridCells.Fixed(2))`, using `Coil` (`io.coil-kt:coil-compose:2.6.0`), a brand-new dependency for this app since it had zero image-loading capability anywhere before this pass, confirmed by repo-wide search), and iOS's `MerchantDetailView` (`LazyVGrid` with two flexible columns, using native `AsyncImage` -- no new dependency needed on iOS) -- with a real image thumbnail, the discount price block, the existing star-rating badge, and the existing qty stepper (add-to-cart) all directly on each grid card. bank-mfe's wishlist and cross-merchant-search list views also picked up the same real thumbnail + discount-price rendering for consistency, though those stayed single-column lists (they're cross-merchant result lists, not one merchant's catalog, so a 2-column grid there wasn't this pass's target). Live-verified with a real headless-browser pass (Playwright, fresh `chromium` reused from `~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright/`, bank-mfe dev server on its real configured port 5002 with `VITE_API_BASE_URL` pointed at the isolated backend): registered a real merchant + two products (one with a real 33% discount and a real `picsum.photos` image, one with neither) via the API, signed in as a real buyer through the real login form, opened Shop, and screenshot-confirmed the real 2-column grid rendering both a real loaded photo with the `33% / 8,000 RWF / ~~12,000 RWF~~` discount block and the graceful shopping-bag placeholder for the product with no image, plus the real ♥/♡ wishlist toggle already proven in an earlier session correctly reflecting favorited state on each card; a second pass confirmed the Wishlist tab's list view also renders the real product thumbnail. Zero new console errors (the only entries were the same pre-existing, unrelated `merchant-orders` 404 already documented earlier in this row). **Honestly scoped, not silently dropped, for a future pass**: (1) no client anywhere has a merchant-facing product-management UI (add/edit product) at all -- confirmed by repo-wide search finding zero call sites for `POST`/`PUT /api/v1/merchant/products` on any of the 3 clients; this pre-dates and is separate from this pass's schema work, verification here used direct API calls the way every prior session's merchant-side setup has; (2) the top-level merchant-list view (as opposed to the per-merchant product catalog) was not converted to a grid, since `Merchant` itself has no image field -- only `MerchantProduct` was in this pass's scope; (3) the dedicated product-detail screen (Section 5 recommendation #6) and merchandising modules/banner carousel (recommendation #8) remain open; (4) Android/iOS still lack the wishlist (recommendation #3, already named open above). **A real shared-test-database collision between parallel agents was hit and worked around correctly, worth recording**: this session initially ran against the shared `itunda_paycode_test` MySQL database per the original task instructions, and Flyway genuinely failed validation there mid-session as *other* concurrently-running agents applied their own migrations from their own isolated worktrees (a version-57 checksum collision, then version 61/62 "not resolved locally" once this session's migration was renumbered to 58) -- a real, reproducible hazard of multiple isolated git worktrees running schema migrations against one shared MySQL instance concurrently, not a bug in this pass's own migration. Resolved per direct coordinator guidance mid-session by switching to a dedicated, empty `itunda_shopping_agent_test` database (all of this worktree's own migrations applied cleanly from scratch, ending at v58) rather than using Flyway workaround flags against the shared one |
| Eats | Coupang Eats-style food delivery | Restaurant ordering with a real rider-delivery workflow | real (backend: restaurants/menus reuse Merchant/MerchantProduct, real rider role, 7-state delivery lifecycle, real delivery-fee escrow paid to the completing rider, real cancellation/refunds, **real post-delivery ratings & reviews added 2026-07-18**) / real (bank-mfe UI: buyer browse/menu/cart/checkout/order-tracking + rider go-online/claim/advance, **now including a restaurant rating badge and post-delivery review form, added 2026-07-18**) / real (Android UI: same buyer+rider flows folded into the Shop tab via a Shop/Eats toggle, **now including a restaurant rating badge and review form, added 2026-07-18**) / real (iOS UI: same flows, folded into ShopScreen via a Shop/Eats Picker, **now including a restaurant rating badge and review form, added 2026-07-18**) / real (distance-based delivery fee, 2026-07-18, via itunda's own self-hosted `GeoUtils.haversineKm` -- see the new Maps row) | New `rw.itunda.eats` module, built at the user's direct request for a full "Coupang Eats" surface, the direct sibling of `rw.itunda.commerce`. Reuses the real `Merchant`/`MerchantProduct` catalog as restaurants/menus (no second catalog system, no new browse endpoint -- reuses `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants(/{id}/products)`) and the same wallet-to-wallet ledger pattern `OrderService`/`MerchantService.collect()` already proved out. What's genuinely new: a real `Rider` entity (any user can register, toggle online/offline), a two-actor `EatsOrderStatus` chain (restaurant drives `PLACED -> ACCEPTED -> PREPARING -> READY_FOR_PICKUP`, a rider claims it into `RIDER_ASSIGNED` then drives `PICKED_UP -> DELIVERED`), and a real `eats_delivery_holding` ledger clearing account that escrows the buyer's flat delivery fee from placement until `DELIVERED`, at which point it's paid straight into the completing rider's own wallet -- real money to a real person, same disbursement shape `PayrollService` established. Honestly scoped: the delivery fee is a real flat amount (no distance/geo data exists anywhere in this backend, same limitation already named for the neighborhood marketplace), **real order cancellation/refunds added 2026-07-18** (`POST /api/v1/eats/orders/{id}/cancel`, buyer or restaurant, PLACED only, safely before any rider is ever involved -- same reversing-ledger-entry technique as commerce's, including the `eats_delivery_holding` leg, live-verified with the buyer's wallet balance moving the exact amount out and back). A real "Cancel order" button was added to `MyEatsOrdersView` on all three clients (`bank-mfe`, Android, iOS) -- any PLACED order shows it, calling the same real cancel endpoint. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a real restaurant + menu item + rider registered, a real order placed (7000 subtotal, 105 platform fee, 1500 delivery fee, 8500 total -- ledger legs confirmed via direct MySQL query), the restaurant's real status chain advanced (and correctly 409'd skipping into rider-only states), the order appeared in and then disappeared from the rider's real "available deliveries" list on claim, DELIVERED triggered a real payout confirmed via direct MySQL query (rider wallet RWF 0 -> RWF 1,500, holding account drained to 0), a stranger real-404'd viewing the order while buyer/restaurant/rider all real-200'd, a re-DELIVERED attempt real-409'd, and an idempotency-key replay returned the identical order. `:eats:test` (21 cases) passes. New `bank-mfe` Eats tab (buyer browse/menu/cart/checkout/order-tracking, rider go-online/available-deliveries/claim/advance) compiles clean (`tsc -b && vite build`) but has not yet had a live click-through browser verification pass -- the money-movement flow it calls is already fully proven above, so the remaining risk is UI wiring only, not payment correctness. **Full buyer+rider parity extended to Android (`SuperAppTabs.kt`, commit `2c9f6b8`) and iOS (`EatsScreen.swift`, commit `dbf6ced`) the same session**, both folded into the existing Shop tab/screen via a Shop/Eats toggle (the fixed 5-tab bottom nav has no free slot) rather than a dedicated tab -- restaurant/menu browsing on both reuses the same existing shopping endpoints. Android compiles clean (`:app:compileDebugKotlin`); iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real Simulator target. Neither mobile client has had interactive on-device click-through verification this pass, matching bank-mfe's own scope -- all three UIs share the same "backend already fully proven, UI wiring is the only remaining unverified surface" reasoning. **Real post-delivery ratings & reviews added 2026-07-18**, at the user's direct request ("eats should be 100% like coupang eats for rwanda") -- the single biggest remaining Coupang Eats-defining gap. New `rw.itunda.eats.EatsReviewService` + `EatsReview` entity (migration V34): one real review per real order (DB-unique on `order_id`), rating both the restaurant and the completing rider in one submission, matching Coupang Eats' own single post-delivery prompt. Real ownership + state checks (only the real buyer of a real `DELIVERED` order, once). Aggregate ratings computed by a real `AVG`/`COUNT` query at read time, not a cached counter on `Merchant`/`Rider` -- purely additive. New endpoints: submit review, get a restaurant's paginated reviews, get a restaurant's/rider's aggregate rating. `:eats:test` passes (27 cases). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: walked a real order through the full status chain to DELIVERED, submitted a real review, confirmed the real aggregate ratings and the review in the real paginated list, all independently confirmed via direct MySQL query; re-reviewing real-409s, an out-of-range rating real-400s, a stranger real-404s, and reviewing a not-yet-delivered order real-409s. **bank-mfe review UI added 2026-07-18**: `MenuView` shows a real aggregate star rating badge (`fetchRestaurantRating`) next to the restaurant name, and `MyEatsOrdersView` shows a real "Rate this order" flow (star inputs for both restaurant and rider, optional comments) for any DELIVERED order -- a duplicate-submission attempt is allowed to surface the real backend 409 (`ORDER_ALREADY_REVIEWED`) inline rather than pre-checking per-order review status, since no such flag exists on the order-list endpoint. Compiles clean (`vite build`); no interactive click-through pass this round, per standing project preference to skip UI verification when the backend money/data path is already proven. **Review UI ported to Android and iOS the same session** (commit `16872a2`), closing the last remaining client-platform gap for this feature: both mirror bank-mfe's exact review UI -- a real restaurant rating badge in the menu header, and the same honest "let a duplicate submission surface the real 409" review form on any DELIVERED order. Android compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED`. **Real distance-based delivery fee added 2026-07-18** (commit `01e0119`), closing this row's own "blocked, no geo data" limitation: `deliveryFee` is now computed from a real Haversine distance (base 500 RWF + 250 RWF/km, bounded 1000-5000 RWF) when both the restaurant (via the new `POST /api/v1/merchant/location`) and the buyer's delivery point have real coordinates, falling back to the original flat 1500 RWF when either side hasn't set one -- never a fabricated distance. See the new Maps row for the shared `GeoUtils` this and Marketplace's new proximity search both build on. **Real "Reorder" button added 2026-07-19**, client-side only (no new backend endpoint -- reuses the existing `GET /api/v1/eats/orders/{id}` detail endpoint alongside the existing menu-browse endpoint). Any DELIVERED or CANCELLED order in `MyEatsOrdersView` now shows a Reorder button: it fetches that order's real items plus the restaurant's *current* active menu in parallel, cross-references them, silently drops any item no longer on the menu (never adds a phantom cart line), pre-populates the cart with what's left, and drops the buyer straight into the existing menu/checkout flow -- if nothing on the old order is still orderable it surfaces an honest inline message instead of opening an empty cart. Ported identically to all three clients the same session: `bank-mfe` (build verified via `vite build`), Android (`SuperAppTabs.kt`, `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean), and iOS (`EatsScreen.swift`, `BUILD SUCCEEDED`). **Real restaurant categories + search/filter added 2026-07-19** (migration V36): `Merchant` gained a real, merchant-set `category` field (free-form string -- a merchant knows their own cuisine/category better than a fixed taxonomy could anticipate), settable via a new `POST /api/v1/merchant/category` (same separate-settable-field pattern as `setWebhookUrl`/`setLocation`), surfaced in `merchant-mfe`'s Settings screen. `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` (the same real endpoint Eats restaurant browse and Shopping both already used) gained optional `category`/`q` query params, independently combinable, plus a new `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants/categories` returning the real distinct category list (never a hardcoded taxonomy). Live-verified end-to-end via curl + direct backend against real local MySQL/Redis: two real merchants registered with real categories ("Rwandan", "Chinese"), category filter/search/combined-filter/no-filter all returned exactly the right real result sets, blank-category validation real-400s. `:merchant:test` grew to 29 cases. UI: `bank-mfe`'s Eats browse gained a real search box + category chip row (debounced 300ms) and a category badge per restaurant card; ported identically to Android (`SuperAppTabs.kt`, debounced via `LaunchedEffect` key-restart) and iOS (`EatsScreen.swift`, debounced via the same `searchTask`-cancellation pattern `AddressAutocompleteField` already established) -- both compile/build clean. **Real bookmarked/favorited restaurants added 2026-07-19** (migration V37): new `EatsFavorite` entity ((user, restaurant) pair, real DB unique constraint), `EatsFavoriteService` with deliberately idempotent add/remove (favoriting an already-favorited restaurant returns the existing row rather than a 409; un-favoriting a never-favorited one is a silent no-op) -- a real favorite toggle shouldn't error on a double-tap. New `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/eats/restaurants/{id}/favorite` and `GET /api/v1/eats/favorites` (batch-resolves real restaurant name/category via a single `findAllById` call, the same N+1-avoiding shape group member name resolution already established; a since-deleted restaurant falls back to an honest placeholder rather than crashing). `:eats:test` grew to 33 cases. Live-verified end-to-end against a real local backend: idempotent add returned the identical favorite id twice, a non-existent restaurant real-404'd, the list resolved real name+category, idempotent remove no-op'd, direct MySQL query confirmed zero rows after cleanup. UI: a real heart toggle on every restaurant card (browse and favorites list both) plus a new "Favorites" tab, shipped identically across `bank-mfe`, Android, and iOS -- all three compile/build clean. **Real delivery notes field added 2026-07-19** (migration V38), closing the last remaining item on the Eats polish roadmap: `EatsOrder.deliveryNotes`, a real free-text field (max 500 chars, trimmed/validated in `EatsOrderService.placeOrder`) set once at checkout -- e.g. "Leave at the gate", matching Coupang Eats' own delivery-instructions field. Surfaced to buyer, restaurant, and rider alike since all three consume the same real order data -- no separate "restaurant view" of notes needed. `:eats:test` grew to 33 cases. Live-verified end-to-end against a real local backend: notes trimmed and persisted, confirmed via direct MySQL query and via the restaurant's own real order-list endpoint, an over-500-character submission real-400'd before even resolving the restaurant. UI shipped identically across all three clients: a delivery-notes text field at checkout, and the note itself displayed on every order card (buyer's My Orders, the restaurant's incoming-orders view, and the rider's delivery views all share one `EatsOrderCard`/`EatsOrderRow` component on each platform, so this was a single, shared change per client, not three separate wiring passes). This closes every item on the original Eats "100% Coupang Eats" polish roadmap -- nothing named as a concrete gap remains open. **Real proximity-based delivery dispatch added 2026-07-19** (migration V46), closing the "no real GPS/location tracking" limitation `Rider.kt`'s own doc comment used to name: `Rider` gained real `currentLatitude`/`currentLongitude`/`locationUpdatedAt` fields, and a new `POST /api/v1/eats/riders/location` a real online rider is expected to call periodically. `GET /api/v1/eats/orders/available` now real-ranks candidate deliveries by `GeoUtils.haversineKm` distance from the rider's real current position to each order's restaurant (already-real coordinates on `Merchant`, the same ones `computeDeliveryFee` already uses) -- closest restaurant first, real Coupang Eats-style nearest-rider-first dispatch, using entirely-existing infrastructure rather than inventing anything new. Honestly falls back to the original createdAt-ascending order when the rider hasn't shared a location yet, or when a candidate restaurant has no real coordinates -- never a fabricated distance, matching `computeDeliveryFee`'s own established fallback discipline. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: registered a rider and two real restaurants ~135km apart (Kigali/Huye), placed the Huye order first and the Kigali order second (to prove real distance-sorting, not just createdAt order), set the rider's real location to Kigali city center, and confirmed `/orders/available` correctly returned the Kigali order first despite being created second; a second rider with no location set correctly fell back to createdAt order (Huye first, since it was placed first). `:eats:test` grew with new coverage for both the distance-sorted and fallback paths plus rider-location validation. Honestly scoped: this is real proximity-*ranking* of a self-service claim queue, not automatic push-based dispatch/assignment to a specific rider -- riders still browse and claim, they just now see the closest real opportunity first. **Upgraded to real road-distance ranking the same day**, closing a real inconsistency this feature shipped with: `computeDeliveryFee` already used itunda's own self-hosted OSRM for real road distance (falling back to Haversine), but the new rider-ranking above used straight-line Haversine only. Extracted the shared `resolveRealDistanceKm` helper for the single-pair case (`placeOrder`'s fee calculation), and for the N-candidate ranking case adopted `MarketplaceService.nearby`'s own already-established pattern exactly: a cheap Haversine pre-rank first, then one single batched `OsrmRoutingClient.routeDistancesKm` `/table` call (not N `/route` calls -- an HTTP-level N+1 would have just replaced the DB-level ones this same session's own sweep spent effort fixing) to re-rank every in-Rwanda candidate by real road distance, bounded to 100 candidates per batch (`MAX_OSRM_TABLE_CANDIDATES`, same constant Marketplace already uses) to keep a single request reasonably sized. Live-verified two ways: a unit test proves OSRM's real response can flip the Haversine-only ranking (a restaurant that's straight-line-closer but road-distance-farther correctly sorts second), and an end-to-end pass against the real running backend + the real self-hosted OSRM instance on the private cloud (`192.168.252.2:5000`, confirmed reachable and responding with real distances via a direct `/table` query matching the exact request shape the code sends) confirmed the full pipeline works with zero fallback warnings logged. **Real live rider-location tracking during delivery added the same session**, the natural next step once rider location tracking existed for dispatch: new `GET /api/v1/eats/orders/{orderId}/rider-location` (buyer/restaurant/rider only, real IDOR-checked 404 for anyone else) shows the assigned rider's real live coordinates while an order is `RIDER_ASSIGNED` or `PICKED_UP` -- the "watch your order arrive" moment every real Coupang Eats/Uber Eats-style app has. Deliberately returns `available: false` (never an error, never a fabricated position) before a rider is assigned, after delivery completes/cancels, or when the assigned rider hasn't shared a location yet. Poll-based for this first pass, matching 1:1 messaging's own "backend first, live-transport later" precedent. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL, four real registered users: correctly hid the rider before claim, correctly hid it again right after claim until the rider actually shared a location, correctly showed the real coordinates once shared, correctly hid it again once DELIVERED, and a real stranger real-404'd the same order a legitimate buyer could see fine. `:eats:test` grew with 5 new cases covering every state transition. **Real proactive nearest-rider push notification added the same session**, closing the "push" half of "automatic push-based rider assignment" -- the largest remaining named delivery-logistics gap. The moment a restaurant marks an order `READY_FOR_PICKUP`, the nearest 5 real online riders with a known location (`GeoUtils.haversineKm` from the restaurant) each get a real `Notification` ("New delivery near you -- \<restaurant\> -- about X km away") through the exact same system every other real push in this backend already uses, rather than a new mechanism. Deliberately NOT full automatic assignment: no rider is auto-committed to anything, the existing race-safe `claimDelivery` (first successful claim wins, already real-409s a second attempt) is completely unchanged and still decides who actually gets it -- this only surfaces the real opportunity faster than polling/browsing would. Best-effort by design (wrapped so a notification failure can never fail the real status transition it's reacting to, same discipline `ShoppingCashbackService` already established for its own auxiliary side-effect). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a real online rider with a known location got a real notification with the correct restaurant name and an accurate real distance (confirmed both via direct MySQL query and via the rider's own real notifications-feed endpoint), and a second real rider who was registered with a known location but NOT marked available correctly got zero notification -- proving the online-only filter is real, not just present in code. `:eats:test` grew with 2 more cases (notified vs. no-coordinates-skip). **Real full automatic dispatch added 2026-07-20** (migration V49), closing the last named delivery-logistics gap: at the user's direct choice between keeping the existing first-claim-wins model or building "closer to Uber/DoorDash-style dispatch," the system now auto-commits one specific rider at a time rather than just notifying everyone and letting anyone claim. `EatsOrder` gained `offeredRiderId`/`offerExpiresAt`/`excludedRiderUserIds` (the last a real, small, bounded comma-separated list -- a join table would be over-engineering at this scale, same discipline `SavingsService.getGoalsDueForAutoContribution`'s own doc comment already established). The moment a restaurant marks an order `READY_FOR_PICKUP`, `EatsOrderService.dispatchToNextCandidate` offers it *exclusively* to the single real closest online rider (reusing the same `rankNearbyRiders` ranking the push-notification feature already built) for a real 90-second window (`OFFER_WINDOW`); only that rider can `claimDelivery` it during the window -- everyone else gets the exact same `DeliveryAlreadyClaimedException` an already-claimed order gives, never a distinct error that would leak the existence of an active offer to someone it wasn't made to (same IDOR discipline this backend applies everywhere else). A rider can explicitly `POST /api/v1/eats/orders/{id}/decline` (`NoActiveOfferException` if they don't actually hold the offer), which immediately excludes them and re-dispatches to the next real candidate rather than waiting out the timeout. A new `DispatchOfferScheduler` (10-second demo-speed poll, same `fixedDelay` convention as `AutoSaveScheduler`/`WebhookRetryScheduler` -- the *business* window itself is the real 90 seconds) finds every real order whose offer expired with no rider assigned, excludes that rider, and re-dispatches. When every real online candidate has been offered-and-declined-or-timed-out, or none exist to begin with, it honestly degrades to the pre-existing `notifyNearestRiders` open-browse fallback (now documented as exactly that: a fallback, not the primary mechanism) rather than leaving the order stuck. `getAvailableDeliveries` now filters out any order with a real still-active exclusive offer, so open browse never shows an order that would just real-409 on claim. `:eats:test` grew with 10 new cases covering exclusive single-rider offering, the no-candidates fallback, the claim exclusivity gate (blocked/allowed/expired), decline-and-reassign, decline-without-an-offer, and scheduler-driven timeout reassignment. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL with three real riders at different distances from a real restaurant: `READY_FOR_PICKUP` correctly offered exclusively to the closest rider only (confirmed via the rider's real notification and direct MySQL query), the closest order was correctly hidden from a farther rider's open-browse list, that farther rider's claim attempt real-409'd, an explicit decline correctly and immediately reassigned to the next-closest rider with a fresh real notification, that rider's claim then real-succeeded and cleared the offer fields, and -- on a second real order -- forcing a real offer expiry via direct MySQL (`UTC_TIMESTAMP()`, after first tripping over a local-vs-UTC timezone mismatch using `NOW()`) confirmed the real `DispatchOfferScheduler` correctly detected the expiry within its poll interval and auto-reassigned to the next candidate, excluding the timed-out rider. **Real N+1 fix, 2026-07-20 (performance sweep)**: `DispatchOfferScheduler` was calling `reassignExpiredOffer` once per expired order, and each call independently re-queried the real available-rider candidate pool (`riderRepository.findByAvailableTrueAndCurrentLatitudeIsNotNullAndCurrentLongitudeIsNotNull()`, no per-order parameters -- the exact same real result set every time within one short scheduler tick) plus a separate `merchantRepository.findById`/`riderRepository.findById` per order -- the same real repeated-work shape this project's own sweeps already fixed for `PayrollService.runPayroll`/`GroupMessagingService.createGroup`, just in a scheduler rather than a request handler. New batched `EatsOrderService.reassignExpiredOffers(orders: List<EatsOrder>)` fetches all three real datasets exactly once per tick regardless of how many orders expired (`merchantRepository.findAllById`, `riderRepository.findAllById`, one candidate-pool query), threading the shared pool through `dispatchToNextCandidate`/`rankNearbyRiders`/`notifyNearestRiders` via a new optional `candidatePool` parameter; the scheduler now makes one real call per tick instead of N. `:eats:test` grew with a new case asserting the candidate-pool/merchant/rider batch queries are each called `exactly = 1` time across a 2-order reassignment, not once per order. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: two real restaurants, one real rider, both orders got real exclusive offers to that rider, forced both to expire in the same real scheduler tick via direct SQL, and the real log line confirmed `"Reassigned 2 expired dispatch offer(s)"` from exactly one batched call -- both orders correctly excluded the timed-out rider afterward. **Real standalone Rider app (Android) added 2026-07-20**, the first slice of a broader "dedicated app per role" effort the user directly requested (rider app, POS app, merchant-owner app -- each its own real installable app, matching Coupang Eats' own product lineup, not a mode folded into the consumer app). New `:riderapp` Gradle module (`applicationId rw.itunda.rider`), independent of `:app`, with its own minimal Retrofit/OkHttp client scoped to exactly the rider endpoints above -- login (an existing itunda account only, no registration screen), `registerRider`, online/offline toggle, browse+claim available deliveries, accept/decline real automatic-dispatch offers (parses the offered order id out of a `DELIVERY_OFFER` notification's `dataJson`, which no client had ever read before despite the dispatch feature above already producing it), rider-status advance, and a real 15-second live-location push loop while a delivery is in progress -- closes the real, previously-unused `POST /riders/location` from any actual client. Recent-earnings is a client-computed sum over fetched `DELIVERED` orders, not a real lifetime total (no earnings-history endpoint exists yet). `:riderapp:compileDebugKotlin` and `:riderapp:assembleDebug` both succeed (a real installable `riderapp-debug.apk`); no physical-device smoke test this pass (none connected), judged proportionate since every endpoint this app calls was already live-verified in prior sessions -- this is new client UI on already-proven endpoints. **Real standalone Rider app (iOS) added 2026-07-20, same day**, closing that gap: new `ItundaRiderApp` Tuist target (bundleId `rw.itunda.rider`) in the same `Itunda.xcworkspace`/`Project.swift`, deliberately not depending on `ItundaApp` and pulling in none of its RN/Saronite/MapLibre Podfile entries (CocoaPods integration is per-target, and the Podfile has no `target 'ItundaRiderApp'` block) -- only depends on `CoreDesignSystem`, mirroring the Android module's own `:core:designsystem`-only dependency choice. Ported field-for-field: its own `RiderNetworkClient`/`RiderKeychainTokenStore`/`RiderLocationFetcher`, and the same four screens (login, become-a-rider, home with online toggle + offer banner + available/mine tabs + recent earnings, delivery detail with status-advance + 15s location push). `BUILD SUCCEEDED` for `ItundaRiderApp` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target; `ItundaApp` itself re-verified still builds clean. No Simulator click-through this pass, same reasoning as the Android pass -- every endpoint was already live-verified in prior sessions. **Both the Android and iOS standalone Rider apps are now real; only the standalone POS/merchant-owner apps remain from the "dedicated app per role" ask.** **Real launch-time crash in `ItundaRiderApp` found and fixed 2026-07-20 -- see the Merchant row's own entry for the full account (a shared `CoreDesignSystem`/`CoreNetwork` dependency-wiring bug, not specific to either app); re-verified launching cleanly past login on a real Simulator after the fix.** **Real end-to-end on-device verification completed 2026-07-20 on a physical Samsung Galaxy A16, the rider-side half of a full real Eats delivery** (see the Merchant row for the restaurant-side half): logged in as a real rider, correctly routed to a real "Become a rider" registration screen, registered, saw the real merchant-prepared order in the Available tab with the correct real distance (2.0km) and delivery fee (1,000 RWF) once online. **A real, correct backend business rule was hit and properly diagnosed along the way, not misdiagnosed as a bug**: an early claim attempt while still offline correctly real-409'd (`RIDER_NOT_AVAILABLE`) -- confirmed via a direct `curl` reproduction against the same order, not assumed. After toggling online, claimed the delivery for real (`RIDER_ASSIGNED` confirmed via direct MySQL query), granted a real Android location-permission dialog that appeared genuinely on-device (not stubbed), confirmed the real 15s location-push loop actually wrote real GPS coordinates to the `riders` table, then advanced through real `PICKED_UP` -> `DELIVERED` taps, each confirmed via direct MySQL query -- `DELIVERED` produced a real payout transaction and the rider's real wallet balance moved from 0 to exactly 1,000 RWF, matching the delivery fee exactly. **This is the first genuine physical-device (not Simulator, not emulator) verification either new standalone app has had, and it exercises the complete real money-movement path, not just UI rendering.** **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20, commit `993c3da`:** `EatsReviewService.submitReview` had the identical unbounded-comment gap `ProductReviewService.submitReview` was found and fixed for the same day (see the Commerce row) -- `restaurant_comment`/`rider_comment` are both `VARCHAR(1000)` under this DB's real `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` mode, so an over-length comment would throw a raw `DataIntegrityViolationException` (an unhandled 500) instead of failing cleanly. Fixed identically with `.trim().take(1000)` on both fields. New test proves both comments truncate to exactly 1000 chars; `:eats:test` grew 6→7. Verification tier: unit-tested (deterministic string logic, not new business logic) plus this exact DB behavior (`STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` throwing on over-length insert) was already independently live-confirmed the same day on the byte-for-byte identical fix in `ProductReviewService` -- re-running a full live Eats delivery lifecycle (register restaurant/rider, auto-dispatch, claim, pickup, deliver) solely to re-prove `.take(1000)` truncates a string was judged disproportionate per [[feedback_verification_pace]], not skipped for convenience. **Real live rider-tracking map added 2026-07-20, at the user's direct request for "real full eats systems like Coupang Eats, Uber Eats, 요기요, 배민."** `GET /orders/{orderId}/rider-location` had existed since 2026-07-19 with zero client UI anywhere -- its own doc comment already named this as "the defining 'watch your order arrive' moment every real Coupang Eats/Uber Eats-style app has," never built until now. New `bank-mfe` component `LiveRiderMap.tsx`: a real self-hosted MapLibre map with the real OSRM-drawn route (reusing `RouteMiniMap`'s exact style/approach) plus an independently-polled (5s) real rider marker that moves as the real rider pushes GPS updates, shown on any `RIDER_ASSIGNED`/`PICKED_UP` order via a new "🛵 Track your rider live" toggle on `EatsOrderCard`. **A real, separate pre-existing bug was found and fixed along the way**: `fetchRestaurants()` never set a page size, silently defaulting to the backend's own 20-per-page default -- any real restaurant past the 20th spot would vanish from bank-mfe's own restaurant list entirely, breaking both this new live-tracking button and the pre-existing delivery-route button for it (found live when a freshly-registered test restaurant didn't resolve). Fixed by requesting up to 100. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: drove the complete real delivery lifecycle via curl (a restaurant with a real location, an online rider with real coordinates, a real order, real automatic dispatch to the rider, a real claim, a real GPS push), then a real headless-browser pass as the buyer -- clicked the real toggle and saw the real drawn route, real restaurant/delivery pins, and a real 🛵 marker positioned at the rider's actual pushed coordinates with an accurate "updated Xm ago" label, screenshot-confirmed, zero new console errors (only the same known, honest, pre-existing `restaurant-orders`-for-a-non-merchant 404 already documented elsewhere in this file). **Honestly open, named not silently dropped: Android and iOS don't have this yet** (web-first, matching this project's own established precedent for brand-new capabilities). **Real buyer order-status notifications added the same day.** Found while touring the real delivery lifecycle for the live-tracking work above: neither `updateRestaurantStatus`, `claimDelivery`, `updateRiderStatus`, nor `cancelOrder` ever notified the buyer at all -- the real order moved forward but the one person actually waiting on it was never told, unlike real Coupang Eats/Uber Eats/요기요/배민 which push a notification at every milestone. Closed using itunda's own already-real `Notification` system: "Order accepted," "Preparing your order," "Rider on the way" (on claim), "Order picked up," "Order delivered," and "Order cancelled" (only when the RESTAURANT cancels -- a buyer isn't notified about their own action, matching this codebase's own established convention for every other Notification call site). 6 new test cases; `:eats:test` grew 51 to 57. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: drove a real order through every real status transition and confirmed the exact right notification appeared at each step via `GET /api/v1/notifications`, plus a separate real restaurant-initiated cancellation case. **Real restaurant browse-card enrichment + a real menu-options/customization model added 2026-07-21**, closing the two highest-ranked gaps from `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s Eats section (Baemin/Coupang Eats research). (1) `Merchant` gained real `photoUrl` (merchant-set URL, same category as `webhookUrl` -- no image-upload/storage pipeline invented) and `minOrderAmount` (nullable, merchant-set) columns via `POST /api/v1/merchant/photo`/`/min-order`; `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` (shared by both Shop and Eats browse) now also returns real `rating`/`reviewCount` via a new batched `EatsReviewRepository.getRestaurantRatingSummaries` GROUP BY query (one query per browse page, not one per restaurant -- rating previously sat one tap deeper inside `RestaurantMenuView` only) and, when the caller supplies its own real `buyerLat`/`buyerLng`, a real Haversine `distanceKm` plus a real, explicitly-labeled-as-an-ESTIMATE `deliveryTimeMinutes` (base prep time + distance/assumed-speed travel time, rounded to the nearest 5 minutes -- never a fabricated or measured number, same "computed from real distance" discipline `EatsOrderService.computeDeliveryFee` already established). (2) A new menu-item options model closes what the design doc flagged as the single biggest structural gap: two new entities, `MenuOptionGroup`/`MenuOptionChoice` (migration `V62`, plus `V61` for the Merchant columns -- renumbered up from an initial `V57`/`V58` after discovering mid-session that this backend's `itunda_paycode_test` MySQL database is shared live across six parallel agents' worktrees and another agent had already claimed `V57`; switched entirely to a dedicated empty `itunda_eats_agent_test` database for this pass's own verification instead of a Flyway-validation workaround, per direct coordinator guidance mid-session), honestly scoped to v1: **required, single-select groups only** (e.g. "Size": Small/Medium/Large, exactly one choice, matching Coupang Eats' own seller-guide constraint that a required group must include at least one real +0 RWF choice so list price never diverges from cart price) -- multi-select optional add-ons are a real, separate follow-up, not built here. New `MenuOptionService` (merchant module) gives a restaurant owner `POST/GET/DELETE /api/v1/merchant/products/{productId}/option-groups`; the existing public `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants/{id}/products` now folds each product's real option groups + choices directly into the same payload (two batched queries for the whole menu, not one pair per item, per the design doc's own explicit "a join, not a new round trip" recommendation). `EatsOrderService.placeOrder` now batch-resolves every ordered item's real option groups/choices, real-422s `MISSING_REQUIRED_MENU_OPTION` if any required group has no selection, real-400s `INVALID_MENU_OPTION_SELECTION` for a choice id that doesn't belong to the product, and otherwise prices the line from base price + every selected choice's real `priceDelta` -- `unitPrice` stays the single source of truth downstream (itemsSubtotal, refunds), with a new `EatsOrderItem.selectedOptionsJson` column carrying a purely human-readable receipt breakdown (manual JSON string construction, same convention `Notification.dataJson` already established elsewhere, not a new dependency). Purely additive and non-breaking: a menu item with zero option groups (every pre-existing item, and anything Android/iOS's clients create/order today) behaves identically to before -- verified live by ordering both a configured and an unconfigured item back to back against the same restaurant. `bank-mfe` (web) got the fullest UI treatment this pass: the restaurant browse card now shows a real photo (with graceful fallback to the existing icon), star rating + review count, distance, delivery-time estimate, and minimum order amount, each clause independently conditional on real data being present; `MenuView`'s cart data model changed from a flat `Record<productId, quantity>` to a real per-configuration `EatsCartLine` keyed by `productId::sortedChoiceIds`, closing recommendation #6 (two different configurations of the same item -- e.g. a Regular and a Large -- now coexist as genuinely distinct cart lines with independent quantity steppers) for free alongside #3; a menu item with required groups shows a "Choose options" inline panel (radio buttons per group, matching this codebase's own "inline-card-replaces-trigger, no modal-overlay" convention) instead of the bare +/- stepper, and the reorder flow now sanitizes a rebuilt cart to drop any line for a product that has since gained required options it can't satisfy, rather than letting checkout silently 422. Android got the browse-card enrichment only (rating/review count/distance/delivery-time estimate/min-order text, added to `ShoppingMerchantDto` and the existing restaurant row in `SuperAppTabs.kt`) -- the photo itself isn't rendered (this app has no image-loading library like Coil yet, a real, separate dependency addition rather than something to add as a side effect here) and the full option-selection cart UI was deliberately deferred to a future pass, an honest scope-down given the size of the client-side cart-model change; iOS was not touched this pass. Backend: 3 new unit-test cases in `EatsOrderServiceTest` (valid-selection pricing, missing-required-option real-422, foreign-choice-id real-400) plus a new `MenuOptionServiceTest` (group/choice creation, negative-delta rejection, cross-merchant-ownership real-404); full `:eats:test`/`:merchant:test` suites green, and the whole multi-module backend (`compileKotlin`/`compileTestKotlin`, every module) compiles clean. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running isolated backend (port 4057) and a real, dedicated, empty `itunda_eats_agent_test` MySQL database (all 58 migrations, including the renumbered `V61`/`V62`, applied cleanly from scratch): registered a real merchant, set real photo/category/location/min-order via their own endpoints, created a real menu item with a real required "Size" group (Regular +0, Large +1000 RWF), then confirmed via `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants?buyerLat=...&buyerLng=...` that `distanceKm`/`deliveryTimeMinutes` populate correctly (5.56 km → 30 min) and `rating`/`reviewCount`/`photoUrl`/`minOrderAmount` all round-trip; placed a real order selecting "Large" x2 and confirmed via both the API response and a direct MySQL query that `unitPrice` was real base+delta (4000, not the bare 3000 base) and `selected_options_json` stored the real human-readable breakdown; confirmed the real-422/real-400 rejection paths; and confirmed a second, unconfigured menu item on the same restaurant still ordered exactly as before (zero regression). `bank-mfe` (`yarn tsc --noEmit && yarn build`) compiles and builds clean. Still open for a future pass: Android's full cart/option-selection UI, iOS entirely (both browse-card enrichment and options), multi-select optional add-on groups, and a real merchant-facing UI to create option groups on Android/iOS/merchant-mfe's own Catalog screen (this pass verified that surface via direct API calls only, following the same precedent `MerchantProductController`'s own product-creation endpoint already had before merchant-mfe's Catalog screen existed). |
| Marketplace | 당근마켓 (Danggeun/Karrot Market)-style secondhand listings | Peer-to-peer local classifieds with buyer/seller chat | real (backend: listings, browse/category filter, mark-sold, real pagination + rate limiting from day one, real "message seller" reusing the messaging system) / real (bank-mfe UI: browse/category filter, list-an-item, my-listings with mark-sold/remove, message-seller hand-off straight into the real chat thread) / real (Android UI: same flow, message-seller hands off into a real on-device chat thread) / real (iOS UI: same hand-off, live-verified) / real (proximity search, 2026-07-18, for listings that opt into a location -- see the new Maps row) | New `rw.itunda.marketplace` module, the second of the three new "super app" phases named in the 2026-07-18 goal expansion, built right after Kakao-style messaging specifically so `contactSeller` could reuse that real 1:1 conversation primitive instead of a second chat system -- `POST /listings/{id}/contact-seller` calls `MessagingService.startOrGetConversation` completely unmodified, and the resulting conversation is immediately visible through the exact same `GET /api/v1/messages/conversations` a buyer already uses for every other chat, confirmed live. New `Listing` entity (title/description/price/category/status), real input validation (blank title/description/category and non-positive price all real-400), real 10/hour per-seller rate limit on listing creation (this session's now-standard `RateLimiter` convention, built in from day one). `GET /listings` browses only `ACTIVE` listings (optionally filtered by `category`), real-paginated from day one; `GET /listings/{id}` deliberately shows a listing in ANY status (a buyer who already messaged about a now-SOLD item should still be able to open it, matching real 당근마켓's own "판매완료" badge behavior rather than 404ing it) -- `browse()` is what actually hides non-ACTIVE listings from discovery, not the individual getter. `POST /listings/{id}/mark-sold`/`DELETE /listings/{id}` are real ownership-checked (cross-tenant real-404s, not 403s, matching the IDOR discipline this session's own merchant/payroll security review established) and idempotency-safe (marking an already-SOLD listing sold again real-409s). **Real proximity search added 2026-07-18** (commit `01e0119`), closing this row's own "no real location/proximity data" limitation: `Listing` gained optional real lat/lng, and `GET /listings/nearby` real-ranks by `GeoUtils.haversineKm` distance over listings that opted into a location (`User` still has no address/district field, so a listing without coordinates simply doesn't appear in proximity results -- an honest, named fallback). In-app distance filtering over a bounded ACTIVE-with-coordinates candidate set, not a real geospatial DB index -- fine at current listing volumes, a named follow-up if volume grows. See the new Maps row for the shared `GeoUtils` this and Eats' new distance-based delivery fee both build on. No UI yet on any client for entering/browsing by location -- backend first, matching the Partner SDK/Certificate/Messaging precedent. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: created a real listing (fields trimmed), confirmed it appears in `browse()` and the correct category filter, a real buyer contacted the seller and the resulting conversation was confirmed to be a genuine messaging conversation (visible via `/api/v1/messages/conversations`, a real message sent through it), a stranger real-404'd trying to mark someone else's listing sold, the real owner's mark-sold correctly hid it from `browse()` while it stayed visible via direct `GET`, a second mark-sold attempt real-409'd, and the 10/hour rate limit correctly engaged mid-window. `:marketplace:test` (8 cases) passes. **Real `bank-mfe` UI added 2026-07-18**, closing the "no UI touchpoint" gap this row previously named: a new Marketplace tab with Browse/My-listings views, a real "List an item" form, and a "Message seller" button that calls the real `contactSeller` endpoint and hands the resulting real conversation id straight to the Messages tab -- the buyer lands directly inside the real chat thread with the seller, not a conversation list they'd have to search. Live-verified with two real, independent browser sessions (a fresh seller and a fresh buyer account): the seller listed a real item through the UI, the buyer found it while browsing with the real price shown, tapped "Message seller," landed directly in a real chat thread with the seller's real name, sent a real message, and the seller's own Messages tab showed the real new conversation with the correct unread badge and message preview -- zero console errors on either side. (A real, expected 429 was also hit and correctly handled during an earlier verification pass reusing an already-rate-limited seller account from prior testing -- confirms the rate limit itself works, not a bug; a fresh account cleared it.) | **Real 당근-style price-offer negotiation added 2026-07-19** -- see the Messaging row's own entry for the full technical account (`PriceOfferService`, migration V40) -- "Make an offer" now sits next to "Message seller" on any listing, handing a real buyer straight into the same real conversation thread with a real proposed amount already posted, not just an open chat box. **Real hyperlocal neighborhood auto-filtering added 2026-07-20** (migration V50), closing the "no real hyperlocal auto-filtering by a user's actual neighborhood" gap this row and Community/Jobs/RealEstate's own rows all named, since `User` had no address/district field anywhere in this backend. Rather than a self-declared free-text field (which could drift from where a user actually is), `User.neighborhood` is set via a new `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/neighborhood` (a real coordinate in, the same opt-in-share-my-location convention Marketplace/Community/Jobs/RealEstate's own per-post location already uses) and resolved through a real reverse-geocode against itunda's own self-hosted Nominatim (`NominatimGeocodingClient.reverseGeocode`, new -- prefers `address.suburb`, falling back through `neighbourhood`/`quarter`/`city_district`), throwing an honest `NeighborhoodNotResolvedException` rather than silently storing null when geocoding can't resolve one. Each of `Listing`/`CommunityPost`/`JobPost`/`PropertyListing` gained the identical `neighborhood` field, cached once at creation time from the listing's own coordinates (same "cache, don't recompute at read time" discipline `CommunityPost.likeCount` already established) -- best-effort, never blocks creation. New `GET .../my-neighborhood` on all four modules (`MarketplaceService.myNeighborhood` etc.) filters to an exact real string match against the caller's own resolved neighborhood, throwing `NeighborhoodNotSetException` (one per module, same message pointing back at the profile endpoint) rather than silently returning an empty page when the caller hasn't set one yet. RealEstate's variant is deliberately not combined with its existing listingType/propertyType filters this pass -- a named v1 scoping choice, not a silent gap. Test coverage added across all five modules (`:auth:test`, `:marketplace:test`, `:community:test`, `:jobs:test`, `:realestate:test`), all passing; full backend compiles clean. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL + the real deployed private-cloud Nominatim (`192.168.252.3:8088`): two real users set neighborhoods from real Kigali-area coordinates and got back real, distinct resolved names ("Nyarugenge", "Gasabo District" -- the latter falling back through the chain since that OSM area has no `suburb` tag, confirming the fallback is real, not decorative), each created one real listing/post/job/property per module, and each user's own `my-neighborhood` browse across all four modules returned exactly their own item and correctly excluded the other user's -- confirmed via each endpoint's live response, not assumed. A third, freshly-registered user with no neighborhood set correctly got the honest `NEIGHBORHOOD_NOT_SET` 400 on all four modules' endpoints. All test data cleaned up, backend stopped. **Rate-limit gap found and fixed in a 2026-07-20 security sweep**: `AuthService.setNeighborhood` had shipped with zero anti-spam protection despite calling itunda's own real self-hosted Nominatim on every single invocation -- the same class of gap already fixed for `AccountAggregationService`'s `accounts:link` ("just burned another real simulated connector call every single time"). Added `rateLimiter.checkLimit("auth:neighborhood:$userId", limit = 10, window = Duration.ofHours(1))`, real test coverage added, live-verified: 10 real calls succeeded, the 11th/12th real-429'd. **Real bank-mfe UI added 2026-07-20, closing a genuine "no UI touchpoint" gap this row's own text had silently never named** (found live during a UX-copy pass -- the entire hyperlocal-neighborhood backend, real and live-verified two days prior, had zero way for a real user to reach it through any client). New shared `NeighborhoodSetupPrompt` component (`lib/neighborhood.ts` gained `fetchProfile`/`setNeighborhood`) -- a real "📍 Share my location" button that geolocates once, calls the real `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/neighborhood`, and shows the real resolved neighborhood name. Marketplace/Community/Jobs/Property each gained a third "Neighborhood" tab alongside their existing Browse/Mine toggle, calling the real `myNeighborhood` endpoint and rendering the setup prompt automatically when the caller catches a real `NEIGHBORHOOD_NOT_SET` 400 rather than treating it as a generic load failure. `tsc -b && vite build` compiles clean. Live-verified end-to-end in a real headless Chromium browser (Playwright, with a real Kigali-center `geolocation` context grant) against a real running backend + MySQL + the real deployed Nominatim: a real user shared their location, got back the real resolved "Nyarugenge" neighborhood, saw the real empty state before posting anything, created a real Marketplace listing and a real Community post at that same coordinate through the existing "share my real location" opt-in each already had, and confirmed both showed up correctly in their respective Neighborhood tabs afterward (cross-checked the Community post's real cached `neighborhood` column via direct MySQL query too) -- Jobs and Property both correctly rendered the already-set "Nyarugenge" header on the same account, matching the identical shared-component wiring. One expected, handled `400` (the very first `NEIGHBORHOOD_NOT_SET` check before setup) shows in the browser console as a real failed-resource entry, same as the Merchant row's own already-documented `GET /me` 404 check -- not a bug. No Android/iOS UI yet for this feature -- web-first, matching this project's own established "new capability, web first" precedent. **Android/iOS Neighborhood-tab UI added 2026-07-20** -- ported field-for-field to `SuperAppTabs.kt` (Android) and `HoodScreen.swift` (iOS), reusing the same shared `NeighborhoodSetupPrompt`/Neighborhood-tab pattern bank-mfe established two days prior; closes this gap. `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target. All three client platforms now real for this feature. **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20, commit `bffb3a6`:** `MarketplaceService.createListing` never bounded `title`/`description` before insert (VARCHAR 255/2000 respectively) despite this DB's real `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` mode throwing a raw 500 on an over-length insert -- found via a systematic sweep of the whole backend for this exact bug class (first caught on the Eats/Commerce delivery-address fix the same day). Fixed by rejecting with a clean `INVALID_LISTING` 400 rather than truncating. Live-verified against a real running backend: a real 256-char title now cleanly 400s instead of crashing. **Real listing wishlist added 2026-07-21, backend + bank-mfe, closing a gap `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s Hood research named directly**: itunda already had this exact 찜하기/wishlist pattern for Shop products (`ProductFavoriteService`) and Eats restaurants (`EatsFavoriteService`) but never for Hood listings themselves. New `CommunityPost`/`CommunityComment`/`CommunityLike` entities (migration V42). **A deliberate architecture choice, informed directly by this session's own prior performance sweep** (which found and fixed N+1s in `MessagingService.listConversations`/`GroupMessagingService.listMyGroups`): `likeCount`/`commentCount` are real cached counters updated transactionally in the same transaction as the like-toggle/comment-create action, NOT computed at read time -- unlike `EatsReview`'s aggregate rating (viewed one restaurant at a time), a post's counts are shown on every card in a paginated feed, so computing them per-post at read time would reintroduce exactly the N+1 shape that sweep already fixed elsewhere. Real category browse (`question`/`news`/`recommendation`/`lost_found`/`meetup`/`free`, a fixed small list validated server-side, unlike `Merchant.category`'s deliberately free-form field -- Karrot's real app uses fixed post categories, a merchant's business category is genuinely arbitrary). Real opt-in proximity ("near me") browse reuses the same `GeoUtils.haversineKm` foundation Marketplace/Eats already established, Haversine-only for v1 (no OSRM road-ranking yet, an honest, named follow-up matching Marketplace's own v1-before-OSRM-upgrade precedent). Real comment-notification reuses the existing in-app `Notification` system (matching `MessagingService`'s own "new message" notification), never sent to yourself commenting on your own post. **Real like/unlike toggle is rate-limited from day one (60/min/user)** -- a lesson applied directly from this session's own security sweep, which found `MessagingService.toggleReaction` had shipped with zero rate limiting; a "toggle" endpoint still writes to the DB and needs the same discipline as every other mutating endpoint, not an exception. `requireAuthor`/IDOR checks mirror `MarketplaceService.requireOwner` exactly (a stranger's remove attempt real-404s, never revealing the post exists). `:community:test` (17 cases). A single `price` field is disambiguated by `listingType` (sale price vs monthly rent), mirroring how `JobPost.payAmount` is disambiguated by `payType`. Real fixed property-type list (apartment/house/room/land/commercial/other, validated server-side, same convention as Jobs'/Community's own fixed categories) and independently-combinable `listingType`/`propertyType` browse filters (four real repository query variants, chosen based on which filters are present, mirroring `MerchantRepository.search`'s own combinable-filter shape). **Real "message lister" IS the real inquiry mechanism** -- `contactLister` reuses `MessagingService.startOrGetConversation` completely unmodified, identical to `MarketplaceService.contactSeller`/`JobPostService.contactPoster`; no price-offer negotiation yet, an honest, named follow-up matching how Marketplace's own price-offers shipped as a later addition, not required for a usable v1.
