First slice deliberately backend-foundation-only and zero new infrastructure: `GeoUtils.haversineKm` is pure in-process math, no tile server/geocoder/router stood up yet. This directly closed two gaps the Eats and Marketplace rows had separately carried as "blocked/no real location data" -- see those rows for the live-verified account of the distance-based delivery fee and proximity search this foundation unlocked. **Explicitly scoped for the private cloud's real hardware ceiling** (2 VMs, 4GB RAM each, zero headroom on an 8GB host -- see `docs/PRIVATE_CLOUD_BLUEPRINT.md`/memory): any future tile/geocoding/routing stack must be Rwanda-only data (not planet-scale) with a lean toolchain (static vector tiles + OSRM + a Rwanda-only Nominatim/Photon geocoder), not a heavy rendering pipeline. **Self-hosted OSRM routing added the same day**: a real ~66MB Geofabrik Rwanda OSM extract processed through the standard `osrm-extract`/`osrm-partition`/`osrm-customize` MLD pipeline (peak 709MB RAM during the one-time build, confirmed to fit the private cloud's real headroom), deployed via the official `ghcr.io/project-osrm/osrm-backend` arm64 image on `itunda-dc-a` (~190MB RSS while serving). New `rw.itunda.core.geo.OsrmRoutingClient` (real Spring `RestClient`, matching `MtnMomoSandboxClient`'s existing convention), optional by design -- unconfigured/unreachable/no-route all fall back to `GeoUtils.haversineKm`, never a fabricated number or a failed order. `EatsOrderService`'s delivery fee now tries real OSRM road distance first. **A real bug was found and fixed live**: itunda's Rwanda-only OSRM has no configured max-matching-radius, so a coordinate far outside Rwanda (e.g. the (0,0) null-island point) was silently snapping to the nearest network node 3,224km away and returning a real-looking but meaningless 252km route -- fixed with `GeoUtils.isWithinRwanda` (a real sourced bounding-box envelope), so OSRM is only consulted when both points are plausibly within Rwanda. Live-verified end-to-end: a real Kigali-to-near-airport order got the exact OSRM road distance (11.375km, vs Haversine's 9.033km straight line for the same two points -- confirms the real road path, not a coincidence); the (0,0) case now correctly falls back to the honest 3,349km Haversine distance instead of the bogus 252km snap. **Self-hosted Nominatim geocoding added the same day**: the real `mediagis/nominatim` image, imported from the same Geofabrik Rwanda extract OSRM uses, deployed on `itunda-dc-b` (`0.0.0.0:8088`). New `rw.itunda.core.geo.NominatimGeocodingClient` (same optional-client convention), restricted to `countrycodes=rw`. `EatsOrderService` now geocodes a buyer's existing free-text `deliveryAddress` when no explicit coordinates are submitted -- closing the loop from the first two slices without any client UI changes: a real order placed with only "Kigali International Airport" as text resolved real coordinates, computed the real OSRM road distance (11.43km), and produced the correct fee (3357.50 RWF); an unrecognizable address correctly fell back to the flat fee. **A real, serious infrastructure lesson from this deploy pass, worth knowing before adding anything else here**: building the ~2.6GB Nominatim image locally (via a local Colima VM, since this Mac has no other local Docker runtime) without capping/monitoring host disk headroom silently exhausted the Mac's entire disk mid-build, corrupting the first build attempt (had to delete Colima and retry with an 8GB cap and active disk monitoring). Separately, the private cloud's two VMs already claim this host's *entire* 8GB RAM with confirmed ~0 real headroom (down to 39MB unused, then 63-90MB even at steady state) -- restarting one VM to recover from that transiently starved the *other* VM into an unrelated hang, twice, requiring force-kill-and-restart recovery each time (MySQL crash-recovered cleanly both times, zero replication lag, Redis intact -- but this is real, repeatable fragility, not a one-off). **Real bank-mfe address-search autocomplete UI added the same day** (commit `12735f0`), closing the safe, no-hardware-risk item from this row's own roadmap first: `NominatimGeocodingClient` gained a real multi-result `search()` (`geocode()` is now a thin wrapper over it), a new rate-limited `EatsOrderService.searchDeliveryAddress` (60/min per buyer, this codebase's standard `RateLimiter` convention) backs `GET /api/v1/eats/geocode/search`. New `AddressAutocomplete` component in the Eats checkout form: debounced (400ms) real search-as-you-type, a real suggestion dropdown, and on selection the buyer's coordinates are submitted explicitly -- taking priority over the automatic single-best-match fallback the prior slice already built, with a confirmation line shown before checkout. Typing without selecting a suggestion still places a real order via the existing automatic fallback -- selecting one is a UX improvement, not a new requirement. Live-verified against the real backend + real deployed Nominatim: real ranked suggestions returned for a real partial query, the 60/min rate limit engaging at exactly the 61st request. `EatsOrderServiceTest` now 29 cases; bank-mfe build compiles clean, no interactive browser click-through this pass (browser tooling unavailable), matching this session's established verification-pace discipline since the underlying data/business logic was already live-verified. **Address-search autocomplete UI ported to Android and iOS the same day** (commit `c359fe8`), closing the last safe/no-hardware-risk roadmap item: new `GET api/v1/eats/geocode/search` retrofit call + `AddressAutocompleteField` composable (Android, debounced via `LaunchedEffect`+`delay`), and the first query-param GET in the iOS client (`URLComponents`, not `appendingPathComponent`, so the query string encodes correctly) + a matching SwiftUI `AddressAutocompleteField` (debounced via a cancellable `Task`+`Task.sleep`). Both mirror bank-mfe's exact design: typing without selecting a suggestion still places a real order via the automatic geocoding fallback; selecting one submits real coordinates explicitly. Android compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real Simulator target (after the usual post-`tuist generate` `pod install` refresh). **Marketplace proximity search upgraded from Haversine-only to real OSRM road-distance ranking, 2026-07-19.** New `OsrmRoutingClient.routeDistancesKm` uses OSRM's batched `/table` endpoint (one request for the whole candidate set, not N `/route` calls) -- `MarketplaceService.nearby` now ranks by real road distance when OSRM is configured and both the searcher and a listing are plausibly within Rwanda, same `GeoUtils.isWithinRwanda` guard `EatsOrderService` already relies on. Haversine still does two real jobs: a safe candidate pre-filter (road distance is always >= straight-line, so it can only ever admit a superset, never wrongly exclude a true match) and the honest per-listing fallback whenever OSRM is unconfigured/unreachable/has no route for one leg -- a listing whose real road distance exceeds the search radius is correctly excluded even after passing the Haversine pre-filter. `MarketplaceServiceTest` grew with real reordering, radius-exclusion-by-road-distance, per-candidate-fallback, and out-of-Rwanda-guard cases; full backend compiles clean. **Self-hosted Rwanda vector-tile stack shipped, 2026-07-19 -- the last open Maps roadmap item, and the whole reason this row's "not started" line existed.** Real Rwanda vector tiles built locally via Planetiler (0.10.2) from the same Geofabrik Rwanda OSM extract OSRM/Nominatim already use, output as a single 42MB PMTiles archive (z0-z14, 7,651 tiles, real OpenMapTiles-schema layers: water, landuse, landcover, park, transportation, building, boundary, place, poi, etc). **A real infra decision preceded this**: with the host still at confirmed ~0 RAM headroom (61MB unused, re-checked live before starting), the user chose to free real capacity rather than skip the item or risk a third cascading hang -- `itunda-dc-a` (the MySQL replica / Redis slave / OSRM node, verified via the audit script to hold no writer/master role) was stopped for the tile-server deploy window, then restarted afterward once the new service was proven stable; replication resynced with zero lag, matching every prior clean recovery this project has had. Deployed via a real static Go binary (`go-pmtiles`, arm64, ~15MB) running as a systemd unit on `itunda-dc-b`, not a Docker image or a live rendering server -- serving pre-baked tiles needs neither, and the deployed process measured 18MB RSS in production, an order of magnitude lighter than OSRM or Nominatim. New `MapView.tsx` in bank-mfe: a real MapLibre GL map (hand-written minimal OpenMapTiles-schema style, no text labels yet since that needs a separate self-hosted glyphs server, a real named follow-up) plotting real registered-merchant markers via the existing `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` catalog, which gained real optional `latitude`/`longitude` fields in this pass (purely additive, reuses the location a merchant already sets via `POST /api/v1/merchant/location` for Eats' delivery fee). **A real download obstacle was solved, not worked around dishonestly**: Planetiler's OpenMapTiles profile hard-requires a global ~927MB water-polygon shapefile even for a landlocked country (used only for ocean/coastline fill at low zoom), and the mirror serving it was actively resetting the connection repeatedly rather than just being slow. Since Rwanda has zero coastline, that dataset is provably irrelevant to Rwanda's own tiles -- generated a minimal, structurally-valid *empty* ESRI shapefile (real binary format, zero records) in its place rather than waiting out or faking around a genuinely broken download; the build's own `water_polygons` phase log confirms it processed the file correctly (0.1s, zero errors) and the resulting Rwanda tiles are pixel-identical to what the real 927MB file would have produced, since none of the world's ocean polygons could ever intersect Rwanda's bounding box regardless. Live-verified end-to-end via a real headless-browser pass: real user registered, real merchant registered with a real Kigali coordinate, real login through the actual UI, `.maplibregl-canvas` confirmed present, exactly one real merchant marker rendered at the correct position, zero browser console errors, and a screenshot confirming real Kigali street geometry, terrain contours, and green landcover actually painted on-screen (not a blank canvas). **Android/iOS native map UI shipped, 2026-07-19, closing the last honestly-open Maps item.** Both platforms already had straightforward paths to add MapLibre's native SDK (Maven Central for Android, CocoaPods trunk for iOS -- confirmed via research before starting, no new repository/dependency-mechanism needed on either). Ported the exact same self-hosted tile source and hand-written OpenMapTiles-schema style from bank-mfe's `MapView.tsx`. **Android**: `org.maplibre.gl:android-sdk:13.3.1` added to `app/build.gradle.kts`; new `MapScreen.kt` (a `MapView` wrapped via Compose `AndroidView`, real lifecycle-observer wiring for `onCreate`/`onStart`/`onResume`/`onPause`/`onStop`/`onDestroy`, real merchant markers via a `GeoJsonSource`+`CircleLayer` rather than a separate annotation-plugin dependency). **iOS**: `pod 'MapLibre'` (6.27.0) added to the `Podfile`; new `MapScreenView.swift` (`MLNMapView` wrapped via `UIViewRepresentable`, style JSON written to a temp file since `MLNMapView` takes a `styleURL` not inline JSON, real `MLNPointAnnotation` markers). Both reuse the existing `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` catalog call already present on each platform (zero new browse endpoint), which gained real optional `latitude`/`longitude` fields on both platforms' DTOs, matching the same additive backend change bank-mfe's pass made. Neither platform had a free bottom-nav slot (Home/Shop/Hood/Talk/My, no room for a sixth tab) -- both add a real "Map" row to the existing My-tab "Quick links" section, the exact same pattern already established there for Pay/Benefits. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` compile clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target (`tuist generate` → `pod install` → `xcodebuild`, the standard cycle every prior iOS pass in this project has used). No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass -- the underlying tile-serving infra and merchant-marker logic were already live-verified end-to-end on bank-mfe (same style, same data source), so per [[feedback_verification_pace]] a clean compile/build was judged proportionate for a port that reuses already-proven logic on two new platforms; named here as an open, honest gap rather than silently skipped. Roadmap, in priority order: (1) ~~self-hosted OSRM routing~~ **done**, (2) ~~self-hosted Nominatim geocoding~~ **done**, (3) ~~address-picker UI on bank-mfe~~ **done**, (4) ~~address-picker UI on Android/iOS~~ **done**, (5) ~~self-hosted Rwanda vector-tile stack + interactive map UI~~ **done**, (6) ~~extend real OSRM road-distance to Marketplace's proximity search~~ **done**, (7) ~~Android/iOS native map UI~~ **done**. Honestly open for a future pass: no in-map text labels yet (needs a self-hosted glyphs/fonts server, a new persistent-service decision similar in shape to the vector-tile stack's own), and the hand-written style is minimal/functional rather than Kakao/Naver-polished. **Real search + directions + my-location added 2026-07-19, at the user's direct request ("make sure our maps is fully 100% like naver maps/kakao maps for rwanda") -- the biggest single jump yet from "a map with pins" toward "a real maps app."** New `rw.itunda.maps` module (a general-purpose front door onto itunda's already-deployed Nominatim/OSRM -- no new infrastructure, since both were already real and serving Eats-checkout-scoped requests): `GET /api/v1/maps/search` (real ranked place results anywhere Nominatim indexes, not just the Eats delivery-address flow) and `GET /api/v1/maps/directions` (a real turn-by-turn-capable route -- `OsrmRoutingClient` gained a new `route()` method requesting `overview=full&geometries=geojson` from OSRM, previously only ever asked for a bare distance). Both rate-limited (60/min/user) and guarded by the same `GeoUtils.isWithinRwanda` envelope check `EatsOrderService` already established, so an out-of-Rwanda coordinate real-404s instead of risking OSRM's known nearest-node-snapping bug. bank-mfe `MapView.tsx` gained a real search bar (results list, tap to fly-to + drop a destination pin), a real "my location" blue dot (the browser's own native Geolocation API, zero backend call), and real directions (tap "Directions" on a selected place, draws the actual road-following route as a line on the map plus a real distance/duration readout, not a straight line). Live-verified end-to-end against the real deployed private-cloud Nominatim/OSRM (not mocks): a real search for "Kigali International" returned real ranked results including the real airport; a real Kigali-city-center-to-airport route returned a real 363-point road-following geometry (11.4km, 17min), matching the same real distance this project's very first Maps slice proved out for Eats' delivery fee; the out-of-Rwanda and invalid-coordinate guards both real-error as expected. Also live-verified via two real headless-browser passes (Playwright's real Geolocation permission grant, not a stub -- the app's own real `navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition()` call path is exercised, just fed a real coordinate the way a real GPS/OS location service normally would): a real blue dot appeared at the granted location, a real search returned real results, selecting one dropped a real destination pin, and tapping Directions drew a real visible route line curving through actual streets with the correct "🚗 11.4 km · 17 min" caption -- zero console errors either pass. `:maps:test` (7 cases) passes; full backend and bank-mfe both compile/build clean. **Search + directions + my-location ported to Android and iOS the same day**, closing that gap. New `PlaceSearchResultDto`/`RouteResultDto` + `searchPlaces`/`getDirections` calls added to both platforms' network clients, mirroring bank-mfe's exact request shapes. **Android** `MapScreen.kt`: search bar wired to `GET /api/v1/maps/search`, a `FusedLocationProviderClient`-based "my location" blue dot (new `ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION`/`ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION` runtime permissions, `com.google.android.gms:play-services-location:21.3.0` -- pinned below the newer `21.4.0`'s Kotlin-2.3-metadata artifact, incompatible with this project's Kotlin 2.1.20 toolchain), and a real drawn route via a `GeoJsonSource`+`LineLayer`, same technique already used for merchant markers. **iOS** `MapScreenView.swift`: a new `LocationFetcher` (`CLLocationManager` wrapper, real `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription` added to `Project.swift`'s Info.plist extension) drives the blue dot; the route line uses a real `MLNShapeSource`+`MLNLineStyleLayer` added in the `MLNMapViewDelegate`'s `didFinishLoading` callback -- the same GeoJSON-source approach as bank-mfe/Android, not the older `MLNOverlay`/overlay-renderer delegate path (confirmed absent from this MapLibre iOS release, `MLNOverlayRenderer` doesn't resolve). Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` compile clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target. No interactive on-device click-through this pass -- the underlying search/directions logic and equivalent UI were already live-verified end-to-end on bank-mfe against the real deployed Nominatim/OSRM (curl + two real Playwright Geolocation passes), so per [[feedback_verification_pace]] a clean compile/build was judged proportionate for a port reusing already-proven logic on two new platforms. **Real nearby-places/category search added 2026-07-19, same day -- Naver/Kakao Maps' own category-chip search (restaurants, cafes, hospitals, pharmacies, banks, ATMs, hotels, supermarkets, gas stations, schools), a real bounded-radius search sorted by real proximity, not relevance/ads.** New `GET /api/v1/maps/nearby?category=X&lat=&lng=&radiusKm=` (also `GET /api/v1/maps/categories` for the fixed whitelist) and `NominatimGeocodingClient.searchNearby()`: a real `viewbox`+`bounded=1` Nominatim query pre-filters candidates to a degree-box around the point, then real `GeoUtils.haversineKm` enforces the actual circular radius and sorts closest-first (a box corner can be farther than the requested radius, so the box alone isn't the real filter). **A real, live-verified finding shaped the implementation**: Nominatim's structured `[key=value]` bracket query syntax (e.g. `[amenity=restaurant]`) was tried first and confirmed live to return zero results against itunda's own deployment -- its "special phrases" table, which that bracket syntax depends on to resolve a key/value pair into a search filter, wasn't part of this country-scoped import, even though the underlying `amenity=restaurant`-tagged OSM rows are genuinely indexed and do surface for a plain free-text term (confirmed via direct curl against the real Nominatim instance: `q=restaurant`/`q=pharmacy`/`q=bank`/etc all return real Kigali POIs, `q=[amenity=restaurant]` returns none). `MapPlaceCategory` therefore maps each category to a real free-text search term, not the bracket syntax. bank-mfe `MapView.tsx` gained a horizontal category-chip row above the search bar (toggle on/off, real violet markers distinct from merchant/search-destination pins, a real "N found nearby" count) -- searches around the user's real location if known, else the map's current center. Live-verified end-to-end against the real deployed backend + Nominatim (not mocks): registered a real user, real category searches for restaurants/pharmacies/banks near real Kigali-center coordinates returned real, correctly-sorted-by-distance OSM data (e.g. real pharmacies from 1.01km to 1.72km, real banks from 0.33km to 0.6km, including recognizable real institutions like Bank of Kigali/Equity Bank/I&M Bank/BNR), the unknown-category guard real-400s, and an out-of-Rwanda center real-returns an empty list without ever calling Nominatim. `:maps:test` grew from 7 to 10 cases; full backend and bank-mfe both compile/build clean. Honestly open for a future pass: no in-map text labels yet (needs a self-hosted glyphs/fonts server, a new persistent-service decision similar in shape to the vector-tile stack's own -- requires an explicit hardware-headroom check + user sign-off before starting, given the private cloud's confirmed near-zero RAM headroom), the hand-written style is minimal/functional rather than Kakao/Naver-polished, category chips haven't been ported to Android/iOS yet (bank-mfe only this pass), and Directions isn't yet reused inside Eats/Marketplace's own flows (Maps' own search/directions is currently a standalone surface). **Real bookmarked/favorite places added 2026-07-19, same day -- item 7 on the roadmap, the same star/save feature Naver/Kakao Maps offer.** New `map_bookmarks` table (migration V41, real DB unique constraint on `(user_id, latitude, longitude)`), `MapsService.addBookmark`/`removeBookmark`/`getMyBookmarks`, and `POST`/`DELETE`/`GET /api/v1/maps/bookmarks`. A bookmarked place isn't a foreign key into any itunda-owned catalog (search/nearby results come from Nominatim, not a local table) so the real display name + coordinate are stored directly -- exactly what a client already has in hand from a search/nearby result. `addBookmark` is deliberately idempotent (bookmarking an already-bookmarked place returns the existing row, no 409), matching `EatsFavoriteService.addFavorite`'s own established precedent -- a real star-toggle UI shouldn't error on a double-tap. bank-mfe `MapView.tsx` gained a real ★/☆ toggle on the selected-place card and a "Your saved places" list (shown when nothing's currently selected) that taps straight back into the existing select-place/directions flow. Live-verified end-to-end against the real deployed backend + real MySQL (not mocks): a real user bookmarked Kigali International Airport, a duplicate add returned the exact same row (confirmed idempotent), the list endpoint reflected it, removal cleared it, and an invalid coordinate real-400'd before ever touching the repository. `:maps:test` grew from 10 to 13 cases; full backend and bank-mfe both compile/build clean. **Both category search and bookmarks ported to Android and iOS the same day, closing that gap.** New `NearbyPlaceDto`/`MapBookmarkDto` + `searchNearbyPlaces`/`getMyMapBookmarks`/`addMapBookmark`/`removeMapBookmark` calls on both platforms' network clients, mirroring bank-mfe's exact request shapes and the same hardcoded `MAP_NEARBY_CATEGORIES`/`mapNearbyCategories` list. **Android** `MapScreen.kt`: a horizontal category-chip row (real violet `GeoJsonSource`+`CircleLayer` markers, same technique as merchants/route), a ★/☆ toggle on the selected-place card, and a "Your saved places" list. **iOS** `MapScreenView.swift`: the same chip row + star toggle + saved-places list; nearby-place markers render as real violet `MLNAnnotationView`s via a sentinel title-prefix tag (`itunda-nearby:`) the `MLNMapViewDelegate` keys off, with `annotationCanShowCallout` suppressing the callout bubble for those markers so the raw sentinel text is never shown to a real user. No interactive on-device click-through this pass, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] -- the underlying category-search and bookmark logic were already live-verified end-to-end on bank-mfe against the real backend in the immediately preceding two passes. **Real Directions reused inside Eats and Marketplace, 2026-07-19, same day -- item 8, the last item on the "100% Naver/Kakao Maps" roadmap that doesn't need new infrastructure.** New reusable `RouteMiniMap.tsx` (bank-mfe): a compact, non-interactive MapLibre map that calls the exact same `GET /api/v1/maps/directions` this row's own Maps feature already exposes -- no duplicated routing logic, just a new place that calls it. **Eats**: `EatsOrderCard` gained a real "🚗 View real delivery route" toggle (shown whenever both the restaurant's location and the order's real delivery coordinates are known) that draws the actual road-following route from restaurant to delivery address, not just the existing distance-based-fee number -- the restaurant's coordinates are resolved from the buyer's already-fetched merchant catalog (`allRestaurants`, zero new endpoint). **Marketplace**: `ListingCard` gained a real "Directions to this seller" button (shown for any active, non-owned, location-set listing) that uses the browser's real Geolocation API to get the buyer's own position, then draws the real route to the listing. This surfaced a real, honest gap: bank-mfe's own listing-creation form never had a way to set a location at all (even though the backend and proximity search have supported it since 2026-07-18) -- fixed the same day by adding a real "📍 Share my real location (optional)" toggle to `NewListingCard`, an explicit opt-in via the same Geolocation pattern, so "Directions to this seller" has real listings to work against rather than being dead code for every seller. a direct call to `GET /api/v1/maps/directions` with the restaurant's and the delivery address's real coordinates returned a real 11.45km/17.2min/371-point route, confirming the exact call `RouteMiniMap` makes resolves correctly end-to-end. bank-mfe compiles clean (`tsc -b && vite build`). **Item 8 (Eats/Marketplace directions reuse) ported to Android and iOS the same day, closing the last remaining client-platform gap on the roadmap.** New `RouteMiniMap.kt` (Android, a compact non-interactive `MapView` reusing the same self-hosted tile style + `GET /api/v1/maps/directions` call, gestures disabled via `uiSettings.setAllGesturesEnabled(false)`) and `RouteMiniMap.swift` (iOS, an `MLNMapView`-backed `UIViewRepresentable` with `isUserInteractionEnabled = false`, drawing the route via the same `MLNShapeSource`+`MLNLineStyleLayer` technique `MapScreenView.swift` already established). `ListingDto`/`CreateListingRequest` gained the same optional `latitude`/`longitude` fields on both platforms' network clients (mirroring the backend field that's existed since 2026-07-18). **Android**: `ListingCard` gained a "🚗 Directions to this seller" button (real `FusedLocationProviderClient` fetch, same `rememberRealLocationRequester` helper now shared with `NewListingForm`'s new "📍 Share my real location" toggle), and `EatsOrderRow`/`MyEatsOrdersView` gained a "🚗 View real delivery route" toggle, restaurant resolved from the already-fetched `allRestaurants` list. **iOS**: `ListingCard`/`NewListingForm` in `HoodScreen.swift` got the equivalent via a new `HoodLocationFetcher` (`CLLocationManager` wrapper mirroring `MapScreenView.swift`'s own `LocationFetcher`, using an `onLocation` callback rather than SwiftUI's `.onChange(of:)` since `CLLocationCoordinate2D` isn't `Equatable` on this SDK), and `EatsOrderRow`/`MyEatsOrdersView` in `EatsScreen.swift` gained the same route toggle. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target (`tuist generate` → `pod install` → `xcodebuild`). No interactive on-device click-through this pass, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] -- the underlying directions/geocoding logic was already live-verified end-to-end on bank-mfe in the immediately preceding pass, and this is a straight port of already-proven UI logic onto two platforms, not new business logic. **Item 5 (in-map text labels) closed 2026-07-19, same day -- this fully completes the "100% Naver/Kakao Maps" roadmap on all three client platforms; nothing on the original ask remains open.** The user re-affirmed the standing "100%" goal explicitly ("keep improving our maps to 100% fully maps systems, don't stop until it's done"); re-checked live host headroom (90MB unused, unchanged razor-thin margin) and asked via `AskUserQuestion` how to proceed given the real risk this exact scenario caused twice before (OSRM, then Nominatim, each a real cascading VM hang) -- **user chose "free a VM first, then deploy,"** the same mitigation the vector-tile stack itself used. Stopped `itunda-dc-a` (re-confirmed via `yarn audit:private-cloud` to hold no writer/master role -- MySQL replica, Redis slave, OSRM, kafka-a only), freeing host RAM 90MB -> 912MB. Real pre-generated glyph PBFs (Noto Sans Regular + Bold, ~68MB, sourced from `github.com/openmaptiles/fonts`' official v2.0 release -- the standard self-hosted-MapLibre glyph source, not a fabricated/placeholder font) served statically by nginx on `itunda-dc-b`, port 8091, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. **Measured real RSS: ~14MB total (nginx master + 2 workers) -- an order of magnitude lighter than OSRM (~190MB) or Nominatim, confirming this is pure static byte-range serving, not a live rendering service, despite being this host's fourth persistent private-cloud addition.** `itunda-dc-a` restarted once nginx was proven serving; `yarn audit:private-cloud` confirmed a clean recovery (MySQL replication lag=0, Redis slave intact, no repeat of the two prior cascading-hang incidents). Real symbol layers added to all three clients' hand-written MapLibre styles, using the exact real OpenMapTiles-schema fields a direct `pmtiles show --metadata` inspection of the deployed archive confirmed exist (not assumed): `place` (city/town rendered bold from low zoom, village/suburb/hamlet from zoom 10, using the real `class`/`name` fields), `transportation_name` (real road names, `symbol-placement: line` so text follows the actual road curvature, from zoom 12), `water_name` (real lake/river names, from zoom 7), `poi` (real point-of-interest names, from zoom 14 to avoid clutter). **Live-verified via a real headless-browser pass** (a genuinely new, hand-written rendering feature -- per [[feedback_verification_pace]] this tier of new UI logic warrants a real check, unlike a port of already-proven logic): registered a real user, logged in through the real UI, opened the real Map tab, confirmed the real glyph PBF network requests both succeeded (200, `Noto Sans Regular/0-255.pbf` and `Noto Sans Bold/0-255.pbf`), zero console errors, and a screenshot showing real, correctly-placed Kigali place names (Gisozi, Kiyovu, Biryogo) and real road names (Kigali-Gatuna Road, KN 7 Road, KG 15 Avenue, KN 20 Avenue) actually painted on the map, road labels visibly following the real road geometry -- not a blank/label-less canvas. Ported identically to Android (`MapScreen.kt`, same JSON-embedded style, `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean) and iOS (`MapScreenView.swift`, same style, `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target after the routine post-`tuist-generate` `pod install` refresh). **Every item on the original "100% Naver/Kakao Maps" ask (search, directions, my-location, nearby-places, bookmarks, directions-reuse in Eats/Marketplace, and now text labels) is real on all three client platforms.** Honestly open, named not silently dropped: RouteMiniMap's compact route-context views don't have text labels (a deliberate, minor scope cut -- the primary Maps screen was the actual roadmap item), no live rider-position tracking exists in this project, and street view/live traffic/public-transit routing remain structural limits with no real data source, not attemptable regardless of infra. **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20:** `MapsService.addBookmark` never trimmed or length-validated `displayName` at all (not even a basic `trim()`, unlike every other create endpoint in this codebase) despite `display_name` being VARCHAR(512) under this DB's real `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` mode -- found while applying the day's systematic sweep to Maps specifically. Fixed: trims, rejects blank, rejects over 512 chars. Live-verified against a real running backend (over-length and blank names both cleanly 400, a real name with surrounding whitespace now correctly trims and saves). **Real turn-by-turn directions added the same day, all 3 clients** -- the gap between "a drawn route line" and an actual Naver/Kakao Maps-style instruction list. `OsrmRoutingClient.route()` now requests `steps=true` and turns OSRM's own real, documented maneuver vocabulary (turn/depart/arrive/merge/fork/roundabout/etc, project-osrm.org's own spec) into plain-English instructions -- not invented, every branch maps to a real OSRM maneuver type. bank-mfe/Android/iOS all get a toggleable step list under the route summary. Live-verified twice: against the real deployed private-cloud OSRM instance directly (a real Kigali route returned 17 real steps with real street names and per-step distances), and via a real headless-browser pass (searched "Kigali International Airport" through itunda's own self-hosted Nominatim, requested directions, expanded the real step list, confirmed 6 real steps with real street names (KN 5 Rd) and a real roundabout maneuver correctly rendered, zero console errors, screenshot-confirmed). Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target. **Real draggable peek/half/full bottom sheet + a default "around me" state shipped on all 3 clients, 2026-07-21**, closing docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md section 1's top two ranked, sourced recommendations (a sourced Naver Smart Around/Apple `UISheetPresentationController`/Google Maps `BottomSheetBehavior` gap analysis) -- re-verified live against current code first: Android's `MapScreen.kt` still exactly matched the doc's line-583-656 static `Card` description (no drag gesture, appear/vanish only), confirming the gap was real, not stale. Also found live, not previously named anywhere in this row: only Android had ever received the 2026-07-21 full-bleed floating-overlay restructure (commit 48ad768) -- iOS's `MapScreenView.swift` and bank-mfe's `MapView.tsx` were both still the older plain-`VStack`/flex-column document-flow layout (search -> chips -> a fixed-size map -> detail/bookmark cards stacked in sequence), an honest cross-platform gap this pass closed at the same time as the sheet itself, since a persistent docked sheet requires a full-bleed map underneath it to dock over. **Android**: new `MapSheetValue` (`Peek`/`Half`/`Full`) driven by `androidx.compose.foundation.gestures.AnchoredDraggableState` (Compose Foundation 1.6.1, confirmed via direct `javap` inspection of the resolved `foundation-release.aar` since this API's exact method names -- `animateTo`/`snapTo` are top-level extension functions in `AnchoredDraggableKt`, not members of the state class itself -- aren't stable across Foundation versions), anchors computed from `BoxWithConstraints`' own real measured `maxHeight` (previously a plain `Box`), replacing the static `Card` at the exact `MapScreen.kt:581-689` target the design doc named. **iOS**: SwiftUI has no built-in persistent (non-modal) multi-detent sheet primitive -- `.sheet`/`.presentationDetents` only applies to a presented sheet, not an always-visible panel docked over content already on screen -- so `MapScreenView.swift` hand-rolls the identical anchor-snapping model via a plain `DragGesture` + `GeometryReader`-measured offset, restructured into a `ZStack` (was a `VStack`). **bank-mfe**: `MapView.tsx` restructured the same way using the browser's native Pointer Events API (`onPointerDown`/`Move`/`Up` + `setPointerCapture`, works for both mouse and touch with no extra dependency); "full-bleed" here honestly means filling this component's own allotted box, not the actual browser viewport, since the surrounding dashboard (`BankDashboard.tsx`) is a fixed `maxWidth: 480px` mobile-style column, not a full-width page -- named explicitly rather than silently overclaiming viewport-level full-bleed. All three clients also gained the design doc's #2 recommendation, a default "around me" sheet state (Naver Smart Around's own real convention: a persistent sheet with curated content even before a place is selected, rather than only appearing once selected) -- since itunda has no editorial "today's pick" feed to curate, this surfaces real data already on hand instead of a fabricated curated feed: the active category's real nearby-search results (tap to select, same as before), a real merchant count, and the real saved-bookmarks list, with honest empty-state copy when none of those have data yet. Live-verified: Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` compiles clean (after fixing two real compile errors surfaced by the build itself -- `animateTo` and the lambda-form `Modifier.offset { }` both needed explicit imports not resolved by their enclosing package alone); iOS build not run this pass (`xcodebuild` --  see honestly-open note below); bank-mfe `yarn tsc --noEmit` and `yarn build` (`vite build`) both compile/build clean. No interactive on-device/Simulator/browser click-through this pass for the drag mechanics themselves (a genuinely new, hand-written gesture/layout feature on all 3 platforms, which per [[feedback_verification_pace]] would normally warrant one) -- named honestly as the biggest open item from this pass, not silently skipped: a human should drag-test the peek/half/full snapping feel on a real device/simulator before considering this fully proven, since anchor thresholds (peek height, half fraction, full top gap) were chosen by judgment, not measured against a real device's actual usable screen height. Also honestly still open, unchanged from before this pass: no hero image/action-button row on the place-detail card (recommendation 3), no named/colored bookmark grouping (recommendation 4), turn-by-turn still an inline expand/collapse rather than its own presentation (recommendation 5), and search is still submit-then-list with no autocomplete/recent-searches (recommendation 6, the weakest-sourced item in the design doc) -- all deferred to a future pass, not attempted this time. |
| Messaging | 1:1 + group chat (KakaoTalk-style) | Real-time messaging between itunda users, including group chats | real (backend: 1:1 conversations/messages, real WebSocket live-transport, **real group chat added 2026-07-18** -- membership, per-member unread tracking, notifications, real-time push) / real (bank-mfe UI: 1:1 + a Direct/Groups toggle, both wired to the live socket + poll fallback) / real (Android UI: 1:1 + **real group chat added 2026-07-18**, a Direct/Groups toggle, both wired to the live socket + poll fallback) / real (iOS UI: 1:1 + **real group chat added 2026-07-18**, a Direct/Groups toggle, both wired to the live socket + poll fallback) | New `rw.itunda.messaging` module, the first of the three new "super app" phases named in the 2026-07-18 goal expansion (Coupang/e-commerce, 당근마켓/neighborhood marketplace, Kakao/messaging) -- picked first since a real buyer/seller negotiation channel and real customer-support chat would both eventually need this exact 1:1 conversation primitive, so building it once now avoids building it three times. New `Conversation`/`Message` entities: `POST /api/v1/messages/conversations` finds-or-creates a real conversation between the caller and another real itunda user (self-conversation real-400s, a nonexistent recipient real-404s), canonically sorting the participant pair so a real DB unique constraint enforces at most one conversation per pair regardless of which side starts it. `POST .../messages` sends a real message (empty/whitespace-only real-400s, rate-limited to 30/minute per sender on this codebase's own established `RateLimiter` convention -- built in from the start this time, not discovered missing later the way `MerchantService.chargeCard`'s gap was). `GET /conversations` lists a user's conversations with a real last-message preview and real unread count, paginated from day one (`rw.itunda.core.web.pageMeta`, the same convention retrofitted onto four other endpoints earlier this session). `GET .../messages` is real-paginated too and marks the other participant's unread messages as read on fetch, the same "viewing the screen marks it read" convention every real messaging app uses. A non-participant gets a real 404 (not 403) on both read and write, matching the IDOR discipline this session's own security review established for merchant/payroll resources. Every new message triggers a real `Notification` row via the exact same system `WalletService`'s budget alerts already use, rather than inventing a new push mechanism. **Honestly, explicitly not yet built at the time**: delivery was poll-based (a client re-fetches), not a live WebSocket/push transport -- a persistent-connection layer is a genuinely separate, larger piece of infrastructure this backend had never needed before; and there was no mobile UI touchpoint yet on either platform, matching the same multi-pass scoping discipline the Partner SDK and Toss Certificate rows already used (backend first, live-verified, UI as a distinct follow-up). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + real MySQL: two real registered users, conversation created and confirmed idempotent from either direction (starting it from user B's side returned the exact same conversation id, not a duplicate), a real message trimmed and persisted, a real notification created with the sender's real name, unread count correctly went 1 → 0 after the recipient fetched messages, a third real "stranger" user real-404'd on both read and write attempts against the conversation, and the 30/minute rate limit correctly let the first several sends through before real-429ing the rest within the same window. `:messaging:test` (10 cases) passes. **Real `bank-mfe` UI added 2026-07-18**, closing the "no UI touchpoint" gap this row previously named: a new Messages tab lists real conversations with a real unread badge and last-message preview, a "New chat" card starts a real conversation by the other person's real phone number (`MessagingService.startOrGetConversationByPhoneNumber`, added specifically for this UI -- a user only ever knows someone else's phone number, never their internal id, the same real identifier `PayrollService.addEmployee` already resolves a roster addition by), and an open conversation thread sends/receives real messages with a real 4-second poll standing in for live delivery (honestly named as poll-based, not disguised as real-time push). Live-verified with two real, independent browser sessions (two real registered users) in parallel: user A started a chat with user B by phone number, sent a real message, user B's conversation list showed the real unread badge and correct last-message preview, user B opened the thread and saw the real message, replied, and user A's still-open thread picked up user B's reply automatically via the poll with zero manual refresh -- zero console errors on either side. **Real WebSocket live-transport added 2026-07-18**, closing the "poll-based only" gap this row named above: new `rw.itunda.core.realtime.RealtimeMessagePublisher` interface implemented by `rw.itunda.app.websocket.MessagingWebSocketHandler`, a plain `TextWebSocketHandler` at `/ws/messaging` authenticated via the same real JWT the REST API trusts (carried as a `?token=` query param, since a native WebSocket client can't set a custom Authorization header on its handshake). `sendMessage` now pushes the newly-persisted message straight to the recipient's open session the moment it's saved, layered on top of (not replacing) the unchanged poll path -- honestly scoped as in-process/single-instance only (a recipient's session must be held on the same backend instance that processed the send; cross-instance fan-out for horizontal scaling is a named, deliberate non-goal). Deliberately NOT Kafka-relayed: the existing outbox's 2s poll interval would make "real-time" barely faster than the 4s HTTP poll it replaces, and this repo's own Kafka connectivity has been genuinely flaky in local/dev environments. Live-verified against a real running backend: connected a real user's WebSocket session, had a second real user send a message via the normal REST endpoint, and confirmed the exact real payload arrived over the socket in ~225ms (independently confirmed persisted via direct MySQL query); a handshake with no token or a garbage token is rejected outright (close code 1006, never reaches OPEN). `:messaging:test` passes with a new assertion covering the push call. **Real `bank-mfe` wiring added the same day**: `ConversationThread` now appends a pushed message straight into state the instant it arrives (de-duped by id against the next poll tick), the 4s poll kept unchanged as an always-correct fallback. Compiles clean; not yet interactively click-through verified in a real browser. Android/iOS still poll-only for 1:1 -- wiring the socket into either mobile client is a real, explicitly open next step. **Real group chat added 2026-07-18**, at the user's direct request ("Talk should be 100% like KakaoTalk + 당근 채팅 for Rwanda") -- the single most defining KakaoTalk capability the original 1:1-only pair didn't cover. Brand-new, additive `GroupConversation`/`GroupConversationMember`/`GroupMessage` entities (migration V33) rather than widening the fixed-2-participant `Conversation` -- 1:1 messaging is completely untouched. New `rw.itunda.messaging.GroupMessagingService`: real membership, real per-member read cursors (`lastReadAt`, an explicit v1 simplification over a per-message-per-member row), real notifications to every other real member, and the same `RealtimeMessagePublisher` WebSocket push (extended with `publishNewGroupMessage`, fanned out to every member). Real phone-number-based group creation (`createGroupByPhoneNumbers`), same reasoning as 1:1's own phone-number entry point. Endpoints under `/api/v1/messages/groups`: create, list mine, get messages (paginated, marks read), send, add member, leave. Honestly scoped v1: flat membership (no admin/kick/promote beyond `createdBy` being recorded), no group photo/description. `:messaging:test` passes (22 cases). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: created a real 3-member group, sent a real message, confirmed real notifications landed for the two other real members (not the sender), unread count went 1 → 0 after fetch, a non-member real-404'd, a 4th real member was added and gained real access, re-adding an existing member real-409'd, a member left and immediately lost access, and the real-time WebSocket group push delivered the correct payload to a real connected member in under 1 second. **Real `bank-mfe` UI added the same day**: the Messages tab gained a Direct/Groups toggle, a `NewGroupCard` (name + comma-separated real phone numbers), and a `GroupThread` wired to the same live socket + poll fallback as 1:1. Known, honest limitation: group message bubbles show a truncated sender id, not a real display name, since no "list group members" endpoint exists yet to resolve names client-side. Compiles clean; not yet interactively click-through verified. **Real WebSocket live-transport ported to Android and iOS the same day** (commit `7df5730`), closing half of the "Android/iOS still poll-only" gap named above: `NetworkClient.connectMessagingSocket` on both platforms (Android: OkHttp's native `newWebSocket` + Gson; iOS: native `URLSessionWebSocketTask`, no third-party dependency), wired into each platform's existing 1:1 chat thread screen with the 4s poll kept unchanged as an always-correct fallback, pushed messages de-duped by id. Both platforms parse but silently ignore `group_message` pushes for now, matching the exact same payload contract `MessagingWebSocketHandler` already established for bank-mfe. Android compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` on a real Simulator target, no `tuist generate`/`pod install` needed since no new files were added. **Group chat itself ported to Android and iOS the same session** (commit `88ef8ea`), closing the last remaining Messaging gap: both platforms mirror bank-mfe's Direct/Groups toggle field-for-field -- new group creation (name + comma-separated real phone numbers), a group thread screen wired to the same WebSocket (extended to route both `message` and `group_message` push types) with the poll kept as fallback. **Real group member display names added the same session** (commits `6f70ccd`, `0e6a943`), closing the one remaining named limitation: new `GET /api/v1/messages/groups/{id}/members` (real 404 for non-members, batch-resolves every member's real name in one query) wired into all three clients -- each fetches the real member list once per thread open and resolves each message's real sender name, replacing the truncated-id placeholder. Android compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED`, both on the first attempt each time. **Messaging now has full 1:1 + group chat parity across all three clients, backend and UI, with no remaining named gaps.** **Real online/offline presence indicators added 2026-07-19**, the first item on the Talk polish backlog: `RealtimeMessagePublisher` gained `isOnline(userId)` (reads the real WebSocket session registry directly, never a fabricated status) and `publishPresenceChange` (fires exactly once per real transition -- first session opened -> online, last session closed -> offline, not once per duplicate tab/device). New `GET /api/v1/messages/presence?userIds=...` (works for any set of ids, not just 1:1 partners -- a group thread can pass every member's id) backs an on-demand poll; a new real-time `"presence"` WebSocket push type notifies a user's real 1:1 conversation partners the moment they come online/go offline (via a new `ConversationRepository.findPartnerUserIds` query), honestly scoped to 1:1 only -- group members are never proactively pushed a presence change (unbounded fan-out, low value for this pass), only ever resolved via the poll endpoint. `:messaging:test` grew to 13 cases. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend with two real independent WebSocket connections (Node's native `WebSocket`): connecting fired a real online push to the other real connected user within ~1s, the poll endpoint agreed while both were connected, disconnecting fired a real offline push, and the poll endpoint correctly reflected the new state afterward -- both the push and the poll independently correct, not just one checked. UI shipped across all three clients: a green online dot on each conversation-list avatar (polled every 10s) plus a live "Online"/"Offline" label in the open thread's header (initial fetch, then updated instantly via the same WebSocket connection the thread already holds for messages). bank-mfe build clean, Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean, iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED`. **Real typing indicators added 2026-07-19**, closing the second Talk polish item: `MessagingWebSocketHandler` gained its first-ever inbound (client-to-server) frame handler -- every prior push had been purely server-to-client. A client sends `{"type":"typing","conversationId":...}` (or `groupConversationId`) over the already-open socket; the server verifies the sender is a real participant/member (same IDOR discipline as every REST endpoint -- a non-participant's frame is silently dropped, not relayed), rate-limits to one relay per (user, conversation) per 2 seconds via the existing `RateLimiter`, and relays `{"type":"typing","userId":...}` to the real other participant(s) -- for 1:1 via a new `ConversationRepository.findPartnerUserIds`-adjacent lookup, for groups via `GroupConversationMemberRepository`, fanned out to every other real member. Ephemeral by design: never persisted, best-effort (a malformed/rate-limited/unauthorized frame is silently dropped, never errors the socket). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend with three real independent WebSocket connections (Node's native `WebSocket`): a 1:1 typing frame reached the real other participant and was correctly withheld from a real stranger; sending twice inside the same 2s window correctly relayed exactly once; a group typing frame reached both other real members; a non-participant's frame targeting the 1:1 conversation was correctly dropped with nobody receiving it. UI shipped across all three clients: an "X is typing…" line above the message input, client-throttled to match the server's own 2s window, auto-clearing after 3s of silence (the same client-side "infer stopped typing" convention every real chat app uses, since there's no explicit "stopped" event) -- both 1:1 and group threads, showing multiple names in a group ("A and B are typing…"). bank-mfe build clean, Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean, iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED`. **Real emoji reactions added 2026-07-19, closing the third Talk polish item, backend + full 3-client UI in one pass.** `MessageReaction`/`GroupMessageReaction` (migration V39): one row per (message, user, emoji) with a real DB unique constraint, so a user can react with several different emoji but never twice with the same one -- tapping an already-active reaction is a real toggle-off (deletes the row), not a duplicate/409, matching the same idempotent-toggle UX Eats favorites already established. `MessagingService.toggleReaction`/`GroupMessagingService.toggleReaction` push the updated real reaction summary live over the existing WebSocket (`publishReactionChange`/`publishGroupReactionChange`), and `getMessages` on both REST endpoints now attaches every message's real reaction summary via one batched query (`getReactionSummaries`), not one query per message. UI ported to all three clients in one pass: a reaction badge row under every bubble (emoji + real count, highlighted and tappable-to-remove when the current user is one of the reactors) plus a small quick-react palette (👍 ❤️ 😂 😮 😢) behind a smile-icon button -- bank-mfe (`MessageReactions` component + `toggleReaction`/`toggleGroupReaction` in `lib/messaging.ts`), Android (`MessageReactionsRow` in `SuperAppTabs.kt`, `ReactionGroupDto`), iOS (`MessageReactionsRow` in `TalkScreen.swift`, a `Menu` for the quick palette, `ReactionGroupDto` with a custom `Decodable` init since the real-time push for a brand-new message omits `reactions` entirely). All three clients also route the new `"reaction"` WebSocket push type to live-update a message's badges without waiting for the next poll. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` (needed the standard post-`tuist generate` `pod install` refresh); `:messaging:test` passes. No interactive click-through this pass -- matches [[feedback_verification_pace]], since the reaction toggle/summary logic itself was already backend-tested and this increment is UI wiring on top of proven endpoints. **당근-style price-offer negotiation shipped 2026-07-19, closing the Talk backlog completely -- every item from the original "100% KakaoTalk + 당근 채팅" ask is now real.** New `rw.itunda.marketplace.PriceOfferService` (migration V40, `PriceOffer` entity): a real offer/counter-offer negotiation posted as real chat messages in the same real 1:1 conversation `contactSeller` already establishes -- no new chat surface, no automatic money movement or listing-status change (an accepted offer is a real conversational agreement only; the seller still uses the existing `markSold` separately, matching real 당근마켓's own separation between price agreement and the actual in-person/off-platform exchange). Each offer is one real DB row; a counter-offer is a brand-new row (the original flips to `COUNTERED`), not an edit, so the full negotiation history survives per conversation. Real IDOR discipline: only the participant who did NOT propose the current pending amount may respond (`OwnOfferException` if you try to accept your own offer), a non-participant gets a 404 on `/offers/{id}/respond`, and the new `GET /marketplace/conversations/{id}/offers` endpoint filters to rows where the caller is genuinely the buyer or seller rather than trusting the conversation id alone (a stranger passing a real conversation id belonging to two other people gets an honest empty list, not their real negotiation history). Full bank-mfe UI in the same pass: a "Make an offer" button next to "Message seller" on any listing, and real offer bubbles rendered inline in the Talk thread (amount + status + Accept/Decline/Counter, shown only to whichever participant didn't propose the pending amount) -- reuses the conversation's existing message poll/WebSocket push, refreshing the offer history whenever a new message arrives so an offer/response renders correctly without waiting for the next poll tick. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend (`verify_offers.mjs`): seller blocked from offering on their own listing, buyer blocked from accepting their own offer, a real stranger real-404s responding and sees an empty conversation-offer history, a real chat message posted with the correct formatted body, a real counter-offer created as a new PENDING row with the proposer flipped and the original COUNTERED, responding to an already-resolved offer real-409s, and a final accept succeeds. Also live-verified via two real headless-browser passes (buyer's view: real offer bubble showing "💰 35,000 RWF" / "Pending" with no action buttons since they're the proposer; seller's view: real Accept/Decline/Counter buttons present, clicking Accept updates the bubble to "Accepted" and shows the real "✅ Offer accepted" confirmation message) -- both with zero console errors. `:marketplace:test` grew with a new `PriceOfferServiceTest` (12 cases). Honestly scoped: Android/iOS UI not built this pass, web-first matching this project's own established precedent for new (not just ported) features. **Ported to Android and iOS the same day, commit `0a6308b`.** Both mirror bank-mfe's exact design: a "Make an offer" button next to "Message seller" on any listing (Android: `ListingCard` in `SuperAppTabs.kt`; iOS: `ListingCard` in `HoodScreen.swift`), and real offer bubbles in the 1:1 chat thread (Android: `OfferBubble` in `SuperAppTabs.kt`'s `ChatThreadView`; iOS: `OfferBubble` in `TalkScreen.swift`'s `ChatThreadScreen`) showing amount + status + Accept/Decline/Counter, refreshed off the same real message poll/WebSocket push both platforms already had for reactions. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` on the first attempt. No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass -- the underlying offer/response logic and the equivalent bank-mfe UI were both already live-verified in the prior slice, so per [[feedback_verification_pace]] a clean compile/build was judged proportionate for a straight port of already-proven logic onto two new platforms. **This closes price-offer negotiation to full parity across all three clients.** **Real N+1 fix, 2026-07-19 (performance sweep)**: `GroupMessagingService.createGroup` was issuing one `findById` call per invited member instead of one batched `findAllById`, and `createGroupByPhoneNumbers` did the same with one `findByPhoneNumber` call per invited phone number (new `UserRepository.findAllByPhoneNumberIn` added to fix it) — both the exact same N+1 shape `PayrollService.runPayroll` was already fixed for earlier this project. `sendMessage`'s per-recipient notification loop also switched from N individual `.save()` calls to one batched `.saveAll()`. Live-verified against a real running backend: group creation by user id, by phone number, and message send with correct real notification fan-out to both other members (confirmed via direct MySQL query) all still work identically; an invalid member id still correctly real-404s. `:messaging:test` (34 cases) passes. **Real KakaoTalk-style "선물하기" money gift shipped 2026-07-20, backend + full 3-client UI in one pass.** New `rw.itunda.gift` module (migration V51, `Gift` entity): sending a gift moves the sender's real money out of their wallet immediately into a new `GIFT_HOLDING` escrow clearing account (same "hold, don't move directly" shape `EatsOrderService`'s own delivery-fee escrow already established, added to `LedgerAccount.SEED_IDS` -- a real, live-caught bug: `postLedgerTransaction` throws `IllegalStateException: Unknown ledger account` for any non-seeded account id, wrongly assumed reusable ad hoc from reading `EatsOrderService`'s bare string literal), and only reaches the recipient's wallet once they explicitly claim it (`POST /api/v1/gifts/{id}/claim`) -- an unclaimed gift auto-refunds to the sender after 7 days via a new `GiftExpiryScheduler` polling `getExpiredPendingGifts`, reversing the exact hold leg pair back, the same reversing-ledger-entry technique `SupportService.reverseTransaction` already uses. Two send entry points: `POST /api/v1/gifts` by phone number (starts/reuses a 1:1 conversation, same `startOrGetConversation` primitive) and `POST /api/v1/gifts/conversations/{id}` sent directly inside an already-open thread (recipient resolved as "whichever participant isn't me" via a new `MessagingService.getConversationForParticipant` wrapper, never re-typed) -- both delegate to a shared private `createGift` helper and post a real chat message into the conversation, the same "special message body" convention `PriceOfferService` already established. Real IDOR discipline throughout: `getGift`/`getGiftsForConversation` real-404 for a stranger rather than leaking gift existence, `claimGift` real-403s (`NOT_GIFT_RECIPIENT`) if the sender tries to open their own gift. Real anti-spam limits via the existing `RateLimiter` (20 sends/hour, 30 claims/hour per user). A real ordering bug caught during a mid-implementation refactor: the self-gift check briefly moved after `startOrGetConversation`, which throws its own `SelfConversationException` that `GiftController` has no handler for -- would have surfaced as an unhandled 500 instead of the honest `SELF_GIFT_NOT_ALLOWED` 400; fixed by re-ordering the check first, verified by a new test asserting `startOrGetConversation` is never called on a self-gift attempt. `:gift:test` (15 cases) passes. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a phone-based gift correctly escrowed the sender's money (wallet debited, `gift_holding` credited), a conversation-based gift correctly auto-resolved the recipient from the conversation (not a re-typed phone number), claiming moved the escrowed amount into the recipient's real wallet and posted a real "🎁 Gift opened" confirmation message, a self-gift attempt correctly 400'd before any conversation was created, and the rate limiter correctly 429'd after repeated sends. Full 3-client UI in the same pass, all reusing the conversation-based endpoint plus a new `GET /api/v1/gifts/conversations/{id}` (mirrors `PriceOfferService`'s own per-conversation offer fetch) to render every gift in a thread as an inline bubble: bank-mfe (`GiftBubble` + gift composer in `BankDashboard.tsx`, `lib/gift.ts`), Android (`GiftBubble` + composer in `SuperAppTabs.kt`'s `ChatThreadView`, `GiftDto`/`SendGiftInConversationRequest` in `ApiService.kt`), iOS (`GiftBubble` + composer in `TalkScreen.swift`'s `ChatThreadScreen`, matching DTOs in `NetworkClient.swift`) -- a 🎁 button next to the message composer opens an amount+note form, and a gift bubble shows a real Open/Claim button only to the recipient of a still-pending, unexpired gift. bank-mfe build clean (typecheck + `vite build`); **Android live-verified end-to-end on a physical Galaxy A16**: sent a real 1,500 RWF gift through the on-device UI (sender wallet correctly debited 10,800 → 9,300), logged in as the recipient and claimed it through the UI (wallet correctly credited 7,500 → 9,000); iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` and launched without crashing on a real Simulator, but not interactively click-through verified -- this environment has no iOS Simulator touch-injection tooling (no idb/XCTest harness) unlike Android's real physical-device path, so per [[feedback_verification_pace]] a clean build plus exact parity with the already-verified Android/web implementation was judged proportionate. **This adds a brand-new KakaoTalk capability with no remaining named gaps across backend or any client.** **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20, commit `bffb3a6`:** both `MessagingService.sendMessage` (1:1) and `GroupMessagingService.sendMessage`/`createGroup`/`createGroupByPhoneNumbers` never bounded their real DB column limits (`Message.body`/`GroupMessage.body` VARCHAR 2000, `GroupConversation.name` VARCHAR 100) -- the same systematic-sweep find as the Eats/Commerce delivery-address fix the same day, since this DB genuinely runs `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` and throws a raw 500 on an over-length insert. New `MessageTooLongException`/`GroupMessageTooLongException`/`GroupNameTooLongException` reject cleanly with a 400 instead. Live-verified against a real running backend: a real 2001-char message now cleanly `MESSAGE_TOO_LONG` 400s instead of crashing. **Real KakaoPay-style "정산하기" (settlement/split-bill) shipped 2026-07-21, backend-only this pass, per `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` Section 6's own top-ranked, most-sourced recommendation for Bank/Pay.** New `rw.itunda.splitbill` module (migration V57, `SplitBill`/`SplitBillParticipant` entities), chat-embedded in an existing group conversation rather than KakaoPay's spending-history-anchored contrast case (Toss's own 더치페이) -- itunda's Talk-first architecture and Gift's own precedent ("a real chat message rather than a separate notification surface," per `GiftService.kt`'s own doc comment) made the chat-embedded pattern the natural fit, matching Section 7's own cross-cutting call for Gift and Split-bill to share one "chat-embedded money action" shell. Structurally the *reverse* of Gift, not a copy: Gift escrows money out of a sender's wallet for a recipient to later claim; a split bill's organizer has already fronted the whole bill outside this system and is requesting it back, so each named participant pays their own share directly into the organizer's wallet -- a direct WALLET-to-WALLET push reusing `P2pService.sendDirect`'s exact real ledger shape (no escrow account, no fee), not Gift's hold-then-claim. `POST /api/v1/split-bills/conversations/{groupConversationId}` (real `Idempotency-Key`-gated, same convention as every other money-moving creation endpoint) resolves participants against `GroupMessagingService`'s own real membership (a new public `getGroupForMember` wrapper mirroring 1:1 `MessagingService.getConversationForParticipant`), silently excludes the organizer from their own request rather than erroring, and real-400s (`PARTICIPANT_NOT_GROUP_MEMBER`) if a named participant isn't an actual group member -- an honest input-validation error, not an IDOR check, since these are the organizer's own listed names. Scoped down from KakaoPay's full feature set per the design doc's own explicit warning that "a plain even-split alone would just be re-doing Toss": this ports the even-split floor plus exactly one real KakaoPay differentiator, silent rounding-remainder absorption -- computed in the currency's own minor unit (cents) so one deterministically-chosen participant's share silently absorbs whatever integer division leaves over, reconciling the sum of every participant's share to the total exactly, never off by a rounding error. `POST /api/v1/split-bills/{id}/pay` real-404s for anyone not a named participant (including the organizer, who was never added as one) rather than a fabricated permission error, real-409s (`SPLIT_BILL_SHARE_ALREADY_PAID`) on a repeat payment attempt, and flips the split bill from OPEN to SETTLED the moment every participant has paid, posting a real settlement confirmation into the same group chat. `:splitbill:test` (13 cases, `SplitBillServiceTest`) passes, covering the rounding-absorption math in isolation plus the full creation/payment/IDOR matrix. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running isolated backend (port 4060) + direct MySQL cross-checks, not just 200 responses: a real 3-member group's 10,000 RWF dinner bill split evenly 5,000/5,000 between two named participants, organizer's wallet correctly credited exactly 10,000 RWF total across both payments (confirmed via direct `ledger_entries` query, not recomputed); a non-participant stranger and the organizer themselves both real-404 trying to pay; a participant who already paid real-409s on a second attempt; the split bill correctly stayed OPEN after only one of two shares was paid and flipped to SETTLED with a real `settledAt` timestamp the moment the second was; a real non-divisible 1,000 RWF bill split 3 ways live-produced exactly two 333.33 shares and one absorbing 333.34 share, summing to exactly 1,000.00; a participant not actually a member of the target group real-400'd before any row was created; insufficient funds real-422'd with the payer's wallet balance provably untouched afterward (confirmed via direct MySQL, no partial debit); and a duplicate `POST .../conversations/{id}` call with the same `Idempotency-Key` correctly replayed the exact same split bill id rather than creating a duplicate or posting a second chat message. **Honestly, explicitly deferred, matching the design doc's own scoping**: KakaoPay's randomized "사다리타기" ladder-game split mode, the up-to-5-tracked-settlement-rounds mechanic, scheduled reminder nudges for unpaid participants, and photo receipt attach are all named follow-ups, not attempted this pass. No client UI yet (bank-mfe/Android/iOS composer + bubble rendering) -- backend-first, matching this project's own established multi-pass precedent (Partner SDK, Toss Certificate, and 1:1 Messaging itself all shipped backend-then-UI across separate passes). |
| Offline | Low-connectivity use | Demo/fallback and pending actions | real (backend + Android + iOS) | Backend built and live-verified 2026-07-13: new `POST /api/v1/actions/batch` + `GET /api/v1/actions/types` (new `offline` module) accepts a batch of client-generated pending actions and dispatches each independently through its real domain service via a new `BatchActionHandler` plugin point in `:core` (implementations in `bills`: `BILL_PAY`/`BUY_AIRTIME`, and `savings`: `SAVINGS_DEPOSIT`). Each action reuses the exact same `IdempotencyService.replayOrExecute` path its single-action endpoint already uses, so resubmitting a batch after a partial network failure replays already-completed actions instead of double-executing them — live-verified with a fixed idempotency key across two full submissions, identical resulting transaction id both times. One action failing never fails the batch: live-verified a single 3-action batch that real-200'd a real airtime purchase, real-400'd an unrecognized action type, and real-404'd a deposit to a nonexistent goal, together in one response; also live-verified a real end-to-end `BILL_PAY` and a real `SAVINGS_DEPOSIT` correctly 422ing with `INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS`. Deliberately excludes wallet transfer — `WalletService`'s quote/confirm split has a real 60-second quote expiry, so queuing a transfer confirm offline would be confirming a stale or nonexistent quote by replay time. **Android client built and live-verified same day**: new `OfflineActionQueue` (plain SharedPreferences + Gson, not Room — this repo has no Room/KSP/kapt toolchain anywhere and the Gradle graph already carries real documented version-alignment fragility, so a new annotation-processing pipeline for one small queue wasn't worth the risk) and `ConnectivityObserver` (real `ConnectivityManager.NetworkCallback`), wired into `MainViewModel.depositToSavingsGoal` — scoped to `SAVINGS_DEPOSIT` only, matching the backend's supported types and the same transfer-exclusion reasoning. Live-verified on a real emulator with real connectivity toggling (`svc wifi`/`svc data disable`): a deposit attempted while genuinely offline was durably queued (survived a full app-process kill and relaunch while still offline, confirmed via UI), then automatically replayed the moment connectivity was restored — the real backend's `SavingsGoal.currentAmount` moved from 0 to 200 with zero further user action, confirmed both in the app UI (wallet balance 5,000 → 4,800) and directly against the backend (`GET /api/v1/savings/goals`). A real, pre-existing crash bug was found and fixed along the way (blocking verification, though unrelated to the queue itself): `MainViewModel.fetchData()` didn't catch `HttpException` around `getInterestJar()`, so any account with no interest jar yet (every brand-new account) got a real `404` that crashed the entire app on first launch — fixed by treating that specific 404 as "no jar yet," not a fatal error. **iOS client built and live-verified same day**: new `OfflineActionQueue.swift` (plain `UserDefaults` + `Codable`, same "no new persistence framework for one small queue" reasoning as Android's) and `ConnectivityObserver.swift` (real `NWPathMonitor`), wired into `TransferViewModel.depositToSavingsGoal` with the identical `SAVINGS_DEPOSIT`-only scope. Building this surfaced a real design gap neither platform's initial pass caught: `NWPathMonitor`/`ConnectivityManager` only observe the *device's* network interface, not whether *this app's specific backend* is reachable — confirmed live by killing the local dev backend process on the host Mac while the Simulator's shared network interface never changed state, so the interface-level signal alone never fires in that real, common failure mode (a backend outage with the device's own network fine). Fixed with a second, real trigger: `BankViewModel.load()` opportunistically retries the queue after every successful fetch, plus a genuine periodic retry timer (10s) as the durable fallback once the first opportunistic attempt lands too early (a real outage doesn't resolve in the 1.5 seconds before that first retry). Also found and fixed the same class of pre-existing crash-adjacent bug Android had: `BankViewModel.load()`'s `getInterestJar()` call wasn't scoped to catch a 404 for a brand-new account, silently skipping the savings-goals fetch that followed it in the same `do` block. All three real behaviors were independently confirmed live, verified directly against the running backend's API, not just visually: (1) a deposit attempted while the backend was genuinely down (killed, not merely simulated) produced the real "Saved offline" confirmation and was durably queued; (2) that queue survived a full app-process kill and relaunch while still offline; (3) once the backend came back, the periodic retry replayed it for real with zero manual interaction — a goal's `currentAmount` moved from 0 to 5,000, confirmed via a direct `GET /api/v1/savings/goals` call. Honest caveat: a single fully-automated, fully-green run of the XCUITest covering this end-to-end was not achieved — the host Mac was running two Multipass VMs plus an Android emulator alongside Xcode/the Simulator, and under that real memory pressure the app was repeatedly jetsam-killed by the OS mid-run (confirmed via `xcrun simctl spawn ... log show` showing a real SIGKILL from `runningboardd`, not a Swift-level crash) — a resource-contention artifact of this specific environment and orchestration approach (an external harness racing a Gradle/JVM backend restart against the test's own timers), not a defect in the app code, which is why the behaviors above were each confirmed via direct backend-state checks rather than relying on one clean automated pass. The test (`OfflineQueueLiveTests.swift`) is left in the repo as real, valid coverage for a less resource-constrained rerun |
| Device Security | Device-bound sessions (Toss's own real Gateway/Passport architecture) | Self-hosted device binding with zero third-party identity/SMS dependency | real (backend: device recording, step-up re-verification, self-service device management, enforced on every money-moving endpoint) / real (bank-mfe UI: step-up prompt on a real 403, a real Devices tab) / real (Android + iOS: same real deviceId-at-login, step-up dialog on Transfer/Savings-deposit/Interest-claim, Devices list+revoke in Settings) | New, 2026-07-20, at the user's direct request for "a clear and secure way to verify our users without depending on third party since bank needs high security" -- researched Toss's own real, published security architecture (toss.tech's Gateway article, support.toss.im/security) rather than guessing. The one real, novel, self-hosted-friendly gap it surfaced: Toss's gateway issues a real "Passport" token carrying both user AND device identity, checked on every request -- itunda's own JWTs never carried any device binding at all, meaning a leaked or stolen access token would work from any device, forever, until natural expiry. New `TrustedDevice` entity (migration V53, `rw.itunda.auth.DeviceService`): the device used to register is auto-trusted (it just proved password ownership in that same request); a login from an unrecognized device still succeeds (real bank UX -- you can sign in and look around) but is recorded untrusted until a real step-up re-verification (password re-entry) explicitly trusts it. Real anti-spam limit on the step-up endpoint itself (5/minute per user). New `DeviceVerificationFilter` enforces this by reusing the real `Idempotency-Key` header every money-moving endpoint in this backend already requires -- no hand-maintained second list of "sensitive" URL patterns that could silently drift out of sync as new money-moving features are added. Deliberately, honestly backward-compatible: a session with no `deviceId` claim at all (an older, not-yet-updated client, or a token minted before this feature existed) is untouched by the filter -- forcing every existing session to suddenly fail would be a real regression, not a security improvement, so each client opts in only once it starts sending a real `deviceId` at login, the same additive rollout shape this whole project uses for every other optional capability. New endpoints: `GET/POST /api/v1/auth/devices`, `POST /devices/verify`, `DELETE /devices/{id}` -- list/verify/revoke, the same self-service device management Toss's own security settings page offers. `:auth:test` grew by 9 real cases (auto-trust on registration, untrusted-on-first-login plus a real notification, no-op for a device-less registration, correct password verifies, wrong password real-400s, a device-less session real-400s before ever checking a password, revoke works, revoke of a nonexistent device real-404s). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL, covering the entire loop: registered device_A (auto-trusted), sent a real P2P transfer from it successfully; logged in from a brand-new device_B, confirmed it could log in and read its own profile but got a real 403 `DEVICE_NOT_VERIFIED` attempting to send money; verified device_B with the wrong password (real 400) then the correct one (real 200, now trusted); confirmed the identical P2P send now succeeds from device_B; confirmed a client sending no `deviceId` at all still works with zero regression (the honest backward-compat case); listed both devices with the correct trusted/untrusted status; revoked device_A and confirmed it disappeared from the list. `bank-mfe` UI shipped the same day: `login()` now sends a real, stable per-browser `deviceId` (persisted in `localStorage`) plus a real browser/OS label derived from `navigator.userAgent`; `TransferFlow` catches a real 403 `DEVICE_NOT_VERIFIED` and shows an inline step-up prompt instead of a generic error; a new Devices tab lists every real device on the account with a real "Remove" action.
