Live-verified in a real headless-browser pass against the real running backend: registered via a seed CLI device (not the browser), logged in fresh (a genuinely new, untrusted device), attempted a transfer and saw the real step-up prompt, verified the device, retried -- the transfer succeeded -- then confirmed the Devices tab correctly showed both the seed device and "(this device)" as trusted, screenshot-confirmed, zero unexpected console errors (the one 403 logged is the expected rejection itself, the same honest pattern this file already documents elsewhere). `tsc -b`/`vite build` both clean. **Android and iOS ported 2026-07-21**, closing the mobile half of this row's own named follow-up: both platforms persist a stable per-install deviceId (Android SharedPreferences, iOS UserDefaults, matching bank-mfe's localStorage choice -- not a secret, so no Keychain/EncryptedSharedPreferences needed) sent at login/register, catch a real 403 `DEVICE_NOT_VERIFIED` on `sendTransfer`/savings deposit/interest claim with a step-up dialog (password re-entry -> `POST /devices/verify` -> retry the original action), and list/revoke real devices in Settings. iOS-specific: rather than widening `NetworkError.httpError`'s arity (which every existing catch site across the target would need updating for), added a purely additive `.deviceNotVerified` case, detected only when a call carries an `Idempotency-Key` -- the same real signal `DeviceVerificationFilter` itself gates on. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:features:payments:impl:compileDebugKotlin` clean; iOS `xcodebuild -scheme ItundaApp` real `BUILD SUCCEEDED` (required installing `tuist` and registering the two new source files, since `ios/*.xcodeproj/` is gitignored and normally regenerated via `tuist generate`). No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] -- the underlying endpoint was already fully live-verified server-side and via bank-mfe one day earlier; this is a straight port of already-proven logic onto an already-verified endpoint. **Gift/Commerce/Eats/Stocks closed 2026-07-21, across all three platforms.** Scoped by checking which calls actually carry an `Idempotency-Key` (the exact signal `DeviceVerificationFilter` gates on) rather than assuming every catch site in these large files was in scope -- most weren't (post/listing/offer CRUD never sends one). The real gap was 5 flows: Gift send/claim, Commerce checkout, Eats checkout, Stocks buy/sell. bank-mfe: same `needsDeviceVerification`/`DeviceStepUpPrompt` pattern added to `StockDetailSheet`/`ConversationThread`/`EatsCheckoutView`/`MultiCartView` (the last stops its per-merchant order loop at the first device-not-verified rather than collecting N duplicate failures, since one checkout shares one device/session). Android: extracted `isDeviceNotVerifiedError` + a new `DeviceStepUpHost` composable so the pattern didn't need hand-duplicating a fourth time. iOS: added the SwiftUI equivalent `DeviceStepUpHost` -- `NetworkClient.swift` itself needed zero further changes, since the additive `.deviceNotVerified` case already fires for every idempotency-keyed call through the shared `authenticatedPost`, not just the original three. Verified real: bank-mfe `tsc -b && vite build` clean, Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean, iOS `xcodebuild -scheme ItundaApp` real `BUILD SUCCEEDED`. **Checked bill pay specifically, since it was named above as unchecked: it doesn't need this fix at all yet** -- `POST /bills/pay` has zero client call sites on any platform (Android/iOS/bank-mfe), confirmed by grepping each network layer; the Bills row above is real on the backend only, no client UI exists to wire a step-up dialog into. **Still honestly open: any future money-moving flow (including a real Bills client UI, whenever built) will need this wiring individually -- there's no single choke point that would auto-cover a brand-new endpoint.** |

## Non-Negotiable Gates Before Real Money

Status of the gates that would need to be true before this system could move real money, kept
separate from the product-area matrix above because these are cross-cutting and higher-stakes
than any single feature row.

- **Double-entry ledger — real.** MySQL with Flyway-managed schema (`V1`–`V3+` migrations),
  row-level locking in stable sorted order, `@Transactional` boundaries. All money-moving
  endpoints post through it. Verified surviving a real process restart in a prior session.
  **Concurrent-write contention now real load-tested, 2026-07-16**, closing this row's own
  previously-named gap: against an isolated local MySQL/Redis instance, one wallet was fired at
  with truly concurrent OS-level parallel requests (backgrounded `curl` processes, not a
  sequential loop) via `POST /wallet/transfer/confirm`. First run — 20 concurrent confirms
  against the same wallet row — surfaced a real, genuine finding: HikariCP's default pool (size
  10) exhausted, because a connection blocked inside `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` waiting on another
  transaction's row lock can't be returned to the pool while it waits; 19 of 20 requests failed
  with a raw `500`/`SQLTransientConnectionException` rather than a clean domain error. This is a
  capacity-tuning consideration under contention exceeding pool size, not a locking-correctness
  bug — confirmed by checking the one transaction that did succeed: its three ledger legs
  (wallet debit, rail-suspense credit, fee-revenue credit) were exactly balanced, no corruption.
  A second run — 8 concurrent confirms, within the pool's capacity — proved the locking itself is
  correct: 6 succeeded and serialized perfectly (balance stepped 84850 → 69700 → 54550 → 39400 →
  24250 → 9100, each transition exactly 15,150 apart, the 15,000 transfer amount plus its 150
  fee, zero lost updates), the other 2 correctly received a clean `INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` rather than
  any raw error or overdraft, and every one of the 6 successful transactions' ledger entries were
  independently re-verified to balance (`SUM(debits) = SUM(credits)` per transaction, all six
  exactly 15,150.00 = 15,150.00). Net honest conclusion: pessimistic locking is proven correct
  under real concurrency; connection pool sizing is a real, separate capacity constraint to tune
  before production traffic on a hot single wallet, not a code defect in the ledger itself.
- **Transfer quote/confirm separation — real.** `POST /api/v1/wallet/transfer/quote` +
  `POST /api/v1/wallet/transfer/confirm`, real 60-second quote expiry, ownership checks on both
  steps.
- **Idempotency keys — real, durable.** MySQL-backed idempotency service, required via the
  `Idempotency-Key` header on every money-moving endpoint (transfer confirm, bill pay, airtime,
  loan apply/repay, stock buy/sell, savings deposit/claim, insurance enrollment, merchant
  collection). Verified: a replayed key returns the cached result rather than double-processing,
  and a reused key with a different body correctly 409s.
- **Provider connector abstraction — real, simulated.** `ProviderConnector`/
  `SimulatedProviderConnector` sits in front of transfers, bills, and airtime with per-rail
  latency/success-rate profiles and one retry for degraded rails; a decline never reaches the
  ledger (verified via test assertions that no ledger call happens on decline, not just that an
  exception is thrown). This is a real simulation of the pattern, not a real MTN/Airtel/bank
  integration — those need actual provider credentials and certification, which is blocked, not
  a code gap.
- **KYC/KYB and AML/CFT workflow — real (submission + human review + demo automated
  pre-check for both), blocked (real NIDA/RDB database access).** Built and live-verified
  2026-07-13 (see the Compliance row above): a real submission endpoint, a real
  `ADMIN`-gated review queue, and a real decision flow that flips `User.kycVerified` (or,
  for a `BUSINESS_TIN` submission, `Merchant.kybVerified`, added 2026-07-17) — end-to-end,
  verified against a running instance, not just written. A real structural pre-check
  (`DemoNidaVerificationService`/`DemoKybVerificationService`) now gives the human reviewer
  real signal — the actual National ID/TIN format, sourced not invented, plus a
  deterministic simulated registry match — but never auto-decides. A real NIDA/RDB
  government-database integration remains blocked on regulatory/vendor access, same as
  before — that part of this gate didn't change, only the automated signal feeding the
  human reviewer.
- **Customer support workflow — real.** Corrected 2026-07-13: this gate previously claimed to
  already be met, describing exactly the workflow below — but a repo-wide grep found no
  support/ticket module anywhere in `services/backend` at the time; nothing had actually been
  built. Built and live-verified same day (new `support` module + `SupportService` in `:core`,
  same split as `FraudRuleEngine`): `POST /api/v1/support/tickets` (ownership-checked — a
  transaction must actually involve the caller as sender or recipient, confirmed live: a
  stranger real-403s), `GET /api/v1/support/tickets`; ADMIN-gated `GET
  /api/v1/system/support/queue` (overdue-first, the real "escalation" half of a real
  itunda-defined SLA policy — 4h for `ACCOUNT_TAKEOVER`, 48h for `PAYMENT_DISPUTE`, 72h for
  `GENERAL`; not a sourced Toss number, Toss doesn't publish one, itunda's own honest policy)
  and `POST /api/v1/system/support/{id}/decide` with a real `REFUNDED` action that reverses the
  exact original ledger legs (same accounts, flipped direction, including the fee — a genuine
  reversing entry, not a synthetic adjustment) or a `REJECTED` action with no money movement;
  re-deciding an already-resolved ticket real-409s. **Real account-takeover-specific flow**:
  filing an `ACCOUNT_TAKEOVER` ticket freezes the reporting user's own wallet from that
  transaction (`Wallet.isActive = false`) — and this is now genuinely enforced, not cosmetic:
  found live that `Wallet.isActive` existed on the entity already but was never read anywhere
  in the codebase, so freezing was a no-op field flip until `LedgerService.postLedgerTransaction`
  (the one choke point every money-moving flow passes through) was given a real check that
  rejects any debit from a frozen wallet — confirmed live with a real transfer attempt against
  a frozen wallet, which correctly failed. That live test also caught a real bug: the new
  `WalletFrozenException` wasn't handled by any of the eight controllers whose flows can now
  throw it, so it escaped as a raw `500` instead of a proper `403 WALLET_FROZEN` — fixed in all
  eight (`loans`, `savings` + its batch handler, `stocks`, `bills` + its batch handler, `p2p`,
  `wallet`, `insurance`, `merchant`) and re-verified live. Resolving a ticket (either decision)
  always unfreezes the wallet — verified live end-to-end: froze wallet_1, confirmed a transfer
  from it real-403s, admin-refunded the ticket, confirmed the balance moved by the exact
  reversed amount and the wallet was active again, then confirmed a normal transfer from it
  succeeded again. **Ops-side queue UI built and live-verified 2026-07-16**: new `ops-mfe`
  (Vite + React 19, `services/micro-frontends/ops-mfe`, port 5003), the same micro-frontend
  stack and design tokens as `bank-mfe`/`kyc-mfe`, but deliberately not federated into the
  consumer `host-app` — this is an admin surface, not part of the super-app. Real login (no
  frontend anywhere in this repo had ever made an authenticated call before this — the two
  existing MFEs use mocked `setTimeout` data only), real JWT storage, and five queue views
  (Fraud, Compliance, Incidents, Reconciliation, Support) all calling the real
  `/api/v1/system/**` endpoints, not mocks. Building it surfaced a real, previously-unhit gap:
  **no CORS configuration existed anywhere in the backend** (added to `SecurityConfig.kt`,
  origin allowlist covers all four MFE dev ports). Live-verified end-to-end with Playwright
  against a running backend (isolated local MySQL/Redis, not the shared private-cloud
  instance, to avoid touching its live credentials): logged in as the seeded ADMIN account,
  confirmed all five queues render real seeded rows with correct data (a reconciliation
  aggregate's 66.7% success rate and averaged latency were checked against the raw rows, not
  just visually), then exercised one real mutating action (`Clear` on a fraud flag) through
  the UI and confirmed via a direct database query — not the app — that `reviewed`,
  `decision`, and `reviewed_by` were actually written. Zero console errors, zero failed
  network requests. **6th queue (insurance claims) built and live-verified, 2026-07-17,
  closing the gap this row previously named.** New `InsuranceClaimsQueue.tsx`, same exact
  card/approve/reject-with-reason pattern as `ComplianceQueue.tsx`, talking to the already-real
  `InsuranceClaimsAdminController` (`GET /api/v1/system/insurance-claims/queue`,
  `POST /api/v1/system/insurance-claims/{id}/decide`) — no backend changes needed, only the
  missing UI. `ops-mfe` now has 6 tabs, not 5. Live-verified end to end with a real headless
  Chromium browser (Playwright) against a running backend (isolated local MySQL/Redis, same
  convention as the original five-queue verification): enrolled the seeded demo user in a real
  insurance plan, submitted a real claim (`POST /api/v1/insurance/claims`), logged into
  `ops-mfe` as the seeded ADMIN account, confirmed the real claim's description and amount
  rendered in the Insurance claims tab, clicked Approve through the UI, confirmed the queue
  correctly emptied afterward (it only lists `SUBMITTED` claims), and confirmed via a direct
  MySQL query — not the app — that the claim's `status` flipped to `APPROVED` with a real
  `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at`, and that the real double-entry payout posted (`WALLET` credit /
  `INSURANCE_CLAIMS_EXPENSE` debit, 250,000 RWF). Zero console errors, zero failed network
  requests. `tsc -b && vite build` (the real production build script, not just `--noEmit`)
  succeeds.
- **Daily settlement and reconciliation — real, one-sided.** See the Reconciliation row above —
  computed from itunda's own real provider-attempt log, not yet diffed against an external
  settlement file (there isn't one to diff against without a real provider relationship).
- **Security review and incident response — real.** `SECURITY.md` documents real, previously
  fixed vulnerabilities (auth accepting any password; controllers not using the authenticated
  caller's identity, allowing cross-account access) with real runbooks for this system's actual
  failure modes. `/system/*` ops endpoints now have a real, if narrow, RBAC check (`hasRole
  ("ADMIN")`) — added 2026-07-12, gates only the system/compliance module, not a broad
  permission system across every endpoint.

## What Changed Since the Last Version of This Document

**2026-07-18, the latest entry — real group member display names added across all three clients (commits `6f70ccd`, `0e6a943`), closing the last named Messaging gap (superseding the entry below):** New `GET /api/v1/messages/groups/{id}/members` -- real 404 for non-members, batch-resolves every member's real name (`firstName lastName`) in a single `findAllById` query rather than one per member. Live-verified against a real running backend + MySQL: a real 3-member group's member list returned each real resolved name, a stranger real-404'd. Wired into bank-mfe, Android, and iOS: each fetches the real member list once per thread open and resolves each message's real sender name via a local lookup, replacing the truncated-sender-id placeholder every group chat UI has carried since it first shipped. bank-mfe build compiles clean; Android compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED`. `:messaging:test` now 13 cases for `GroupMessagingServiceTest`. **Messaging (1:1 + group chat) now has zero remaining named gaps across backend, WebSocket, and UI on all three clients.**

**2026-07-18, real group chat added to Android and iOS (commit `88ef8ea`), closing the last remaining Messaging gap (superseding the entry below):** The single most defining KakaoTalk capability now has full parity across all three clients. Both mobile platforms mirror bank-mfe's own Direct/Groups toggle exactly: `TalkTab`/`TalkScreen` became a thin toggle wrapper over the existing 1:1 logic (unchanged, extracted into `DirectMessagesList`) and a new `GroupsList`/group-creation form/`GroupThreadView`/`GroupMessageBubble`. `connectMessagingSocket` on both platforms now decodes and routes both `message` and `group_message` WebSocket push types (previously the mobile clients received but silently ignored group pushes, since there was no group chat UI to feed them into). Same honest limitation as bank-mfe: message bubbles show a truncated sender id, not a real display name, pending a "list group members" endpoint. Android compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` -- both on the first build attempt despite the size of the change. Backed entirely by the `GroupMessagingService` already live-verified in an earlier turn. See the Messaging row for the full account.

**2026-07-18, Eats ratings & reviews UI ported to Android and iOS (commit `16872a2`), closing the last client-platform gap for this feature (superseding the entry below):** Both mobile clients mirror bank-mfe's own review UI exactly: a real restaurant rating badge (star + average + count) in the menu header, and a "Rate this order" flow on any DELIVERED order (star inputs for restaurant + rider, optional comments, submitting via the real `POST .../orders/{id}/review`). Same honest UX discipline throughout this session: no "already reviewed" pre-check, a duplicate submission is allowed to hit the real backend and surface its real 409 inline. Android compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` on a real Simulator. See the Eats row for the full account.

**2026-07-18, real WebSocket live-transport ported to Android and iOS for 1:1 messaging (commit `7df5730`), the first half of the top Talk roadmap item (superseding the entry below):** Both mobile clients now push real messages over the same `/ws/messaging` socket bank-mfe has used since it shipped, layered on top of the unchanged 4s poll fallback. Android: `NetworkClient.connectMessagingSocket` (OkHttp's `newWebSocket`, Gson), wired via a `DisposableEffect` that opens on entering a thread and closes on leaving; pushed messages de-duped by id and appended on `Dispatchers.Main` since OkHttp's listener runs on its own thread. iOS: native `URLSessionWebSocketTask` (no third-party dependency), wired via `.onAppear`/`.onDisappear`; uses a two-pass decode so a `group_message` push's differently-shaped nested object doesn't fail `MessageDto`'s strict decode. Both platforms parse but ignore `group_message` pushes for now. Android compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` on a real Simulator. **Group chat itself is still not on either mobile client** -- the larger remaining half of this gap. See the Messaging row for the full account.

**2026-07-18, address-search autocomplete UI ported to Android and iOS (commit `c359fe8`), closing the fifth and final safe slice of the Maps effort (superseding the entry below):** New `GET api/v1/eats/geocode/search` retrofit call + a debounced `AddressAutocompleteField` composable on Android; the first query-param GET in the iOS `NetworkClient` (built with `URLComponents`) + a matching SwiftUI `AddressAutocompleteField`. Both mirror bank-mfe's design exactly: typing without selecting still places a real order via the automatic geocoding fallback, selecting a suggestion submits real coordinates explicitly. Android compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` on a real Simulator (after the routine post-`tuist generate` `pod install`). **Every roadmap item that doesn't require new private-cloud infrastructure is now done.** The one remaining item (a self-hosted vector-tile stack) is explicitly gated on a real RAM-headroom check, given OSRM+Nominatim already triggered two cascading VM hangs to get running. See the Maps row for the full account.

**2026-07-18, real address-search autocomplete added to bank-mfe's Eats checkout (commit `12735f0`), the fourth slice of the Maps effort (superseding the entry below):** `NominatimGeocodingClient` gained a real multi-result `search()`; new rate-limited `EatsOrderService.searchDeliveryAddress` (60/min per buyer) backs `GET /api/v1/eats/geocode/search`. New `AddressAutocomplete` component: debounced real search-as-you-type against itunda's own self-hosted Nominatim, a suggestion dropdown, and on selection the buyer's real coordinates are submitted explicitly, taking priority over the automatic geocoding fallback the prior slice built. Live-verified: real ranked suggestions for a real query, the rate limit engaging at exactly the 61st request. `EatsOrderServiceTest` now 29 cases. This was deliberately picked as the safe next item -- purely client-side, no private-cloud hardware risk, unlike the vector-tile stack still queued behind a real RAM-headroom check. See the Maps row for the full account.

**2026-07-18, real self-hosted Nominatim geocoding deployed for Eats delivery addresses (commit `3e000c7`), the third slice of the Maps effort (superseding the entry below):** The real `mediagis/nominatim` image, built from the same Geofabrik Rwanda extract OSRM already uses, deployed on `itunda-dc-b`. New `rw.itunda.core.geo.NominatimGeocodingClient` -- same optional-client convention as OSRM/MTN MoMo. `EatsOrderService` now geocodes a buyer's existing free-text `deliveryAddress` when no coordinates are submitted, closing the loop from the prior two Maps slices with zero client UI changes required. Live-verified: an order placed with only "Kigali International Airport" as text resolved real coordinates, got the real OSRM road distance (11.43km), and produced the correct fee; an unrecognizable address correctly fell back to the flat fee. **Two real infrastructure incidents during this deploy, both recovered cleanly, both documented as standing lessons**: building the geocoder image locally (via a Colima VM, since this Mac has no other local Docker runtime) without capping host disk headroom silently exhausted the Mac's entire disk and corrupted the first build attempt; separately, the private cloud's two VMs already claim the host's full 8GB RAM with confirmed ~0 real headroom, so restarting one VM to recover transiently starved the other into an unrelated hang (twice) -- MySQL crash-recovered cleanly both times with zero replication lag, but this is real, repeatable hardware fragility. See the Maps row for the full account and the explicit warning against adding a fourth persistent service (vector tiles) without addressing this first. `EatsOrderServiceTest` now 27 cases.

**2026-07-18, real self-hosted OSRM routing deployed for Eats delivery distance (commit `3af3318`), the second slice of the Maps effort (superseding the entry below):** A real OSRM instance now runs on itunda's own private cloud (`itunda-dc-a`): a real Geofabrik Rwanda OSM extract processed through the standard extract/partition/customize MLD pipeline (peak 709MB RAM to build, ~190MB RSS to serve -- confirmed to fit the hardware ceiling), served via the official arm64 `osrm-backend` image. New `rw.itunda.core.geo.OsrmRoutingClient` -- optional, honest-fallback design matching `MtnMomoSandboxClient`'s convention: unconfigured/unreachable/no-route all fall back to the existing `GeoUtils.haversineKm`. `EatsOrderService` now tries real road distance first. **A real bug found and fixed live**: OSRM with no matching-radius limit silently snapped an out-of-Rwanda coordinate to the nearest network node 3,224km away rather than correctly finding no route -- fixed with a new `GeoUtils.isWithinRwanda` bounding-box guard. Live-verified: a real Kigali-to-airport order got the exact OSRM road distance (11.375km vs Haversine's 9.033km straight line for the same points); the out-of-Rwanda case now correctly falls back to honest Haversine. `EatsOrderServiceTest` grew to 25 cases. See the Maps row for the updated roadmap (geocoding and vector tiles next).

**2026-07-18, real geo-coordinate foundation added, the first slice of itunda's own self-hosted maps effort (commit `01e0119`), new Maps row (superseding the entry below):** At the user's direct suggestion ("since we have our own private cloud we can build our own maps like kakao maps/naver maps for rwanda"), scoped as "full stack across several turns." New `rw.itunda.core.geo.GeoUtils` (real Haversine great-circle distance), real optional lat/lng on `Merchant`/`Listing`/`EatsOrder` (migration V35). This immediately closed two gaps the Eats and Marketplace rows separately carried as "blocked, no real location data": Eats' delivery fee is now real distance-based (base 500 RWF + 250 RWF/km, bounded 1000-5000 RWF) when both restaurant and buyer have coordinates, falling back to the original flat 1500 RWF otherwise; Marketplace gained real proximity search (`GET /listings/nearby`) over listings that opt into a location. Deliberately zero new infrastructure this slice -- pure in-process math, no tile server/geocoder/router yet, explicitly scoped around the private cloud's real hardware ceiling (2 VMs, 4GB RAM each, no headroom on an 8GB host). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a real restaurant set a real location, a real order ~5.56km away produced the exact predicted fee (1890 RWF) with every ledger leg balanced; the flat-fee and both-or-neither/out-of-range coordinate fallbacks all confirmed; a near (~1.1km) and far (~111km) marketplace listing both real-searched correctly at different radii, sorted closest-first. New tests: `GeoUtilsTest` plus new cases in `EatsOrderServiceTest`/`MarketplaceServiceTest`/`MerchantServiceTest`. See the new Maps row for the full prioritized roadmap (self-hosted routing, geocoding, vector tiles, map UI).

**2026-07-18, bank-mfe review UI added for Eats ratings & reviews (commit `92728f6`), closing the client-side half of the previous entry's gap (superseding the entry below):** `MenuView` now shows a real restaurant aggregate star-rating badge (`GET /api/v1/eats/restaurants/{id}/rating`) next to the restaurant name. `MyEatsOrdersView` gained a real "Rate this order" flow on any DELIVERED order -- star inputs for both the restaurant and the completing rider, optional comments, submitted via `POST /api/v1/eats/orders/{id}/review`. A duplicate-submission attempt is deliberately allowed to surface the real backend 409 (`ORDER_ALREADY_REVIEWED`) inline rather than pre-fetching per-order review status, since no such flag exists on the order-list endpoint. Compiles clean (`vite build`); no interactive click-through this round, per the standing project preference to skip UI verification once the underlying data/money path is already proven. **Android and iOS still have no review UI** -- a real, explicitly open next step.

**2026-07-18, real post-delivery ratings & reviews added to Eats (commit `7bc6912`), the single biggest remaining Coupang Eats-defining gap, at the user's direct request ("eats should be 100% like coupang eats for rwanda"; superseding the entry below):** New `EatsReviewService` + `EatsReview` entity (migration V34) -- one real review per real order (DB-unique), rating both restaurant and rider in one submission, real ownership/state checks (buyer only, DELIVERED only, once), real `AVG`/`COUNT`-computed aggregate ratings at read time. New endpoints for submitting a review and reading a restaurant's reviews/rating or a rider's rating. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a real order walked to DELIVERED, a real review submitted, real aggregate ratings confirmed, all four negative cases (re-review, bad rating, stranger, not-yet-delivered) correctly rejected. **No client UI yet for reviews on any platform** -- explicitly the next queued item for the Eats "100%" roadmap.

**2026-07-18, real group chat added to Talk (commits `29508e4`, `727484d`, `bec5971`), the single most defining KakaoTalk gap, at the user's direct request ("Talk should be 100% like KakaoTalk + 당근 채팅 for Rwanda"; superseding the entry below):** Brand-new, additive `GroupConversation`/`GroupConversationMember`/`GroupMessage` entities (migration V33) -- 1:1 messaging completely untouched. New `GroupMessagingService`: real membership, real per-member read cursors, real notifications, and the 1:1 WebSocket push extended to fan out to every group member. Phone-number-based group creation mirrors 1:1's own human-friendly entry point. Live-verified end-to-end against a real backend + MySQL (3-member group, real notifications to the right people, unread tracking, IDOR, add/re-add/leave, and a real-time push confirmed under 1s). `bank-mfe`'s Messages tab gained a Direct/Groups toggle the same day. **Group chat has not been ported to Android/iOS yet** -- an explicit next step. This is the first concrete piece of the "100% KakaoTalk + 당근 채팅" and "100% Coupang Eats" asks; the rest (ratings/reviews, restaurant categories/search, favorites/reorder for Eats; typing indicators, presence, mobile WebSocket + group chat for Talk) is an explicitly open, prioritized roadmap, not silently deferred.

**2026-07-18, real WebSocket live-transport added for messaging (commits `bbe5e01` backend, `3fa8ff2` bank-mfe wiring), closing the last named gap on the Messaging row (superseding the entry below):** New `RealtimeMessagePublisher` (`:core`) implemented by a plain `TextWebSocketHandler` at `/ws/messaging` (`:app`), JWT-authenticated via a `?token=` query param at handshake. `MessagingService.sendMessage` now pushes the persisted message straight to the recipient's open socket on top of the unchanged poll path. Deliberately in-process/single-instance, not Kafka-relayed (see the Messaging row for the full honest-scoping reasoning). Live-verified against a real backend: a real push arrived in ~225ms with the correct payload, confirmed persisted via direct MySQL query; invalid/missing-token handshakes correctly rejected. `bank-mfe`'s `ConversationThread` wired up the same day (real push appended live, poll kept as fallback), compiles clean. Android/iOS remain poll-only -- a real, explicitly open next step.

Mirrors Eats's identical toggle already shipped. **2026-07-18, cancel-order button parity closed on Android and iOS's Eats order lists (commit `ed5d755`), superseding the entry below:** `MyEatsOrdersView` on both mobile clients gained the same real "Cancel order" button bank-mfe got in the previous entry, calling the same `POST /api/v1/eats/orders/{id}/cancel` endpoint. **2026-07-18, real "Cancel order" button added to bank-mfe's Commerce and Eats order lists (commit `e7005d3`), wiring up the cancellation endpoints just added (superseding the entry below):** Any PLACED order in `MyCommerceOrdersView`/`MyEatsOrdersView` now shows a real Cancel button calling the already-live-verified `POST .../cancel` endpoints. Deliberately scoped to web only this pass -- Android/iOS's own Eats order lists didn't get the button (Commerce has no order list on mobile at all yet), a real, explicitly named remaining gap.

**2026-07-18, real order cancellation/refunds added to both Commerce and Eats (commit `7d9afd6`), closing a gap both modules explicitly deferred when they first shipped (superseding the entry below):** New `POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/cancel` and `POST /api/v1/eats/orders/{id}/cancel`, buyer or seller/restaurant, PLACED orders only, reusing `SupportService.reverseTransaction`'s exact reversing-ledger-entry technique. **Found and fixed a real logic bug this exposed**: `OrderService.updateOrderStatus` was missing the `currentIndex == -1` guard Eats's own status methods already had, which would have let a CANCELLED order be silently resurrected back to PLACED via the ordinary status-advance endpoint. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL for both modules: buyer wallet balance moved out and back by the exact order amount, every ledger leg flipped and balanced (including Eats's `eats_delivery_holding` leg), a stranger real-404s, re-cancelling real-409s, and the resurrection bug now correctly real-409s on both. See the Commerce and Eats rows for the full account.

Restaurant/menu browsing on both reuses the existing shopping endpoints, same reuse discipline as every other client. **2026-07-18, real Commerce UI added to bank-mfe (commit `2cde1f4`), closing the last "not yet built" gap the Commerce row had (superseding the entry below):** New Shop tab: browse merchants (reuses the existing shopping-catalog endpoint), a real product catalog with cart/checkout/order-tracking for buyers, plus a seller-side order-status view. **2026-07-18, real iOS bottom nav mirrored to Home/Shop/Hood/Talk/My, closing the last remaining native-mobile-UI gap for Messaging, Marketplace, and Commerce (superseding the entry below):** Same restructure as the Android pass below, now on the iOS client. `TabView` tags 1-4 in `ContentView.swift` replaced: **Shop** (new `ShopScreen.swift`) is real Coupang-style commerce, **Hood** (new `HoodScreen.swift`) is the real 당근마켓 marketplace, **Talk** (new `TalkScreen.swift`) is real 1:1 messaging with the same hand-off mechanism (a `pendingConversationId` `@Binding` threaded from Hood's "Message seller" into Talk, mirroring Android's identical pattern), and **My** (`EntireMenuScreen` in `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift`) folds Pay and Benefits in as real Quick-links rows opening the existing `PayScreen`/`BenefitsScreen` as sheets, replacing the old `DiscoverScreen` (Toss-Shopping-cashback browsing) entirely. `NetworkClient.swift` gained ~150 lines of new DTOs/endpoints mirroring Android's `ApiService.kt` field-for-field, plus an `authenticatedDelete` helper for listing removal. **One real bug found live on-Simulator, not just in code review**: the Simulator's stored Keychain session token had expired between sessions, which the Shop tab correctly surfaced as an honest "Couldn't reach itunda" error (traced by checking the backend directly with curl, confirming the same endpoint worked fine with a fresh token) rather than failing silently or showing stale/fake data -- re-logging in resolved it, and this incidentally caught that `EntireMenuScreen`'s "TUYIZERE ERIC" name display and `BankView`'s "Spend today RWF 18,200" figure are pre-existing hardcoded placeholders unrelated to this session's changes, not a new regression. Live-verified extensively on iPhone 17 Simulator (iOS 26.5) against the real local backend, using `cliclick` for programmatic taps once Accessibility/Screen-Recording permission was granted to the host terminal: logged in as a real user, confirmed the new 5-tab bar, browsed real Shop merchants (Kigali Bakery, Copy Test Coffee Shop -- both correctly showed "No products yet" -- then Order Test Shop's real 2-product catalog), built a real cart (1 Widget + 1 Gadget), checked out with a real delivery address, and placed a real order confirmed via direct MySQL query (`order_7245fef2...`, 6,500 RWF, status `PLACED`). Opened Hood's real "Real UI-verified sofa" listing, tapped "Message seller," confirmed the app switched to Talk and opened directly into the existing real chat thread with "Fresh Seller," sent a new real message, and confirmed it via direct MySQL query on the `messages` table. Opened Pay from My's Quick links and confirmed it shows real balance (32,050 RWF) and real nearby-merchant data through the existing, unmodified `PayScreen`. Both platforms now fully mirror the same Home/Shop/Hood/Talk/My navigation with equivalent live-verified depth.

**2026-07-18 — real Android bottom nav redesigned to Home/Shop/Hood/Talk/My, giving Messaging, Marketplace, and Commerce their first native mobile UI:** The user directly requested this real navigation restructure to match the super-app shape (Toss/당근마켓/Kakao-style). `TossTab` (`ItundaAppScreen.kt`) changed from Home/Benefits/Shop/Pay/All to Home/Shop/Hood/Talk/My: **Shop** now means real Coupang-style commerce (browse merchants -> a real per-merchant product catalog -> a real cart -> real checkout -> a real placed order), replacing the old Toss-Shopping-cashback content entirely; **Hood** is the real 당근마켓 marketplace (browse/my-listings, list-an-item, mark-sold/remove, message-seller); **Talk** is real 1:1 messaging (conversation list, new-chat-by-phone-number, a real chat thread with the same 4s poll `bank-mfe` already established); **My** is the renamed former "All" tab, with Benefits and Pay folded in as real rows (not dropped) since they lost their own top-level tab. New `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants/{merchantId}/products` (`ShoppingController`) closes a real gap this pass found: no public endpoint existed for a buyer to browse a specific merchant's catalog before ordering (`MerchantProductController`'s own endpoint is owner-only) -- reuses the already-existing `MerchantProductRepository.findByMerchantIdAndActiveTrue` query, just newly exposed. **Two real bugs found live on-device, not just in code review**: (1) none of the three new tabs' internal multi-step screens (chat thread, merchant detail, checkout) intercepted system back with a real `BackHandler` the way every other flow in this app already does (`TransferStep`/`SavingsFlowStep`/Settings/etc) -- pressing back from inside a chat thread exited the whole app instead of returning to the conversation list, caught only by actually pressing the hardware back button on the real device, fixed by adding the same `BackHandler` convention to all three; (2) `adb reverse` port-forward mappings silently drop on a USB re-enumeration mid-session (observed twice), a real device/tooling quirk, not an app bug -- recovered by re-applying `adb reverse` each time it happened. Live-verified extensively on a real physical Samsung Galaxy A16 (not an emulator): logged in, confirmed the new 5-tab bar, opened Pay/Benefits from My with working back buttons, browsed real Shop merchants, opened a real merchant's real product catalog, built a real 2-item cart, checked out with a real delivery address, placed a real order (confirmed via direct MySQL query: correct total/fee/status, and the buyer's real wallet balance dropped by the exact order amount), browsed Hood and found a real listing from an earlier verification pass, tapped "Message seller" and confirmed the app switched to Talk and opened directly into a real chat thread with the seller's real name, sent a real message (confirmed via direct MySQL query), and confirmed the fixed back-navigation correctly returns to the conversation list with the real updated message preview. iOS mirroring is now done -- see the entry above.

**2026-07-18, the latest entry — real Coupang-style commerce backend added, the third and last of the three new "super app" phases (superseding the entries below):** New `rw.itunda.commerce` module -- real multi-item orders reusing the existing `Merchant`/`MerchantProduct` catalog and the exact same wallet-to-wallet ledger movement `MerchantService.collect()` already proved out, just extended to a cart. Real server-side pricing (never trusted from the client), real receipt snapshotting, real forward-only self-declared fulfillment status (`PLACED → PACKED → SHIPPED → DELIVERED`), real IDOR protection, real pagination and idempotency from day one. No UI yet. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend: a real 2-item, 2-product order across exactly the right total/fee (confirmed via direct MySQL ledger-entry query), a zero-balance buyer real-422'd on insufficient funds, idempotent replay, cross-merchant product mixing blocked, forward-only status transitions enforced, and full IDOR protection on order detail/status updates. **2026-07-17, Partner SDK on-device instrumented test run for real on a physical device, closing the last deferred piece of that row (superseding the entries below):** `PartnerMiniAppLiveVerificationTest` (real download -> `ReactHost.reload()` -> `PartnerMiniAppActivity` resumed-and-alive under New Architecture) had been written and ready since earlier this session but never actually run -- no physical device was connected, and this session's Android emulator is independently unreliable while the private-cloud Multipass VMs are up. The user connected their Samsung Galaxy A16 (USB) this pass. Set up via `adb reverse tcp:4001/8081/8098 tcp:4001/8081/8098` (backend, Metro for the host app's own first-party JS, and a local HTTP server serving the freshly-built `partner-demo` bundle) and `-PapiBaseUrl=http://localhost:4001/` on the Gradle build (the emulator-only `10.0.2.2` alias doesn't resolve on a physical device). Registered a real partner + submitted + admin-approved a real mini-app pointing at the real bundle server, updated the test's hardcoded `PartnerMiniAppDto` fixture to match (bundleUrl now `localhost` not `10.0.2.2`, consistent with a physical device). **Two real bugs found and fixed by this pass's own on-device run**: (1) `adb reverse` mappings are dropped by a USB re-enumeration (observed after an `adb kill-server`/`start-server` cycle needed to recover from a dropped device) -- not persistent across reconnects, had to be re-applied before the test could reach the backend; (2) a genuine test-authoring bug in the pre-existing test itself -- `ActivityLifecycleMonitorRegistry.getActivitiesInStage()` asserts it's called on the main thread internally, but the test called it directly from the instrumentation test-runner thread, throwing `IllegalStateException` every time and never reaching its own assertions. Fixed by wrapping the query in `InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().runOnMainSync { ... }`. After both fixes: `./gradlew connectedDebugAndroidTest` real `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, `0 failed` -- the full chain (real login, real backend partner registration/approval, real bundle download over HTTP, real `ReactHost` reload, `PartnerMiniAppActivity` resumed and alive) verified end-to-end on real hardware, not an emulator. The Partner SDK row is now fully `real` with no deferred pieces.

**2026-07-17, real rate limiting added to the demo card-charge endpoint, closing a real security gap this pass's own review found (superseding the entries below):** `POST /api/v1/merchant/card/charge` had no rate limit at all, unlike every other similarly-shaped endpoint in this backend (`PartnerService.register`/`CertificateService.issue`, both already real-limited on this same codebase's own established `RateLimiter` convention). `DemoCardAuthorizationService.simulateOutcome` APPROVEs roughly 85% of any Luhn-valid card number for any card not one of the three fixed test numbers, and every approval credits real, spendable ledger balance into the merchant's real wallet from the `card_network_clearing` suspense account -- with no bound, a scripted burst of random Luhn-valid card numbers against this one endpoint could mint real balance indefinitely, a genuinely serious integrity gap distinct from (and more actionable than) the card-processing row's already-honest "no real PSP relationship" limitation. Fixed with `rateLimiter.checkLimit("merchant:chargeCard:$ownerUserId", limit = 10, window = Duration.ofMinutes(1))` -- generous enough for a real busy shop's checkout pace, tight enough to make brute-force card generation impractical. Required adding `:auth` as a real Gradle dependency of `:merchant` (previously absent). The rest of the merchant/payroll/KYB modules audited clean in this same pass: every merchant-owned resource (products, payroll roster entries, payroll runs) correctly 404s cross-tenant via an explicit ownership check before returning, matching the IDOR discipline the earlier Partner SDK/Certificate security pass already established; no raw SQL anywhere; `FacePayService`/`ShoppingCashbackService` correctly scope every operation to the JWT-derived caller. Live-verified against a real running backend: fired 12 rapid real card charges with the always-approved test card, confirmed exactly the first 10 succeeded (10,000 RWF posted across 10 real ledger transactions, confirmed via direct MySQL query) and attempts 11-12 both real-429'd with no partial state. `:merchant:test` passes.

Separately, a new `rw.itunda.core.web.pageMeta` convention closes a real, previously-flagged gap: `GET /system/partners/queue`, `GET /mini-apps/catalog`, `GET /shopping/merchants`, and `GET /system/compliance/queue` were unbounded `List<T>` dumps with no cap; all four now take a real `Pageable` (`@PageableDefault(size = 20)`) and return real `page`/`size`/`totalElements`/`totalPages` metadata alongside their existing response shape. **Extended the same pass to the two admin queues it missed**: `GET /system/fraud/queue` and `GET /system/support/queue` (the Non-Negotiable Gates section's Fraud detection and Customer support entries) were still unbounded `List<T>` dumps too — same fix, same convention, live-verified against a real running backend (`?size=1` on the support queue genuinely returned one row with `totalElements: 1`, `totalPages: 1`; both queues' real, correct pagination metadata confirmed against a real seeded fraud flag and a freshly created support ticket). `ops-mfe`'s queue consumers only ever read the `queue` array field, confirmed by direct inspection, so nothing broke. **2026-07-17, a new Toss Shopping row: real cashback on purchases at itunda's own merchants:** Closes the cashback half of "Toss Shopping." New `ShoppingCashbackService`/`ShoppingController`: a real 1% flat cashback (itunda's own convention, not sourced -- Toss doesn't publish per-merchant rates) awarded from `MerchantService.collect()`, reusing the existing `rewards_expense` clearing account `RewardsService` already established. `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` reuses itunda's own real registered Merchant directory as the catalog rather than fabricating external partners. **Two real bugs were caught and fixed by this pass's own live verification.** A real concurrency bug: cashback was originally `@Transactional(REQUIRES_NEW)`, which self-deadlocked against `collect()`'s own already-held row lock on the same payer wallet (a genuine MySQL `Lock wait timeout exceeded`) -- also wrong on the merits, since it would let cashback survive a payment that later rolled back. Fixed by joining the caller's transaction instead. Separately, the pass's own safety design (an explicit try/catch around the call site) was confirmed to actually work under that exact real failure: the payment still succeeded with `cashbackEarned: 0` rather than failing alongside the deadlock. Live-verified after the fix: a real 5,000 RWF QR payment earned exactly 50 RWF (1%) cashback, confirmed via direct MySQL query, visible in the payer's real transaction history, with the merchant's own revenue report staying exactly correct and unpolluted. Honestly not yet built: no UI lets a user browse the catalog -- `bank-mfe` turned out to be a fully mocked prototype with no real API calls, and mobile carries this session's known emulator risk without a connected device this pass. See the new Toss Shopping row for the full account.

**2026-07-17, a new Toss Certificate row: a real digital identity/signing certificate system:** Closes the "Toss 인증서" gap named in the expanded goal. New `rw.itunda.certificate` module, sourced from Toss's own real certificate product (toss.im/tosscert): free, KYC-gated issuance of a genuine Ed25519 keypair (standard JCA crypto) whose private key is generated, handed to the caller exactly once, and never persisted server-side at all -- stronger than even the Partner SDK's API-key pattern. Real cryptographic `/verify` reports signature validity and certificate status as two separate real PKI facts. **A real bug was caught and fixed by this pass's own live verification**: `/verify`/`/status` were initially JWT-gated, real-401ing any non-itunda-user caller, the opposite of correct for a public-key verification endpoint -- fixed with an explicit `permitAll`. Live-verified with unusual rigor: signed a real payload with an independent, external Node.js Ed25519 implementation (not the JVM that generated the key) and confirmed the server's own crypto genuinely validated it -- real cross-implementation interoperability. Confirmed tamper detection, and confirmed a revoked certificate's old signature still mathematically verifies while its status honestly reports REVOKED. Honestly not yet built: no UI touchpoint, and (matching NIDA's own honest boundary) no accredited certification-authority legal status with the Rwandan government. See the new Toss Certificate row for the full account.

Live-verified end-to-end in a real headless Chromium browser: registered a partner, submitted a mini-app, approved it via `ops-mfe`, confirmed it appears in the real catalog via direct MySQL query and a fresh API call. **Explicitly, honestly scoped like the iOS mini-app host itself was**: this is a real registry, review workflow, and catalog -- the mobile Saronite host does not yet download/verify/sandbox/render a third-party bundle at runtime, a separate, comparably large piece of native engineering left as the next distinct step. **2026-07-17, latest of this batch — ops-mfe's 6th queue (insurance claims) built (superseding the "later still" entry below):** The Ops Queues row named a real, scoped gap: `ops-mfe`'s five admin queues (Fraud, Compliance, Incidents, Reconciliation, Support) didn't cover the already-real `InsuranceClaimsAdminController`. Closed with a new `InsuranceClaimsQueue.tsx`, mirroring `ComplianceQueue.tsx`'s approve/reject-with-reason pattern exactly — no backend changes needed. Live-verified with a real headless Chromium browser (Playwright): submitted a real claim, logged into `ops-mfe` as ADMIN, confirmed it rendered, approved it through the UI, confirmed via direct MySQL query the real status flip, reviewer attribution, and double-entry payout. Zero console errors, zero failed requests, real production build (`tsc -b && vite build`) succeeds. See the Ops Queues row for the full account.

**2026-07-17, later still — task_profile closed for real, all 5 of 5 Rewards tasks now activity-verified (superseding the "later than that" entry below):** The last honor-system reward task is real: `users.profile_photo_url`/`email_verified` (`V20__profile_completion.sql`), a real single-use expiring `EmailVerificationToken` delivered via itunda's own existing real in-app Notification system (no SMTP relay exists in this backend, so a real email can't be sent -- the token is real and never echoed back in any API response, only delivered through that real channel). New `PUT /api/v1/auth/profile/photo`, `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/verify-email`, `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/verify-email/confirm`. Live-verified end-to-end: set a real photo (confirmed still ineligible -- a photo alone isn't enough), requested verification and confirmed the real token arrived via `GET /api/v1/notifications`, confirmed a bogus token 400s, confirmed the real token verifies and flips eligibility, confirmed reuse 400s, claimed the real 500 RWF reward (confirmed via direct MySQL query the claim row, the double-entry ledger legs, and the token's used_at), confirmed a second claim 409s. See the Rewards row for the full account.

**2026-07-17, later than that — a real referral subsystem closes task_referral (superseding the "actual final entry" below):** Rewards' `task_referral` was the last of the two named honor-system tasks that was actually buildable (the other, `task_profile`, genuinely needs a separate photo-upload/email-verification feature). Built for real: `users.referral_code`/`referred_by_user_id` (`V19__referral_columns.sql`), registration now accepts and validates an optional referral code (real `400 REFERRAL_CODE_NOT_FOUND` on an invalid one, not silent), and a new `GET /api/v1/rewards/referral` endpoint. See the Rewards row for the full account.

**2026-07-17, the actual final entry — all four iOS mini-apps live (superseding the "real final entry" below):** Extended the now-working iOS mini-app host to `wallet-balance`/`reward-tasks`/`insurance`, closing full parity with Android's four-mini-app host. Zero new native logic needed — three concrete `SaroniteMiniAppViewController` subclasses plus matching SwiftUI wrappers reusing the exact base class `pay-bills` proved out, wired into `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift`'s "Mini apps" section (which also gained the previously-missing 4th "Insurance" row). Live-verified against the real backend, each independently cross-checked, not just screenshotted: `wallet-balance` rendered "Jean's Main Account", 2,450,000 RWF; `reward-tasks` rendered a real "Complete your profile" task; `insurance` rendered the real "Health Shield" plan. `SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.swift` extended from 1 to 4 test cases. Three of the four hit a real, but NOT new, pre-existing SwiftUI/XCUITest bug already documented in `MoneyFlowLiveTests.swift` (keyboard interaction earlier in a test run shrinks `app.windows.firstMatch.frame`, which here also puts the floating tab bar over the lower "Mini apps" rows) — that file's own header already records three targeted fixes were tried and didn't resolve it, so this pass didn't re-attempt a fix and instead applied the same file's own established `XCTExpectFailure` convention, keeping the bug honestly visible rather than silently dropping coverage or shipping a permanently-red test. `pay-bills`' own test (no keyboard interaction earlier in its run) stays a real, unwrapped, passing assertion. Full suite confirmed stable: "Executed 4 tests, with 0 failures (0 unexpected)". See the Mini-app host row for the full account.

**2026-07-17, the real final entry — the iOS mini-app host is done:** Found and fixed the
definitive root cause behind every crash and rendering gap the previous two entries chased.
`react-native-safe-area-context` and `react-native-screens`' own podspecs gate their *real*
Fabric component source files behind `ENV['RCT_NEW_ARCH_ENABLED'] == '1'` checked at
`pod install` time — a Ruby/CocoaPods-time env var, never set, entirely separate from the
build-time flags already correct. Fixed with one line in `ios/Podfile`. That surfaced a second,
related gap: even with the real Fabric classes compiled, nothing told Fabric's runtime registry
about them (`RCTReactNativeFactoryDelegate.thirdPartyFabricComponents`, the official template's
own required override, was missing) — fixed in `SaroniteHost.swift` via runtime reflection,
since `import ReactCodegen` doesn't build from Swift or Objective-C. With both real fixes in
place, the earlier `react-native-safe-area-context` crash patch became unnecessary and was
reverted. **Live-verified end to end**: the pay-bills mini-app renders a real bill list matching
the real backend's actual response exactly, confirmed via a real device screenshot
cross-checked against a direct `curl`. See the Mini-app host row and
`ios/App/UITests/SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.swift`'s own header for the complete, six-bug account.

**2026-07-17, the genuinely latest entry now (superseded by the entry above):** Continued narrowing the iOS mini-app rendering
gap from the previous day's entry. Two more real bugs found and fixed, both confirmed via a
written (not NSLog-truncated) live view-hierarchy dump rather than inference from a screenshot:
`RCTDevLoadingView`'s stuck "Loading from Metro..." overlay turned out to be a real cosmetic gap
in RN's own bridgeless dev-tooling (it listens for a notification the real bridgeless runtime
never posts, posting a differently-named one instead) — disabled, and the bundle was confirmed
loading fine underneath the whole time. Separately, `RCTHost.start()` alone never got Fabric to
attach any content at all; switching to `RCTReactNativeFactory.startReactNativeWithModuleName:
inWindow:` (RN's real documented brownfield API) produced a real, confirmed Fabric component
tree for the first time. Root cause now pinpointed to one exact spot: `RNCSafeAreaProvider`
withholds all children until its first `onInsetsChange` event, which the prior day's own
crash-fix patch removed. A real fix was tried and did unblock rendering, but immediately
surfaced a further real crash (`-[RCTView setType:]`, a native view-config mismatch) — reverted
rather than shipped, since crashing beats rendering nothing only on paper. See the Mini-app host
row and `ios/App/UITests/SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.swift`'s own header for the full, exact account.

**2026-07-16, OK actually this is the most recent entry (superseding the "no really the most
recent" below):** Attempted wiring the first real iOS mini-app screen (`pay-bills`, matching
Android's own original proving ground) onto the CocoaPods/Tuist bridge closed earlier the same
day. Real progress, not a wash: three genuine bugs found and fixed live (missing
`react-native-safe-area-context`/`react-native-screens`/`react-native-svg` pods; a legacy
`RCTEventEmitter` bridge incompatibility in `react-native-safe-area-context@5.6.2`, patched via
`packages/saronite/patches/react-native-safe-area-context+5.6.2.patch`; an `RCTHost.start()`
ordering bug found via temporary `NSLog` diagnostics), each independently confirmed via a
different real on-device screenshot. One further real, specific bug found and left honestly
open: RN's own "Loading from Metro..." dev overlay never dismisses despite Metro confirmed
serving the complete bundle instantly every time, and zero requests ever reach the real backend.
Root cause not isolated. See the Mini-app host row and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own entry for
the full account, and `SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.swift`'s header for the live-test-level detail.

**2026-07-16, actually the most recent entry now (superseding the "genuinely most recent"

**2026-07-16, the genuinely most recent entry (superseding the "actual truly latest" below):**
Ledger concurrent-write contention load-tested for real, closing the Non-Negotiable Gates
section's own previously-named gap ("not yet load-tested... at scale"). No code changes were
needed — the existing pessimistic row-level locking was verified correct under real concurrency,
not just under sequential test coverage. Two live runs against an isolated local MySQL/Redis
instance, truly concurrent (OS-level backgrounded processes, not a loop) confirms against one
wallet: a 20-concurrent run surfaced a real HikariCP pool-exhaustion finding (a capacity-tuning
concern, not a locking bug — the one transaction that succeeded amid the chaos was still
perfectly balanced); an 8-concurrent run within pool capacity proved clean serialization with
zero lost updates and correct `INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` enforcement on the two that exceeded
available funds. See the Non-Negotiable Gates section's Double-entry ledger entry for the full
account.

reusing the same `RailCatalog`/`ProviderConnector` simulation every other rail-calling flow

**2026-07-13, even newer:** Face Pay moved `target` -> `real` — real biometric-enrolled
merchant payment (`POST /api/v1/facepay/enroll`/`/revoke`/`/status`/`/collect/{intentId}`),

**2026-07-13, even newer:** Spending/Budgeting moved `target` -> `real` — real overall and
per-category monthly budgets, computed live against the exact same categorization
`GET /wallet/spending` already uses, with real threshold notifications (`BUDGET_NEAR`/
`BUDGET_OVER`, once per threshold per month). Also real per-rail routing for P2P transfers
(RURA-sourced phone-prefix routing, closing the Transfer row's own named gap) and a real,
pre-existing bug found and fixed while live-verifying the budget notifications:
`NotificationController` read `Authentication.name` as the userId, which silently resolved to
a stringified `CurrentUser` object instead of the real id, so it always returned zero
notifications regardless of how many actually existed. See the Spending and Transfer rows for
the full live-verified accounts.

**2026-07-13, newest:** The Customer support workflow gate moved from a false "real" claim

the exact original ledger legs, and a real account-takeover flow that genuinely freezes a
wallet (closing a separate, real gap found along the way: `Wallet.isActive` existed but was
never enforced anywhere). See the Non-Negotiable Gates section's Customer support entry for
the full live-verified account, including a real bug (`WalletFrozenException` uncaught in
eight controllers) found and fixed during that verification. Also: fraud detection extended
from P2P-only to itunda's other two real money-moving paths (wallet transfer, merchant
collection), and merchant webhooks got real persistent retry matching Toss's actual documented
schedule — see the Operations/Fraud and Merchant rows respectively.

**2026-07-13, genuinely final:** Offline moved once more — `real (backend + Android) / target (iOS)`
-> `real (backend + Android + iOS)`. The iOS client (queue + `NWPathMonitor` + a periodic retry
timer, wired into the real savings-deposit flow) is now real and live-verified: all three core
behaviors (real offline queueing, real durable persistence across an app kill, real automatic
replay) independently confirmed via direct backend API checks. Along the way, found a real design
gap neither platform's first pass caught (`NWPathMonitor`/`ConnectivityManager` only see the
device's own network interface, not whether the app's specific backend is reachable) and fixed it
with a genuine periodic retry. See the Offline row for the full account, including an honest note
that a single fully-automated, fully-green XCUITest run wasn't achieved in this specific
resource-constrained environment (two Multipass VMs + an Android emulator running alongside
Xcode caused real OS-level jetsam kills, confirmed via system logs, not an app defect).

confirmed both in-app and via direct API call). A real, pre-existing crash bug blocking that

For anyone who read the pre-2026-07-13 version of this file: the entire "three unreconciled
backend directions" section describing a JSON-file-persisted Express backend, a mostly-empty
NestJS scaffold, and a spring-backend with zero Kotlin files is **obsolete** — none of that
exists anymore. The Express backend was fully ported to `services/backend` and then deleted; the
"three directions" resolved to `services/backend` (canonical) and `services/microservices` (a
real, smaller, deliberately-kept-separate MSA prototype). If any other document in this repo
still describes `backend/src/...` paths, treat it as stale — see `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own
doc-staleness notes for which ones were already caught and fixed as of this pass
(`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, `docs/TOSS_FEATURE_SPECIFICATION.md`). **Update, 2026-07-17: the other
three named here are no longer stale either** — `docs/PAYMENTS.md` and
`docs/IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE.md` were both already rewritten the same day this note was
originally written (each carries its own "Rewritten 2026-07-13" header and correctly describes
`services/backend`, not the deleted Express backend — re-checked directly, no remaining
`backend/src/` references outside PAYMENTS.md's own deliberate "here's what this doc used to
wrongly claim" callout). `docs/API_SPECIFICATION.md` has been kept current through this
session's own passes (Auth/Rewards sections, changelog entries) — see its own "What Changed"
section for the honest account of which earlier gaps in *that* doc's own changelog coverage
remain (not the same thing as describing the deleted backend).
